# earlier-900-1999

DEC-900 to DEC-1999 — the live Robinhood run (R0) and its rehearsal, the cell and its processes,
release engineering, and the first research loop. The decisions are verbatim below; the rules
still in force:

- The live run: paper and live never share a stream — a live workspace of its own (DEC-952); a
  live run needs `--confirm-live` and turns core dumps off (DEC-937); the paper run stops on any
  reconciliation mismatch (DEC-948); the asked opening holds Robinhood's own ask to the opening's
  quote age bound (DEC-945); `ApprovalTimedOut` and `ApprovalCanceled` are closed at version 1
  (DEC-944, DEC-950).
- The cell: the runtime runs as its own process on the journal alone, reads the kill switch
  first (DEC-1012); the deployment manager runs as its own process with least Kubernetes access
  and journals each start, stop and crash loop (DEC-1170, DEC-1172); the journal's Postgres runs
  with a synchronous standby, WAL and base backups (DEC-1050); the scheduler measures its clock
  over SNTP itself and paces ticks by its monotonic clock (DEC-1011).
- Releases: empty-base release images written by xtask, cosign signatures, a start-up check that
  trusts only signed images (DEC-971); static musl binaries under cargo-auditable with an SBOM
  from each binary, two native builds (DEC-972, DEC-1060).
- The vault: `SecretSource` with local and OpenBao backends, per-process policies, leases,
  manual unseal (DEC-977); the local vault's entries, file checks and import (DEC-976).
- Research: the loop calls through the model gateway and the call record carries the verdicts
  (DEC-1120); OpenRouter is the aggregator for every hosted model call, locked to one upstream
  with zero data retention (DEC-997); the vetted-source allowlist file and the news and filings
  stores (DEC-1130); backtests run in `mandate-backtest-runner`, which stores artifacts only
  (DEC-1144).
- Objects and verification: the object store's lock check, and the finding that Cloudflare R2's
  locks are weaker than journal spec needs (DEC-973).
- CI: per-PR jobs scoped to the diff's affected crates, everything nightly on main (DEC-1190,
  superseded in part by DEC-2600's category ranges and DEC-2641's caps); a quote becomes, stays
  and stops being the mark in the workspace data service (DEC-1200).


<a id="dec-900"></a>

## DEC-900: A kill switch's unknown scope member is dropped whole, even when it is malformed inside

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, DEC-176) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-09 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane L2 (`claude-code`), from L2's range DEC-900 to DEC-909, confirmed by the merge coordinator. The reading refuses fewer stops and applies nothing new: it weakens no safety invariant and no `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable, and it follows rule 13 |
| **Story or claim** | E10-10, claim [#750](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/750); refines [DEC-886](earlier-600-899.md#dec-886) items 1 and 6; raised by the review of [#1204](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1204) |

### Context

DEC-886 item 1 drops an unknown member inside the kill switch's hard `scope` and lists it at
`/scope/<name>`. Item 6 refuses a duplicate hard member `duplicate_member`. #1204's
`decode_lenient` reads the whole `scope` through the strict decoder's duplicate scan before it
drops anything, so a duplicate nested inside an unknown member refuses the whole stop. #1204's
review found:

```json
{"scope": {"kind": "agent", "id": "x", "zz": {"a": 1, "a": 2}}}
```

which #1204 refuses `duplicate_member` at `/scope/zz/a`, though `zz` applies nothing.

The two readings:

- **(a) Refuse.** Treat the scope as hard all the way down, so any duplicate inside it refuses the
  stop, as #1204 does.
- **(b) Drop.** Drop the unknown member whole, whatever it holds, keep `kind` and `id`, and let the
  stop go through.

### Decision

1. **Option (b).** An unknown member inside the kill switch's `scope` is dropped whole and listed
   once at `/scope/<name>`, whatever it holds: a duplicate inside it at any depth, a value of any
   type, a nested object naming `kind`, `id` or a workspace, or the member itself named twice
   (DEC-886 item 6 for a non-hard member). `kind` and `id` are kept as the scope without it would
   keep them. Nothing else changes.
2. **Guard: a duplicate hard member is still refused.** `kind` or `id` named twice inside the
   scope is refused `duplicate_member` (DEC-886 item 6), with or without an unknown member beside
   it. Which agent, connection or workspace was meant cannot be resolved, so the stop's target is
   never guessed, widened, or narrowed.
3. **Guard: a dropped member never moves the target.** For every body, the kept target (`kind`,
   `id`) equals the target of the same body with the unknown member removed. Nothing inside a
   dropped member is read, so it can never change which agent, connection or workspace the stop
   applies to.
4. **Order.** This decision, then a tests-correction PR that pins items 1 to 3 (pending, with
   `xtask/behaviour-only` rows), then the implementation fix, after #1204 merges.

### Rationale

The merge coordinator ruled (b), citing `AGENTS.md` rule 13: the kill switch is always available,
and risk reduction is never denied. Under (a), a client that adds a malformed member the platform
never reads loses its stop. DEC-886 item 1 already says an unknown scope member applies nothing,
and item 6 refuses duplicates only where the meant value cannot be told: `kind` and `id`. A
duplicate inside a dropped member leaves no such doubt, since nothing in it is read. The guards
keep what item 6 protects: the stop never acts on a guessed or widened target.

### Alternatives considered

- **(a) Refuse any duplicate inside the scope.** Refuses a stop over a member that applies
  nothing, against rule 13.
- **Drop the whole scope on a malformed unknown member.** Leaves no target, or defaults to the
  workspace, which widens the stop.
- **Read `kind` or `id` from inside the unknown member, or keep the last duplicate `id`.** Guesses
  or redirects the target.

<a id="dec-901"></a>

## DEC-901: The loss answer and its proposed ladder never draft for an allocation of 0 or less, a negative answer, or a drawdown outside (0, 1)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, DEC-176), confirmed by the merge coordinator. Not a founder matter |
| **Date** | 2026-10-09 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane L2 (`claude-code`) under DEC-79 and DEC-176, confirmed by the merge coordinator. It refuses inputs the schema already excludes and drafts nothing new, so it weakens no safety invariant and touches no `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable |
| **Story or claim** | E10-7 S1b, claim [#755](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/755); [mandate spec](../../specs/mandate.md) §7; [DEC-695](DEC-695.md) items 6 and 7; #1199's review (minor 2); PR #1216 |

### Context

DEC-695 items 6 and 7 define `loss_answer_fields` and `proposed_ladder`, but neither they nor §7
give an outcome for some inputs. These are an allocation of 0 or less, a negative answer, and a
drawdown D of 0 or less, or of 1 or more. #1199's review left these open. The merge coordinator made
pinning them a condition of the S1b implementation.

On some of these inputs `reference/mandate/ref.py` drafted, and on others it raised:

- **It drafted for:**
  - a fraction answer of 0.1 of an allocation of 0, or of -10000;
  - a USD answer of -100 of an allocation of -10000, which gives F = 0.01;
  - `proposed_ladder` with D = 1, 1.5, or 2.
- **It raised:**
  - `DivisionByZero` for a USD answer of 100 of an allocation of 0;
  - `InvalidOperation` for a USD answer of 0 of an allocation of 0.

### Decision

1. `loss_answer_fields` never drafts for an allocation of 0 or less, or for a negative answer, given
   as a fraction or in dollars.
2. `proposed_ladder` never drafts for a drawdown D of 0 or less, or of 1 or more.
3. For each of these inputs, either function returns a typed error or `AskAgain`, and never panics.
   Which of the two is left to the implementation.
4. `reference/mandate/ref.py` is aligned with items 1 to 3: both functions return `None`, the
   reference's "ask again", for every one of these inputs instead of drafting or raising.

### Rationale

This records consistency with the schema, not a new reading. The schema already excludes every one
of these inputs:

- `capital.allocation_usd` is `positive_decimal`.
- `capital.max_loss_from_allocation` and `risk.max_drawdown` are `open_fraction`
  (`^0\.[0-9]{0,27}[1-9]$`), which is strictly between 0 and 1.
- V-014 requires `max_loss_from_allocation ≥ max_drawdown`.

A draft for any of these inputs could therefore never be a valid mandate. Refusing it, or asking
again, only refuses what validation would refuse later.

### Alternatives considered

- **Keep `ref.py`'s drafts.** It would propose fields the schema rejects, from an allocation the
  schema rejects.
- **Let the functions raise or panic.** It would turn a caller's mistake into a crash on the
  onboarding path, against `AGENTS.md`'s rule that trading paths return typed errors.
- **Fix one outcome, typed error or `AskAgain`.** Nothing needs it yet. Leaving it to the
  implementation keeps this decision to what the schema already says.

### Consequences

- PR #1216's pending tests pin items 1 to 3 for both functions:
  - `a_loss_answer_without_a_positive_allocation_or_amount_is_never_drafted`;
  - `a_drawdown_outside_zero_to_one_proposes_no_ladder`, which covers D = 0, -0.08, -1, 1, 1.5,
    and 2.
- Item 4 ships in its own PR, on branch `claude/l2-e10-7-ref-dec-901`. ES-22's spec guard refuses
  a change under `reference/` in the same PR as code under `crates/`.
- `reference/mandate/fuzz.py` checks the same inputs against `ref.py`.
- `reference/mandate/mutants.py` seeds two bugs: dropping the allocation guard, and proposing a
  ladder beneath D ≥ 1. The fuzz catches both.
- No reference case or fixture changes: none drafted any of these inputs.

<a id="dec-902"></a>

## DEC-902: The Robinhood preflight reads exactly the assumed shapes, and refuses any other (C4)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted — item 6: the sui generis input `symbol` is assumed under DEC-849 item 5 until R0 step 4 records the real contract shape |
| **Date** | 2026-10-10 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The E7-6 C4 tests builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: each item fills a gap the contract and the connections spec leave by the reading that refuses, and none loosens a rule or touches live money |
| **Story or claim** | E7-6, the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md)'s C4; [DEC-470](earlier-303-599.md#dec-470) item 1; [DEC-875](earlier-600-899.md#dec-875) items 4 and 6; connections spec §6.2 |

### Decision

`RobinhoodConnector::account_snapshot` and `RobinhoodConnector::preflight_facts` map the answers
of the preflight's reads to what DEC-470 item 1 checks. The contract publishes no output shapes
([robinhood-contract.md](../tasks/robinhood-contract.md), U-R6), so DEC-875 item 4's assumption
holds until the founder's run reads real answers (R0 step 4), and the records are read as below.

1. **Closed shapes.** Each record has exactly the keys listed here. A key missing, a key not
   listed, a value of another type, a JSON number where decimal text is listed, or decimal text
   that `mandate-num` does not read (canonical text only, so `250.70` and `1e3` refuse) makes the
   whole read `Unreadable` with the read's code (`portfolio`, `positions`, `orders`,
   `tradability`, `quotes`). Nothing is defaulted and no number is filled in. A real shape that
   differs refuses, and that is a finding for the connector, never worked around (DEC-875 item 4).
2. **`get_portfolio`:** one record, `account_number`, `cash` and `buying_power`, the two amounts
   decimal text at or above zero. The snapshot carries those two amounts and no other account
   figure: no equity, margin or multiplier is assumed. The gate's 1× buying power, the lower of
   the two, is the runner's (DEC-529 item 11, G1a).
3. **`get_equity_positions`:** `positions[]`, each `account_number`, `symbol`, `quantity` (signed
   decimal text) and `average_buy_price` (a positive price). Every record of the agentic account is
   mapped in the broker's order, a zero quantity included, and none is merged or dropped, so
   DEC-470 item 1 sees and refuses it.
4. **`get_equity_orders`:** `orders[]`, each with the keys `mandate-rh-sim` serves: `id` (non-empty
   text), `account_number`, `symbol`, `side` (`buy`, `sell`), `type` (`market`, `limit`,
   `stop_market`, `stop_limit`), `quantity`, `limit_price` and `stop_price` (decimal text or
   `null`), `time_in_force` (`gfd`, `gtc`), `market_hours` (`regular_hours`, `extended_hours`,
   `all_day_hours`), `state` (the contract's ten), `filled_quantity`, and optionally `ref_id` as
   text. A record in a working state (`new`, `queued`, `confirmed`, `unconfirmed`,
   `partially_filled`) is an open order, with §6.2's status (DEC-860 item 3) and no
   `client_order_id` (U-R2). One in any of the other five is read in full, then left out.
5. **One page or none.** A list answer's `structuredContent` holds its records' member and nothing
   else. A next-page cursor, or any other member, refuses the read: reading only the first page
   could hide an open order. Following pages is C3's list request; the preflight does not page.
6. **`get_equity_tradability`:** read through `read` with the filter `symbol`, which the
   contract's input lists for that tool (`contract/tools.json`), so DEC-879 item 5 adds it to
   that tool's allowlist. One record: `account_number`, `symbol` byte-equal to the instrument's,
   and `tradable` a boolean. `false` is `RobinhoodError::NotTradable`; zero records (another
   account's record is dropped by `read`), two, or another symbol is `Unreadable`.
7. **`get_equity_quotes`:** called on the `Ordinary` budget with exactly `{"symbols": [symbol]}`.
   The contract names no account for it and its answer is about no account, so it is not an
   account read and does not go through `read` (CN-8 is kept: nothing about another account is in
   it). `quotes[]` must hold exactly one record, `symbol` byte-equal, `bid_price` and `ask_price`
   prices with the bid at or below the ask (DEC-470 item 2's uncrossed quote), or it is
   `Unreadable`. No time field is assumed. The quote's age is measured against the runner's clock
   read after the call, as DEC-470 item 2 reads its clock after the GETs. The cross-check with
   IEX is G1b's (DEC-529 item 12).
8. **Every account read is scoped.** `get_portfolio`, `get_equity_positions` and
   `get_equity_orders` go through `read` with no filter but the recorded account, and
   `get_equity_tradability` with `symbol` too, so each runs the account check afresh (DEC-875
   items 1 and 2). A refusal of any read is the preflight's refusal, unchanged. No order, cancel
   or exit waits on a read (`AGENTS.md` rule 13, DEC-875 item 5).
9. **Not in C4's tests PR.** `mandate-rh-sim` serving `get_portfolio`, `get_equity_positions`,
   `get_equity_tradability` and `get_equity_quotes`, and the end-to-end preflight against it in
   that crate's `tests/`, come with C4's implementation (DEC-875 item 6). The in-crate tests use a
   recording double.

### Rationale

Every item refuses where the contract is silent. A refused preflight blocks an opening, which
adds no risk. Dropping a zero row, ignoring a cursor, defaulting a missing price, or reading an
unknown key as harmless could each let a non-flat account look flat. None of these readings can
hold a risk reduction, because exits never pass through the preflight. That makes each one a
DEC-176 reading an agent may accept.

### Alternatives considered

- Ignoring keys the shape does not list: rejected. An unknown key could carry the fact that
  matters (a margin flag, a held quantity), and the brief asks that any unknown field refuse.
- Mapping into `mandate-executor`'s `BrokerAccount`: rejected. Its status, multiplier, margin and
  fee fields have no source in the contract, so they would have to be invented. The snapshot
  reuses `BrokerPosition` and `BrokerOrder`, which the contract's records fill.
- Treating `rejected`, `failed` or `voided` records as open: rejected. §6.2 reads them as not
  working. Their re-read by `order_id` concerns our own intents (C3), and a flat account's history
  would otherwise refuse every run.
- Following every page here: left to C3, which owns paging. Until then, a second page refuses.

<a id="dec-911"></a>

## DEC-911: What Alpaca's crypto `limit` cells declare (DEC-854's precondition)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering under DEC-79; each reading is the one that refuses more, DEC-176) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-10 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, the builder of E7-26's precondition tests PR) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26, first-live-trade row G1a's precondition ([DEC-854](earlier-600-899.md#dec-854) consequence, [brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md) row G1a); [DEC-531](DEC-531.md) items 5 and 6; [DEC-630](DEC-630.md) item 9; [DEC-838](earlier-600-899.md#dec-838) item 3; LT-14 |

DEC-854's consequence says an Alpaca crypto mandate is refused until `mandate-alpaca`'s profile
declares trading spec §5.2's crypto `limit` cells, before the G1a runner's deployment calls
`mandate_builder::deployable` or the cycle calls `propose_on`. §5.2's crypto row names its order
types (`market, limit, stop-limit; simple orders only`) and its times in force (`gtc, ioc`) for the
row as a whole, but not what each `limit` cell declares; the equities rows split their cells by
quantity form and crypto's does not. These are the readings the cells' pins take.

### Decision

1. **Times in force: exactly `gtc` and `ioc`, in both cells.** §5.2's crypto row names `gtc, ioc`
   and nothing else, so neither cell lists `day` or any other time in force. The row's
   parenthetical "(Mandate uses stop-limit as GTC only)" narrows the `stop_limit` cells alone
   (DEC-838 item 3), and §5.4's crypto adds are limit IOC orders on the same row, so the `limit`
   cells take the full set: an opening that carries either of §5.2's crypto times in force finds
   a form (DEC-854 items 2 and 6).
2. **Quantity forms: whole and fractional, neither more nor fewer.** The row's own precedent is
   DEC-838 item 3's reading, whose `stop_limit` cells the crypto row already declares in both
   forms. No notional cell is added: §5.2's equities rows name notional and crypto's row does
   not, the tighter set is taken (DEC-176), and the builder never opens notional anyway
   (DEC-854 item 1). A cell a row does not declare offers nothing (DEC-630 item 9).
3. **No protection form in a `limit` cell.** §5.2's crypto row is "simple orders only", and
   protection on crypto is the one resting stop-limit (DEC-36): a crypto limit order is never a
   bracket's entry or an OCO's parent, in either quantity form.
4. **Scope: the `limit` cells alone, beside B2a's.** The precondition that DEC-854 names is the
   `limit` cells, because §5.1 opens crypto with a limit order; `market` and `stop` cells wait
   for a story that reads them (DEC-531 item 6), and the row keeps exactly `stop_limit` (B2a) and
   `limit`. §5.2's "maximum 200,000 USD notional per order" line is a broker-side cap the mandate
   caps first (`max_order_usd`) and the broker enforces at submission; it is not a capability
   cell, and no reading of this slice adds it to the profile.
5. **Where the cells are pinned: at the declaration seam, in `mandate-alpaca`'s tests.** B3's
   reader (`opening_form`, `propose_on`, `deployable`) is implemented and pinned over generated
   and hand-built profiles in `crates/mandate-builder/tests/opening.rs`, and a connector's tests
   do not reach `mandate-builder` (layer 7 takes no builder dependency; a tests-only change adds
   none). What this slice pins is the missing half alone: Alpaca's declaration — each cell's
   existence, its exact times in force, its quantity forms and its protection forms — which is
   the entire contract the builder intersects. The wiring of `deployable` and `propose_on` to
   Alpaca's profile is G1a's implementation's, and #1297's precondition note holds it to these
   cells.

### Rationale

Each item declares less than a broker might accept, so the intersection is never wider than
§5.2's crypto row; none loosens a safety rule or changes an Alpaca outcome (LT-14 — the existing
B2a `stop_limit` pins and B1's equities pins are untouched, and adding cells can only end a
refusal that today's absence causes, never begin one). The readings follow the same route DEC-838
took for the stop-limit cells: §5.2's row is the recorded contract, and where it is silent the
tighter set is taken.

### Alternatives considered

- **`gtc` only, as DEC-838 narrowed the `stop_limit` cells.** Rejected: §5.2's "as GTC only"
  parenthetical names the stop-limit alone, and §5.4's crypto adds are limit IOC orders the same
  row must carry.
- **A notional cell, since Alpaca's crypto API accepts dollar amounts.** Rejected: §5.2's crypto
  row names no notional form, and refusing more is DEC-176's route; the builder never opens
  notional (DEC-854 item 1).
- **`market` cells beside the `limit` cells, since §5.2's row lists `market`.** Rejected: DEC-531
  item 6 declares only what a story reads, and the precondition names the `limit` cells.
- **Pinning the intersection through `mandate-builder` in the connector's tests.** Rejected: the
  connector may not take a builder dependency for a tests-only change, and B3's semantics are
  already pinned in `mandate-builder`'s own tests; item 5 records the seam.

<a id="dec-913"></a>

## DEC-913: A prefix whose rows span more than one stream gives no connection anchor, and its range fails closed

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, DEC-176) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-11 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane L2's builder, a DEC-77 tests-first change under DEC-79 and DEC-176, recording the founder-approved "mixed-stream prefix tightening" the dormant L2 lane's coordinator proposed and the founder approved; the founder reviews after the fact under DEC-79. The refusal is DEC-892 item 6's hold-anchor rule brought to the connection anchor: it refuses more and admits nothing new, weakens no safety invariant, and touches no `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable |
| **Story or claim** | E7-17 follow-up, claim [#764](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/764); [journal spec](../../specs/journal.md) §11 `connection_lifecycle_mismatch`; tightens [DEC-889](DEC-889.md) item 1 by [DEC-892](DEC-892.md) item 6's reading of a mixed-stream prefix, on DEC-892 items 3 and [DEC-885](DEC-885.md) item 4's fail-closed run; #1242's "Not done", #1280's "Not done" |

### Context

`ConnectionAnchor::from_verified` (DEC-889 item 1) folds the rows a `VerifiedPrefix` bound and
anchors the last row's stream. `VerifiedPrefix::bind` verifies §11's checks 1 to 6, which walk rows
by position in the order given, so a prefix that chains two streams' rows into one hash chain —
`seq` 1 to n contiguous and each `prev_hash` the row before it — binds exactly when the trusted
start commits to its tail. `from_verified` then folds each stream on its own, and nothing breaks a
rule in a clean pair of streams, so it gives an anchor for the last row's stream: the range judges
that one stream, and every other stream fails closed at its first judged record (DEC-889 item 2).

That reading passes nothing the full chain passes on: #1242's "Not done" already noted no false
pass exists, because a rewritten row fails `bind`. But the hold anchor folded from the same prefix
reads a wider rule: DEC-892 item 6 gives `HeldAnchor::Unknown` for "a prefix that is not one agent
stream's", covering "rows of more than one stream", because "such a prefix is not a chain the fold
can read". Two anchors of one prefix should not disagree on whether that prefix is a chain at all.

### Decision

1. **A mixed-stream prefix anchors nothing.** `ConnectionAnchor::from_verified` gives
   `ConnectionAnchorError::Broken` when the prefix's rows are not all on one stream. This is
   DEC-892 item 6's reading for the connection anchor: rows of more than one stream are not one
   stream's chain, which is what DEC-889 item 1's anchor is. An empty prefix keeps its meaning
   (DEC-889 item 2): no rows, no stream, the anchor of the range's first judged record's stream.
2. **The caller runs the range unanchored**, as it does for any refused anchor (DEC-892 item 3):
   the range fails closed with `ConnectionCheck::Unanchored` at its first judged record, of any
   stream — the stream the looser reading anchored included, whose records then pass.
3. **`VerifiedPrefix::bind` is unchanged.** It is the shared check, built by lane L5 and accepted
   by DEC-892 item 1 for every anchor fold; the approved scope of this change is the connection
   anchor only, so the refusal is `from_verified`'s, the connection anchor's own constructor.
4. **Nothing else moves.** A prefix of one stream still binds and anchors its range's stream
   exactly as DEC-889 accepted, and `bind` verifying a mixed chain is not itself refused here.

### Rationale

A prefix whose rows span two streams reaches `from_verified` only through a writer that numbered
one range's rows of several streams as one chain, or a caller that handed two streams' rows to the
anchor as one prefix: §2 gives a stream its own numbering, and §11's checks verify the chain they
are given, not the streams it names. Today's reading refuses everything the tightened reading
refuses at every stream but the last row's; the only difference is that one stream, judged from a
fold whose rows another stream owns a part of. Refusing it closes that gap while binding no
caller that the looser reading bound: DEC-890's callers read one stream at a time. And it gives
the two anchors of one prefix one answer to "is this prefix a chain", so the next reader cannot
copy the looser one where the tighter belongs. Refusing more and admitting nothing, it is a
DEC-176 tightening.

### Alternatives considered

- **Keep today's looser reading**, leaving the follow-up proposed by #1242 open. Rejected: the
  founder approved the tightening, and DEC-892 item 6 already read a mixed prefix as no chain for
  the hold anchor of the same prefix.
- **Refuse in `VerifiedPrefix::bind`.** Rejected: `bind` is the shared check of every anchor fold,
  and changing it moves L5's hold anchor and DEC-892 item 1's accepted semantics; the approved
  scope is the connection anchor only.
- **Anchor each stream from its own rows.** Rejected already by DEC-889: no caller needs it, and
  DEC-890's callers read one stream at a time.

<a id="dec-916"></a>

## DEC-916: The verification walk judges only its own stream's rows, over the whole snapshot it is handed

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, DEC-79 and DEC-176: each item refuses more, or resolves a gap by the reading that adds no risk; the founder reviews after the fact) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-11 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane L5's builder (`claude-code`), E12-3's walk-stage tests |
| **Story or claim** | E12-3, claim [#748](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/748); [workspace API spec](../../specs/workspace-api.md) §4.8.1 "Verification"; [DEC-787](DEC-787.md) item 1 |

### Context

The run's walk stage (§4.8.1 "Coverage", T1b) takes the range's rows as data, the way the plan stage
takes the control snapshot. §4.8.1 says what the run reads — "The run reads the events of
`from_seq` to `to_seq` in that snapshot and walks them in `seq` order" — and DEC-787 item 1 folds
the folds' prefix and the segments' rows past `to_seq` into the same snapshot, but neither says
what the walk does when the rows it is handed are not rows of the stream it was asked to verify.
Under a genesis start, a cleanly chained foreign row passes every per-event check: check 2 compares
a row's columns with its own body, and check 3 and check 5 compare it with its own neighbours, so
nothing in §11 distinguishes another workspace's chain from the range's own.

### Decision

1. **The walk judges only rows of the stream it was asked to verify.** Every row of the input must
   name, in its `stream_id`, the request's own stream — another workspace's, another of the same
   workspace's, or a row whose `stream_id` does not parse, alone or among the range's own rows —
   or the run is refused the one `NotFound` of AU-1 and DEC-760 item 4 (the refusal the request's
   own `stream_id` gets at step 4), before any position is judged and with nothing recorded. This
   is AU-1's own sentence — "a verification reads only streams whose `stream_id` names the path's
   workspace" and "no byte of the other workspace appears in any response" — read onto the rows
   the walk is handed: walked, such a row would put another workspace's event hash into
   `to_hash`, a byte of the other workspace in a served response.
2. **The walk's rows parameter holds the stream's whole slice of the one snapshot**, from `seq` 1
   to the head it holds (DEC-787 item 1: "The range's events, the workspace's control-stream
   records the run reads, the trusted-start record, and the hold anchor and connection anchor
   below are all read in one database snapshot"). The walk judges the positions `from_seq` to
   `to_seq` of them and only those; the folds of the per-range steps read the rows before
   `from_seq`, and the segment checks read the rows after `to_seq`, which §4.8.1 says are
   "compared but not counted in `checked`" — so a range ending before the head walks its own
   positions only and the tail rows neither fail it nor count.

Each item is pinned by E12-3's walk-stage tests, in
`crates/mandate-audit/tests/verification_walk.rs`: item 1 by
`rows_of_another_stream_refuse_the_run_alike`, item 2 by
`a_clean_range_walks_every_event_but_never_its_tail`. No spec text changes: AU-1 already names
verification, and §4.8.1 already states the one snapshot and the counted range.

### Rationale

An input the walk cannot tie to the stream it was asked to verify is a bug in whatever assembled
the snapshot, and refusing it is the cheapest outcome for the caller: nothing is recorded, so no
false SEV-1 follows, and the refusal is retryable once the snapshot is right. A refusal also keeps
AU-1's "no byte of the other workspace appears in any response" true of the run's own answer, not
only of the reads the adapter made. Taking the whole snapshot slice as one parameter keeps
`walk`'s signature the one the per-range steps (T2, T3) and the run vectors (V2) finish against,
without a later change to a merged API.

### Alternatives considered

- **Walk the rows as they come (no stream check).** Rejected: the record would vouch for events
  the verifier was never asked about, and a genesis-started range would carry another workspace's
  `to_hash` into a served response.
- **Skip the foreign rows and let `seq_gap` fail the range.** Rejected: on a healthy stream it
  records a found fault that is no fault, a SEV-1 for nothing, and it names the wrong code for
  what went wrong.
- **Pass only the range's rows, sliced by the caller.** Rejected: the same snapshot must also
  serve the folds' prefix (DEC-892) and the segment checks' rows past `to_seq` (DEC-787 item 4),
  so the caller would slice three times from one snapshot and the run could not check that the
  slices agree.

<a id="dec-917"></a>

## DEC-917: The ES-22 split guard does not count a crate's tests-directory files as code

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176: it resolves a gap between the guard and ES-22's purpose by the reading that adds no risk, and weakens no safety rule or `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-21 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | an agent (`deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017`, Cursor), acting as the coordinating session under DEC-79, at the founder's direction of 2026-10-20 22:39 PDT to act autonomously. Proposed once before under this number by PR #1321, which closed without merging; that PR's text is the source of items 1 to 3 |
| **Story or claim** | ES-22 (ADR-0001); the spec guard's refusal of [PR #1320](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1320), [PR #2022](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/2022) and [PR #2055](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/2055); [DEC-2632](process.md#dec-2632) |

### Context

ES-22 makes a protected path (a spec, a schema, a reference case) ship separately from code, and
`xtask`'s `classify` enforces it with two prefix lists: `PROTECTED_PATHS` and
`CODE_PATHS = ["crates/", "xtask/src/"]`. A path under `crates/` therefore counts as code
whatever it is, so the guard cannot tell `crates/mandate-journal/tests/credentials.rs` from
`crates/mandate-journal/src/lib.rs`.

Three changes have now been refused by it, and in each the every-possible split is mutually red:

- **#1320** (E7-17 reference vectors, [DEC-913](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-913)): the vectors, their generated
  fixtures, the Python reference and the Rust test that asserts them travel together or the change
  lands inconsistent.
- **#2022** (J1, lane D1): it changes `docs/specs/journal.md`, `docs/specs/reference-cases/journal.yaml`,
  `fixtures/refcases/journal.json`, `reference/journal/` and `crates/mandate-journal/src/deployment.rs`.
  The spec half cannot compile the test that reproduces it; the code half needs the spec's new
  records to exist.
- **#2055** ([DEC-2632](process.md#dec-2632)): the schema half needs `SCHEMA_AHEAD_OF_TYPE`, which lives in
  `crates/mandate-api/tests/body_workspace.rs`, and the tests half needs the stems its schemas
  introduce. Both halves are red alone; it merged with `spec-guard` still failing, and that gate
  has been red on `main` since.

### Decision

1. **A crate's tests directory is tests, not code.** The ES-22 split guard classifies a changed
   path as code only when it is not a file under `crates/<crate>/tests/**`. A tests path for this
   reading is that shape, the crate-level integration-test directories.
2. **A `src` file stays code wholesale.** A change under `crates/<crate>/src/**` still splits from
   any protected path it travels with, whatever `#[cfg(test)]` modules the file holds inline. The
   classifier reads paths only, never contents.
3. **`xtask/src/` stays code unconditionally**, and so does a `crates/<crate>/tests/` path that is
   itself a protected path (none is today).
4. Every other ES-22 requirement stands: a protected change still cites a `DEC-<n>`, still cannot
   ship with `src` code, and still takes a green CI and an independent review before merge.

### Rationale

ES-22's split exists so a reviewer cannot smuggle a live-code edit in under a "docs only" banner. A
tests-only Rust change does not serve that smuggling: it alters no behaviour any spec describes. It
is the change ES-22 most wants next to a spec edit, because it is the spec edit's own
reproduction — and the repository already treats such a file as non-behavioural everywhere else
(the mutation gate, the pending gate, the layer check all read it as a test).

The guard's `classify` keeps its shape — a protected prefix list, a code prefix list — and gains
one exclusion. That is the smallest reading that closes the gap, and it is reversible by deleting
one function.

The alternative, and the more common fix, is to edit the *merge gate* or to grant per-PR
exceptions the way [DEC-462](earlier-303-599.md#dec-462) did for E7-7. Both were refused: a per-PR marker
accumulates and nobody removes it, and the merge-gate path does not make the change landable — the
guard still fails the PR.

### Alternatives considered

- **Leave the guard as it is and keep splitting.** Refused: the halves are red, so the change
  cannot land at all. #2055 merged with this gate red and main has carried that red since.
- **Classify `src` files that hold only `#[cfg(test)]` as tests.** Refused: it needs file contents,
  not paths, and a `src` file's test module sits beside real code.
- **Exempt a PR that cites a DEC and pairs a spec with its test.** Refused: a marker is a hole that
  nobody revisits, which is why DEC-462 confined its own to one PR number.
- **Broaden it to every file under `crates/` that is not `src/`.** Refused as too wide: benches,
  examples and build scripts are code a spec does not describe, but they also are not the
  reproduction of one, and nobody has shown they need to travel.

### Consequences

- A spec change and the integration test that reproduces it can land in one PR, which unblocks
  #2022 and every later change of that shape.
- A `src` change beside a spec still splits, so ES-22's stated purpose is intact.
- `cargo xtask ci spec-guard`'s behaviour changes for exactly one path shape; `xtask`'s own tests
  cover both sides, and the existing `status_toml_is_protected_not_code` keeps passing.
- No journal event: this changes no runtime state.

<a id="dec-921"></a>

## DEC-921: Live-arm rows land as one PR carrying both tests and implementation, with every merge gate kept

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder (kunwarshivam), DEC-79, answering the coordinator's decision batch of 2026-10-16 |
| **Story or claim** | R0 live-arm rows of the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md); DEC-77 |

### Decision

1. For the rows on the path to the first live order (R0), a builder may land the tests and the
   implementation in one PR, instead of the DEC-77 order (a tests PR with pending pins, then an
   implementation PR that only deletes them).
2. Every other gate is kept: the tests are written first in the PR's own history and shown to
   fail on the stub or on a seeded bug before the implementation commit; the PR gets an
   independent review by an agent on a different model; every mutant on the diff of a
   safety-critical crate is killed; CI `fast` and `full` are green on the merged head.
3. Rows off the R0 path keep DEC-77 unchanged. This decision ends when R0's evidence PR merges.

### Rationale

The founder has about a week. The tests-then-implementation order doubles the review and CI
cycles per row. The safety comes from the tests existing, failing first, and being reviewed;
keeping all of that and dropping the second PR saves one cycle per row.

### Alternatives considered

- Keep DEC-77: about twice the cycles; code-complete moves well past 10/29.
- Drop review or mutants for speed: refused; that weakens a safety rule.

<a id="dec-923"></a>

## DEC-923: Live cli_confirm and live approvals approve are admitted only for a Robinhood live deployment under DEC-529's limits, until E9-4

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder (kunwarshivam), DEC-79, answering the coordinator's decision batch of 2026-10-16 |
| **Story or claim** | DEC-529 item 3; DEC-155 item 4; E9-4 |

### Decision

1. The runtime admits a `cli_confirm` step-up, and `mandate approvals approve` accepts a grant,
   for a live deployment only when all hold: the connection is Robinhood, the confirmed mandate
   is within DEC-529's limits (`max_order_usd` 100, the one instrument DEC-529 names), and the
   approver is the workspace owner.
2. Each admission and grant is journaled with the approver and the step-up kind before the
   order intent it releases.
3. Every other live deployment keeps refusing `cli_confirm`. This admission ends when E9-4's
   step-up authentication lands; a later decision then retires it.
4. Risk reduction is never gated by this (rule 13).

### Rationale

Without it no live opening can be approved before E9-4, which adds about a week. The scope
limits exposure to DEC-529's one small order.

### Alternatives considered

- Wait for E9-4: no live order this week.

<a id="dec-924"></a>

## DEC-924: The shell's unconditional live refusal is replaced by a gated admission

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder (kunwarshivam), DEC-79, answering the coordinator's decision batch of 2026-10-16 |
| **Story or claim** | DEC-910 item 2; DEC-918; LT-1; LT-9; R0 |

### Decision

1. `DeploymentRefusal::Live` in `mandate-shell`'s control is no longer unconditional. A live
   deployment is admitted only when all hold: the binary is built with `live`; the run passed
   `--confirm-live`; the live door's connection and unspent checks passed on the run's real
   records and held state (DEC-918, never the placeholder); and the MCP host is the pinned
   Robinhood host constant.
2. Any one missing still refuses `Live` before a credential is read.
3. The constant is Robinhood's published MCP host, set in one place behind `live`, and reviewed
   in the PR that sets it.

### Rationale

This is the last lock between the built live arm and a real order. Each condition is one the
founder controls or the door already verifies.

### Alternatives considered

- Keep the lock until the sim rehearsal passes: about one more day.

<a id="dec-925"></a>

## DEC-925: The CLI gestures accept a live mandate on the owner's recorded, unspent Robinhood live connection only

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, the K1b builder, Lane A1), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: each reading below applies DEC-529 item 3 (founder, Accepted) and, where that item leaves a gap, takes the reading that refuses more and adds no risk |
| **Story or claim** | K1b (E7-11), [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md); DEC-529 item 3; mandate spec V-001; claim [#1340](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1340) |

### Context

DEC-529 item 3 narrows V-001 and DEC-530 item 2 for one founder-run live order: "a `live` mandate
is accepted only on a Robinhood connection the founder recorded through the CLI", and V-001 adds
"and has not spent", checked "when the version is confirmed and deployed". K1b's first half
(`connection record`) records that connection. Until now `version create`, `version confirm` and
`agent deploy` ran only in `paper` (DEC-530 items 1 and 2): the owner's environment was hard-coded
and every other environment refused `paper_only`. Nothing said how the owner says "live", what
"recorded through the CLI" reads as on the control stream, or which code each other combination
gets.

### Decision

1. **The owner says it.** The three gestures take `--environment`, `paper` by default and `live`
   when given; any other value is refused `environment_invalid` before any journal is opened. The
   owner's environment is the envelope `environment` of every event the gesture commits. `config
   register`, `model register`, `workspace open` and the approvals commands are unchanged and stay
   paper only (their own lanes).
2. **One check, `version::admitted`, for every gesture.** `create`, `confirm`, `deploy`, and the
   read-only `confirmation` and `deployment` call it once the document is read (for `create`,
   once it parses): an owner neither in `paper` nor in `live` is still refused `paper_only`
   before anything is read (DEC-530 item 2). Then:
   - a `paper` owner takes only a `paper` document; any other is `mandate_not_paper`. This is new
     for `confirm` and `deploy`, which could not meet a live document before: a live version
     created by a live owner cannot be confirmed or deployed in paper;
   - a `live` owner takes only a `live` document whose `connection_id` is a **recorded Robinhood
     live connection**; any other document or connection keeps `paper_only`, the existing code.
3. **"Recorded through the CLI" reads as the record `connection record` writes.** The latest
   `ConnectionEstablished` or `ConnectionRevoked` naming the mandate's `connection_id` on the
   control stream must be an establishment whose payload names broker `robinhood`, environment
   `live`, a `margin_attestation` (`cash_account` or `margin_disabled`, DEC-529 item 11),
   `step_up.method` `cli_confirm`, and `user` the owner's own id (DEC-529 item 3, "on the
   founder's own connection only"). A revoked, paper, Alpaca, Kraken, unattested, passkey or
   another user's record refuses. Each condition only refuses more.
4. **Unspent at every gesture.** V-001 checks "unspent" at confirmation and deployment; the CLI
   checks it at creation too, the tighter reading. Spent is V-001's two cases, read on the account
   stream `acct:{workspace}:{account_ref}` the record binds: an order that filled, wholly or
   partly, which is DEC-910 item 3's evidence, a `FillApplied` or `LateFillApplied`, the same the
   runner's lapse reads; and an order whose answer was lost, an order (by `client_order_id`) whose
   latest `OrderStateChanged` is `unknown`, until reconciliation moves it to another state (DEC-529
   items 1 and 4). A spent record refuses `connection_spent`, the runner's code. The runner's lapse
   reads fills alone (DEC-910 item 3); its flat check and the instrument block hold an `Unknown`
   order there, so the CLI's wider reading only refuses more. A fill or a lost answer that lands
   between the check and the commit is caught by the runner's own check at start.
5. **A re-run is refused like a fresh gesture.** The check runs before a gesture finds its earlier
   event, so a re-run after the record is revoked or spent is refused rather than answered with
   the earlier event. It commits nothing either way; an agent already deployed stays deployed.

### Rationale

The gate sits where the owner acts, before anything is stored or appended, and reads only the
journal the gestures already read. Every combination but DEC-529 item 3's one is refused with the
code it had, so the paper path and every existing test are unchanged.

### Alternatives considered

- **Derive the environment from the document.** Rejected: the owner would never state "live";
  an explicit flag that must agree with the document refuses a mistaken live file.
- **Admit any live Robinhood record.** Rejected: DEC-529 item 3 names the founder's own
  connection recorded through the CLI; the user and `cli_confirm` conditions read that literally.
- **A new code for each live refusal.** Rejected: the dispatch and DEC-530 keep `paper_only`; only
  the spent record, whose remedy differs, gets the runner's own code.

<a id="dec-927"></a>

## DEC-927: How the runtime and the CLI read DEC-923's scoped live `cli_confirm`

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (items 1 to 3 and 5 to 7 under DEC-176; item 4's reading of "the workspace owner" is a founder-reserved reading of DEC-923, Accepted (agent, under DEC-929)) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Claude Code (builder, Lane B2, dispatched by the merge coordinator), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: each reading below only narrows DEC-923, adds no risk, and weakens no safety invariant |
| **Story or claim** | [DEC-923](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-923) items 1 to 4; DEC-529 item 3; DEC-155 item 4; claim [#1342](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1342) |

### Decision

1. **One judge.** `mandate_approval::live_cli_confirm` is the only place DEC-923's conditions are
   written. The runtime's admission check 6 and `mandate approvals approve` both call it, so the
   two cannot drift. It checks, in this order, and the first failure names the refusal: a scope is
   known (`live_scope_unknown`); the connection's broker is Robinhood (`live_not_robinhood`); the
   confirmed mandate's `max_order_usd` is at most 100 USD (`live_mandate_cap_above_limit`); the
   mandate pins exactly one instrument (`live_not_one_instrument`); the bound order is on it
   (`live_other_instrument`); the bound order is a US equity (`live_not_equity`, DEC-529 item 13);
   the bound order's value, quantity times limit, is at most 100 USD
   (`live_order_above_limit`); and the approver is the workspace owner (`live_not_owner`).
2. **"The one instrument DEC-529 names."** DEC-529 names no ticker (its item 2 leaves the ticker
   to the founder's confirmed mandate). The reading: the confirmed mandate pins exactly one
   instrument and the bound order is on it. Both caps are checked: the mandate's own
   `max_order_usd`, and the bound order's value, so a mandate edited above the cap and an order
   sized above it are each refused even if the gate would have caught them.
3. **Only the method is admitted.** On `live`, a grant whose `cli_confirm` evidence fails only for
   its method counts when the judge admits it. Missing, stale, and reused evidence are refused as
   before, and every other check (1 to 5, and 7) is unchanged. A refusal is journaled with the
   existing reason `step_up_method`, so no journal schema changes. A skip needs no step-up on
   `live` either (PX-7).
4. **Where the scope comes from.**
   - *Runtime:* `ApprovalSettings::live_cli_confirm`, set by the trusted run context: the
     connection's broker, the confirmed mandate's `max_order_usd` and pinned instruments, and the
     workspace owner. `None` keeps refusing. The shell's adapters set `None` today; the live
     runner (DEC-529 item 1) fills it from the connection record and the mandate when it is wired,
     and until then no live grant is admitted, which is the conservative reading.
   - *CLI:* on a request whose agent-stream envelope is `live`, `approve` reads the bound mandate
     version from the store and the control stream's latest record for its `connection_id`. Only a
     `ConnectionEstablished` with `environment` `live` gives a scope; a revoked, missing, or paper
     record gives none. The workspace owner is the user who recorded that connection with
     `cli_confirm` (DEC-529 items 3 and 11), so the owner running `approve` must be that user. This
     is the least-risk reading under DEC-929: it admits at most the one user who attested the
     account. A later re-record by another user moves it; E9-4's identity replaces it. A request
     whose envelope does not say `paper` is judged as `live`, so a missing environment fails
     closed; a `paper` request is judged as before.
   - *Backtest:* the runtime passes the scope to admission only when the stream is `live`, so a
     backtest view never admits `cli_confirm`, whatever scope it carries.
5. **Journal before release (DEC-923 item 2).** The admission is the `ApprovalResponded` with
   `result` `admitted`, whose `responder` is the approver and whose `step_up.method` is
   `cli_confirm`; it is written in the same batch as, and before, the `ApprovalRevalidated` and
   the `IntentProposed` it releases, and the intent is journaled before it is handed (rule 5). The
   grant is the control stream's `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` with the same members, committed
   before the runtime reads it. No new event or member is needed.
6. **Risk reduction is untouched (DEC-923 item 4, rule 13).** An exit is never asked, so it never
   reaches admission. Owner commands and the kill switch keep their existing step-up judgment;
   this decision does not extend DEC-923 to them.
7. **Lapse.** DEC-923 item 3: when E9-4's step-up lands, a later decision retires the judge and
   the `live_cli_confirm` field.

### Rationale

DEC-923 states conditions, not a mechanism. One pure judge in the layer-1 approval core keeps the
runtime and the CLI on one reading, and keeping the refusal reason `step_up_method` means the
narrowing needs no protected-path spec change. Leaving the shell's scope `None` until the live
runner is wired means nothing becomes admissible by accident.

### Alternatives considered

- A new `Environment` variant for the scoped live stream: it would leak DEC-923 into every
  environment match, owner commands and the kill switch included, which DEC-923 does not cover.
- A new refusal reason per condition in `ApprovalResponded`: a journal spec change, and the
  refusal is already recorded as the method's.
- Taking the workspace owner from a CLI flag: an untrusted surface naming its own owner.

<a id="dec-928"></a>

## DEC-928: The shell binds the Robinhood connector over one long-lived runtime, and refuses to reconcile a Robinhood account until its account fields have a source

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted; item 3 Accepted (agent, under DEC-929), superseded by DEC-933 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The Claude Code builder of lane C1, under DEC-79's delegation of reversible engineering decisions and DEC-176's rule that a gap is resolved by the reading that adds no risk |
| **Story or claim** | E7-6; the first live trade brief's C1 wiring (DEC-531); the lane's claim issue and PR |

### Decision

1. **The tool seam is `mandate-mcp`'s.** The `Tools` trait (one `tools/call`, DEC-860 item 2)
   moves from `mandate-robinhood` to `mandate-mcp`, which implements it for `McpClient`: the
   client's allowlist, halt and budget refuse exactly as `McpClient::call_tool` does, and the
   answer is the server's `result` JSON byte for byte. `mandate-robinhood` re-exports it, so
   `mandate_robinhood::Tools` still names it. The implementation sits beside the client because
   only `mandate-mcp`'s own test build may dial its loopback test server over plain `http`
   (DEC-849 item 2), so only there can a test judge it.
2. **One runtime for the connector's life.** `mandate_shell::robinhood::RobinhoodBridge` holds a
   tokio runtime and the one `RobinhoodConnector`; the shell's `Connector`
   (`RobinhoodShellConnector`) drives each `BrokerConnector::call` to its end on that runtime.
   Unlike the Alpaca connector's runtime, built per call, it is not rebuilt: an MCP session's HTTP
   connections belong to the runtime they were opened on, so the client is connected on the
   bridge's runtime and every later call is made on it. `Connector::profile` is
   `robinhood_profile`, never the trait's Alpaca default.
3. **The Robinhood reconciler reads, then refuses.** `RobinhoodReconciler::snapshot` reads the
   agentic account's portfolio, positions and orders through `account_snapshot` (each read scoped
   to the recorded account, CN-8, DEC-875) and then answers `Cause::Absent` (`STATUS_UNPUBLISHED`),
   so the run stops at `Stage::Reconcile` before it opens anything. `BrokerAccount` takes the
   account's status flags, equity, last equity, maintenance margin and multiplier from the broker
   (trading spec §7.2 and §7.3), and Robinhood publishes none of them (connections spec U-R6,
   U-R7); inventing them would put figures in `AccountSnapshotRecorded` that no broker gave
   (`AGENTS.md` rule 3). A read that is refused or unreadable answers `ACCOUNT_UNREAD` instead.
   `reconcile` refuses every snapshot the same way, since none is ever handed out.
4. **The shell's simulator tests have their own harness crate,** `mandate-shell-rh-sim` (layer
   11, tests only). They cannot live in `mandate-rh-sim`: a simulator harness judges the crate at
   the top of what it drives (DEC-866), so a `mandate-shell` dev-dependency there would make the
   shell the top and drop `mandate-robinhood` from the mutants `mandate-rh-sim`'s tests judge.
   They cannot live in `mandate-shell`, which may not depend on the simulator. The harness takes
   `mandate-rh-sim`'s shared test fixtures by path rather than copying them.
5. **Left for later lanes:** the runner's ports and `connect()` choosing this pair from the
   connection record, and `mandate-shell`'s `control.rs`, are unchanged.

### For the founder

Item 3 answers a founder-reserved question with the least-risk option, as DEC-929 directs, and
the founder may replace it with a later decision. It holds every Robinhood run at its startup reconciliation, which the live run needs to
pass. How Robinhood's account is read into `BrokerAccount` is the founder's to decide, because
any answer either invents a broker fact or weakens §7.3's gate requirement that the broker report
the account active. The recommendation, for a later decision: `status` `ACTIVE` and every blocked
flag `false` exactly when `get_accounts` lists the recorded account with `agentic_allowed` true
(any other answer already refuses the read); `multiplier` 1 on the strength of DEC-529 item 11's
attestation; `cash` and `buying_power` from `get_portfolio`, with `non_marginable_buying_power`
the lower of the two (DEC-620 item 2); and `equity`, `last_equity` and `maintenance_margin`
either added to the assumed `get_portfolio` shape (DEC-902) once the founder's run shows real
fields, or set to `cash` and zero with the reconciler journaling that they were not read.

### Rationale

The seam belongs with the client it abstracts, and putting the implementation there makes it
testable without a TLS fixture or a new dependency. A runtime per call would drop the HTTP
connections an MCP session's later calls reuse. Refusing the reconciliation is the reading that
adds no risk: the run stops before any order, and nothing is journaled that the broker did not
say.

### Alternatives considered

- **`impl Tools for McpClient` in `mandate-robinhood`:** no test outside `mandate-mcp` can build
  a connected client over plain `http`, so its mutants could not be killed.
- **A runtime per call, as `AlpacaConnector` does:** breaks the MCP session's pooled
  connections.
- **Filling `BrokerAccount` with the recommendation above now:** decides a founder question
  (§7.3's status from the broker) inside an engineering lane.
- **Shell tests in `mandate-rh-sim`:** weakens the mutation coverage of `mandate-robinhood`.

<a id="dec-929"></a>

## DEC-929: Until R0, the coordinator decides without asking, taking the most conservative option on founder-reserved questions

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder (kunwarshivam): "I need everything to be autonomous, you do things and dont ask me to" |
| **Story or claim** | R0 ([first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md)); DEC-79; DEC-176 |

### Decision

1. Until R0's evidence PR merges, the coordinator and its builders do not stop to ask the founder.
   Engineering and process questions are decided by the agent as DEC-79 and DEC-176 already allow.
2. A question DEC-79 reserves for the founder is decided by taking the option that adds the least
   risk. Each such call is recorded in a decision file with the status `Accepted (agent, under
   DEC-929)` and listed in the coordinator's report, so the founder can revert it after the fact.
3. Unchanged: no agent places a real order, enters or reads a credential, spends money, or
   weakens a safety rule or a non-negotiable in AGENTS.md. Green CI, the independent
   different-model review, and zero missed mutants still gate every safety-critical merge.
   The founder's own steps (entering paper or live credentials, E2, the R0 live order) stay the
   founder's.

### Rationale

The founder has about a week; waiting on answers is now the critical path.

### Alternatives considered

- Keep asking for each founder-reserved question: slower, and the founder asked for this to stop.

<a id="dec-931"></a>

## DEC-931: the mutation job optimises what no mutant rebuilds, so a `mandate-executor` shard's tests fit their cap

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, under DEC-176: it lets the gate judge mutants it could not judge before, and removes, lowers, or skips no mutant, test, cap, baseline, or check; CI and xtask are founder-owned in `CODEOWNERS`, so the founder may overturn it) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The mutants-executor-timeout builder (agent), on the coordinator's choice of option 1, under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | CI correction for [#1337](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1337)'s `mutants (k/21)` timeouts; [DEC-464](earlier-303-599.md#dec-464), [DEC-498](earlier-303-599.md#dec-498), [DEC-519](earlier-303-599.md#dec-519), [DEC-538](earlier-303-599.md#dec-538), [DEC-873](earlier-600-899.md#dec-873) item 5 |

### Decision

1. **Every build the mutation job makes is at `opt-level = 1`**, for the `dev` profile and the
   `test` profile that inherits it, except in the packages named in item 2. xtask adds
   `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_OPT_LEVEL=1` and `CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_OPT_LEVEL=1` to `MUTANT_BUILD_PROFILE`,
   beside DEC-519's no-debuginfo settings. The `mutants` matrix sets the same two values, so they
   are part of rust-cache's key, as DEC-519 does for debuginfo.
2. **The packages a mutant can make cargo rebuild stay at `opt-level = 0`.** These are each
   package with a mutant in the run, and each workspace package built on one of them through any
   kind of dependency (`unoptimised_packages`, from the `workspace_closure` DEC-497 already uses).
   xtask passes `--config=profile.{dev,test}.package.<name>.opt-level=0` for each one. It goes to
   the pre-flight's `cargo nextest list` and, as `--cargo-arg`, to every cargo invocation of
   `cargo mutants`: the baseline's and each mutant's, build and test alike. A mutant therefore
   rebuilds what it rebuilt before, at the cost it had. Cargo has no environment form for a
   package override, so this part is arguments, not environment.
3. **Debug assertions and overflow checks stay on.** Cargo's `dev` profile enables both whatever
   its `opt-level` is. `the_mutants_job_keeps_assertions_and_overflow_checks` holds that the
   workspace `Cargo.toml` turns neither off for `dev` or `test`, and it was shown to catch a
   manifest that does. Optimisation changes how fast a test runs, not what it asserts.
4. **DEC-498's caps and budget stand unchanged.** The caps are `MUTANT_TEST_TIMEOUT` 180 s (it
   also caps the baseline's tests) and `MUTANT_BUILD_TIMEOUT` 60 s, so the three capped phases
   still cost at most 420 of a shard's 600 seconds. The matrix, one mutant per shard, one worker,
   the tested packages, the baseline, the pre-flight and the zero-survivor rule are untouched.
5. **DEC-873 item 5's closed world admits exactly two more names**, `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_OPT_LEVEL`
   and `CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_OPT_LEVEL`, so seven in all. An optimisation level selects no feature,
   dependency or `cfg`, so the live-build check reads it as it reads the debuginfo names. The
   release profile's level stays refused, and so does every name that could turn debug assertions
   or overflow checks off: `every_cargo_name_but_the_seven_cannot_be_read` names them. That is the
   one edit this decision makes to DEC-873, recorded on its status line.

### Rationale

#### What failed

#1337 (E7-6, touching `mandate-journal` and `mandate-executor`) failed 14 of its 21 shards in run
[38866039803](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/actions/runs/38866039803). Its mutants
were never the problem. The **unmutated baseline** timed out: `TIMEOUT Unmutated baseline in 26s
build + 180s test`. Nextest ran 648 of the 709 `mandate-executor` tests, all passing, in 179.6
seconds, then sent SIGTERM. The runner ran two tests at a time. The finished tests' times add up
to 346 seconds, mostly the `properties` suite (8 to 24 s each) and `rule_13_holds_over_random_scripts`
(41 s), with about twenty properties still to run. The same failure was seen before #1345's
profile fix, so it comes from the suite, not from the diff. No `mandate-executor` mutant could
be judged at all, so the gate failed every PR that changes that crate.

#### Why not raise the cap

DEC-498 item 5 prices the baseline's tests and a mutant's tests at one cap each. An unoptimised
executor phase of roughly 285 seconds would need the baseline cap and the mutant cap together to
use more than the ten-minute limit before any remainder (DEC-464). Fewer proptest cases or bigger
runners were ruled out: the first judges mutants by less evidence, and the second is spending.

#### Why not optimise everything

Run [38870140804](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/actions/runs/38870140804) (a
`workflow_dispatch` of #1337's head with every build at `opt-level = 1`, from a cold cache)
brought the executor baselines down to **21 to 40 s** of test. But every viable mutant it reached
ran out of its build, `TIMEOUT … in 60s build`, because the mutated crate and everything built on
it were now compiled optimised. Four of its shards never started: "recent account payments have
failed or your spending limit needs to be increased". So CI was not run again (below).

#### What this decision measured

Measured locally on #1337's head (4 cores, nextest at 2 test threads as on CI's runner,
`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, debuginfo off). The mixed profile is items 1 and 2: optimised, with
`mandate-executor`, `mandate-journal` and `mandate-refcases` kept at 0.

| Phase | Unoptimised | Mixed profile |
|---|---|---|
| `cargo nextest run -p mandate-executor`, all 709 passing | 163 s | 41 to 43 s (86 s at one test thread) |
| Rebuild of the three tested packages after a `mandate-executor` edit | 19 s | 19 s |
| The same after a `mandate-journal` edit | 25 s | 26 s |
| Cold build of the three tested packages, `-j2` | 39 s | 59 s |

The test phase is about four times shorter and the mutant rebuild is unchanged. Scaled to CI by
the unoptimised ratio (285 s on CI against 163 s here), the executor baseline should take about
75 s of its 180-second cap. The suite's hot code is in the layers under the executor and in
dependencies (`sha2`, `ruint`, `rust_decimal`, `proptest`), which this profile optimises.

End to end, `MANDATE_MUTANT_SHARD=0/21 cargo xtask ci mutants` on #1337's head with this change
reported `ok Unmutated baseline in 30s build + 43s test` and `1 mutant tested in 2m: 1 caught`,
in 179 seconds. That shard's mutant (`schema_version -> u64 with 0`) was a 60-second build
timeout under the all-optimised profile.

#### The remainder (DEC-498 item 6, item 9)

Not measured on CI, because billing stopped the second run: the cold-cache cost. Changing the
matrix's environment changes rust-cache's key, and optimised dependencies take about half as long
again to build from cold (39 s against 59 s here). A PR's first run after this merges therefore
builds its dependencies from nothing. The all-optimised cold run spent 4m10s in the pre-flight
and 85 to 132 s in the baseline build. A shard there that judged one mutant at the build cap took
9m27s, and this profile compiles the mutated layers unoptimised, which is cheaper than that run.
This term is named, not bounded. If it overruns, the failure is loud (a cancelled job at ten
minutes, which `full` refuses), and DEC-498 item 8 applies: the matrix is not the answer.

### What holds it

- `the_mutants_job_builds_without_debuginfo` requires the four `MUTANT_BUILD_PROFILE` settings on
  the job's building children and in the matrix's environment.
- `only_what_a_mutant_rebuilds_stays_unoptimised` checks `unoptimised_packages` on a fixture graph
  and on the repository's own. A `mandate-executor` mutant keeps `mandate-executor`,
  `mandate-refcases` and `mandate-shell` unoptimised and optimises `mandate-journal`,
  `mandate-canon` and `mandate-num`. The test also requires the configuration on the pre-flight
  and, as `--cargo-arg`, on the run. It fails on a seeded bug that keeps only the mutated
  packages themselves unoptimised.
- `the_mutants_job_keeps_assertions_and_overflow_checks` covers item 3.
- `every_cargo_name_but_the_seven_cannot_be_read` and
  `a_repository_shaped_workflow_passes_the_closed_world` cover item 5.

<a id="dec-932"></a>

## DEC-932: The landing page's long page is decluttered: one header, no flying owl, thread, moon, broker strip or painting, three parts, and plainer copy

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder (kunwarshivam), shown the long page: "the owl and the trail that follows seems cluttered. okay lets do all of these in your list. and refine the copy" |
| **Story or claim** | Landing page (DEC-907, DEC-908) |

### Decision

1. **One header.** The long page's bar (`scroll-bar.tsx`) is pinned to the top only once the page has
   covered the desktop, and is hidden (and inert) before, so it is never on screen with the desktop's
   own bar. Sign in and Sign up stay in view throughout (DEC-906 item 8). Amends DEC-907 item 1.
2. **Removed:** the flying voxel owl (`owl-flight.tsx`, DEC-907 item 5), the pixel thread
   (`pixel-thread.tsx`, item 9), the moon and its reflection over the pixel sea (DEC-908), the broker
   strip under the opening, and the Met painting behind the request form (`plate.tsx`). The sea's
   waves and the lock screen's night stay.
3. **Three parts, not four.** "Your rules" and "Set the stakes" become one part, limits in dollars,
   with the gap caveat beside it; the verdict and check pictures are dropped. Then asking you (on
   tide) and the reason behind every move.
4. **"It won't" is a plain list** under "What it won't do.", on paper, with no strike-throughs.
5. **Copy** is shorter and plainer; the opening's button reads "Join the beta".
6. **The request for a place closes on sun**: its heading and words in ink on a field of sun, the
   form in a card beside them.
7. **Quietly alive.** The brand owl stands still on the top edge of the limits picture, the
   reasons picture and the request form (`Watcher`), blinking and following the pointer with its
   eyes as every owl on the site does; nothing flies. With the waves, the lock screen's stars and
   the pieces settling, that is all the page's motion.

<a id="dec-933"></a>

## DEC-933: A Robinhood account is read by the least-risk mapping, its preflight is Robinhood's account beside Alpaca's market reads, and the live door's lapse takes the real snapshot and journal rows

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, under DEC-929): item 1 answers the founder-reserved question DEC-928's note left open, by its recommendation and the least-risk value for every unpublished figure; items 2 to 4 are reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The Claude Code builder of brief row C4's shell half, under DEC-929 (item 1) and DEC-79 and DEC-176 (items 2 to 4) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-6; claim [#1353](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1353); the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md)'s C4; DEC-529 items 3, 11 and 12; DEC-470 item 1; DEC-928 item 3; DEC-918; DEC-919; DEC-921 (one PR, tests first) |

### Context

DEC-928 item 3 made `RobinhoodReconciler` read the agentic account and then refuse every
Robinhood run at `Stage::Reconcile`, because `BrokerAccount` takes a status, flags, equity, last
equity, maintenance margin and multiplier from the broker and Robinhood publishes none of them
(connections spec U-R6, U-R7). Its note left the mapping to the founder; DEC-929 says such a
question is decided by the option that adds the least risk and recorded for the founder to
revert. The runner's live door, meanwhile, called the lapse with an empty read and zero counts
(DEC-919's "trap").

### Decision

1. **The account mapping** (`mandate_shell::robinhood::robinhood_account`). From the agentic
   account's `AccountSnapshot`:
   - `cash` is `get_portfolio`'s, as reported; `buying_power` and `non_marginable_buying_power`
     are the lower of `get_portfolio`'s cash and buying power (DEC-620 item 2), so no buying
     power figure the gate or the executor reads ever exceeds cash;
   - `status` is `ACTIVE` and `trading_blocked`, `account_blocked` and
     `trade_suspended_by_user` are `false`: a snapshot exists only when each of its reads found
     the recorded account listed by `get_accounts` with `agentic_allowed` (DEC-875), and any
     other answer refuses the read (DEC-928's recommendation);
   - every figure Robinhood does not publish takes the value that adds no buying power and
     lifts no limit: `crypto_status` `INACTIVE` (no crypto opening), `multiplier` 1 (DEC-529
     item 11's attestation), `equity` the cash alone (a long-only account's equity is never
     less), `last_equity` zero (below every equity threshold: the gate reads §9.2's `legacy_pdt`
     budget only for a margin account, so under the declared cash account it decides nothing
     today, and were the type ever margin the budget would always apply),
     `maintenance_margin` the whole equity (no maintenance excess), `accrued_fees` zero;
   - the account rules are a cash account under `legacy_pdt` (trading spec §7.2's Robinhood
     row; `robinhood_account_rules`).

   `RobinhoodReconciler` now hands that snapshot, with the broker's positions and working
   orders, to the executor's own reconciliation, as Alpaca's is. Robinhood publishes no account
   activity, so the snapshot carries no fill and leaves the executor's cursor where it was; the
   live run starts only on a flat, unspent account (DEC-529 item 3), so no fill is outstanding.
   `STATUS_UNPUBLISHED` is removed; a refused or unreadable read still answers
   `ACCOUNT_UNREAD`.
2. **The preflight facts of a Robinhood connection** (`mandate_shell::paper::robinhood_facts`).
   The account, positions and working orders are `mandate-robinhood`'s preflight (read through
   `RobinhoodBridge::preflight_facts` on the bridge's runtime); the asset record, the latest IEX
   quote and the minute bars are Alpaca's market reads (`market_facts`, the reads `preflight`
   already made, split out unchanged). The gate's quote stays the IEX quote: it is the one whose
   age the run can judge (DEC-902 item 7: Robinhood's quote has no time field). The Robinhood
   quote must name the same instrument, or the facts refuse. The runner's
   `robinhood_cross_check` then compares Robinhood's ask (the broker's) with the IEX ask (the
   independent one) through `cross_check_the_opening`, inside the registered collar's own `x`
   for the instrument's 20-day median dollar volume (DEC-529 item 12, DEC-912 item 2); quotes of
   two instruments refuse `quote_absent`.
3. **The live door's real inputs** (`mandate_paper::lapse_read`). `the_live_door` takes the
   agentic account's snapshot and calls `lapse_read`, which for a live connection counts `Held`
   from that snapshot (every position record, a zero quantity included, and every working order;
   DEC-470 item 1) and reads the rows of the account stream `account_stream` names from the run's
   own journal, ungated by `--place-one-order` (DEC-918 item 2); a journalless run reads none.
   A Postgres-backed test appends a `FillApplied` to the account stream and sees the record
   spent. A live connection with no snapshot refuses `lapse_evidence_not_supplied` through
   DEC-919's `unsupplied`, before any read; one whose account stream cannot be read refuses at
   `Stage::Reconcile`. A paper connection makes no check and reads nothing (LT-14). The empty-read
   literal is gone. `run()` hands no snapshot until the runner's ports connect the Robinhood
   connector (DEC-928 item 5, another lane), so a live connection refuses at the door until then.
4. **Unchanged:** the Alpaca paper run (its preflight makes the same reads in the same order;
   the door makes no check on a paper connection), `control.rs`'s live refusal, and the runner's
   `Ports` and `connect()`.

### For the founder

Item 1 answers the question DEC-928 left for you, by the reading that adds the least risk. It
journals `AccountSnapshotRecorded` figures Robinhood did not state (`equity` as the cash, zero
`last_equity`), each the value that can only tighten a limit; replace it with a later decision if
the founder's run shows real fields (DEC-902).

### Rationale

Each unpublished figure is set where an error can only refuse more: understated equity tightens
every fraction-of-equity limit, zero prior-close equity keeps the day-trade budget on, no
maintenance excess and no crypto status open nothing, and 1× with the lower of cash and buying
power spends no margin. The cash account type, and so the settled-cash rule that binds in place
of the day-trade budget, rests on the founder's attestation (DEC-529 item 11). Keeping the IEX quote as the gate's, with Robinhood's checked against it
before anything runs, keeps the freshness check the opening already requires. Counting `Held`
from the broker's own snapshot type, and reading the rows from the journal, gives the lapse
evidence no literal can stand in for.

### Alternatives considered

- **Keep refusing (DEC-928 item 3):** the live run could never pass startup reconciliation.
- **`equity` and `last_equity` zero:** zero equity refuses every opening, so the live order could
  never be placed; the cash is the least equity a long-only account can have.
- **Robinhood's quote as the gate's quote:** it carries no time, so the opening's age check could
  not be made; DEC-529 item 12's agreement is kept by the cross-check instead.
- **A `Ports` method for the account stream and the snapshot:** the runner's ports belong to the
  lane that connects the Robinhood connector (DEC-928 item 5); `lapse_read` reads the journal from
  the run's own DSN and takes the snapshot as an argument, so that lane only hands it in.

<a id="dec-934"></a>

## DEC-934: The shell admits a live deployment only on four typed run inputs, and every caller passes the refusing value until the runner holds them

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176 — each reading refuses at least as much as DEC-924 item 1 asks, and nothing on `main` is admitted by it) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, the shell live-admission builder), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-929 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim [#1360](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1360); DEC-924 items 1 to 3; DEC-910 item 2; DEC-918 item 3; DEC-919; DEC-921; LT-1, LT-9, R0 |

### Context

DEC-924 item 1 replaces `mandate-shell`'s unconditional `DeploymentRefusal::Live` with an
admission that needs four conditions: the binary is built with `live`; the run passed
`--confirm-live`; the live door's connection and unspent checks passed on real inputs; and the MCP
host is the pinned Robinhood host constant. Item 2: any one missing still refuses `Live` before a
credential is read. None of the four exists on `main` in a form the shell can read: the shell may
not declare `live` (`xtask/layers.toml` gives it to the runner alone), and the flag, the host
constant and the door's real lapse inputs are being built by other lanes (#1354, #1356).

### Decision

1. **Where the inputs go.** `RunFacts`, "what the run supplies beside the stream", gains a
   required field `live: LiveAdmission`. `confirmed_version`'s signature is unchanged, and no
   caller can build `RunFacts` without saying what it supplies, because the field has no default.
2. **One type per condition.** `LiveAdmission { build: Build, confirmation: Confirmation, door:
   DoorChecks, host: McpHost }`. `Build` is `Default` or `Live`; `Confirmation` is `Absent` or
   `ConfirmLive`; `DoorChecks` is `NotPassed` or `Passed`; `McpHost` holds the host the run dials
   (`Option<String>`) and the pinned constant (`Option<&'static str>`). Two-variant enums rather
   than `bool`s, so a call site names which condition it sets.
3. **The host condition.** It holds only when both are present, the pin is not empty, and the
   dialed host equals the pin byte for byte: no case folding, no trailing-dot or other
   normalization. The shell does not hold a copy of the constant: DEC-924 item 3 puts it in one
   place behind `live`, which is the runner (#1354's `PINNED_HOST`), so the runner hands it in. A
   build that pins no host hands `None` and is refused.
4. **The refusing value.** `LiveAdmission::refusing()` has all four conditions missing. Every
   current caller passes it: `mandate-paper`'s `run()` and the shell's own tests. A live
