   deployment therefore refuses `Live` exactly as before this decision, in every build, until the
   lanes building `--confirm-live`, the host constant and the door's real inputs replace it in
   `run()`. That includes the build condition, although the runner could state its build today:
   one admission value per call site, replaced as a whole by the lane that wires the live arm, is
   easier to review than four values arriving in four PRs.
5. **Where it is checked.** In phase 1, at the same line the unconditional refusal stood:
   after the document is read and parsed, before the fold and before anything reads a credential
   (phase 1 takes none). A live deployment that is admitted goes on to the fold unchanged, so
   every V-rule still applies to it, V-001 on its connection included. A paper deployment never
   reads the admission.
6. **What the door condition means.** The shell cannot tell a real read from a placeholder. The
   runner may hand `DoorChecks::Passed` only after `chosen` and `unspent` both returned `Ok` on
   the run's own control-stream records, its journal read of the account stream and its snapshot's
   held counts (DEC-918 item 1, DEC-919 item 2). `unsupplied`'s refusal already stops a live
   connection at the door before the shell is reached, so today's runner could not reach the
   shell with a live connection even if it passed `Passed`. The review of the PR that passes it
   judges that the inputs are real.

### Rationale

Each reading refuses at least as much as DEC-924 item 1. Typed inputs on `RunFacts` make the
admission explicit at every call site without changing `confirmed_version`'s signature, which
the runner's source-shape test pins. Exact host equality refuses any variant of the pinned name.
Passing the refusing value everywhere means this PR admits nothing on its own: the founder's lock
opens only when the four inputs are wired, each by a reviewed PR.

### Alternatives considered

- **Add a fourth parameter to `confirmed_version`:** rejected. It would change every call site and
  the runner's source-shape pin for no gain over a required field on the struct that already
  carries the run's facts.
- **A copy of the host constant in the shell:** rejected. DEC-924 item 3 says one place, and two
  copies can drift.
- **Pass the runner's real build (`cfg!(feature = "live")`) now:** rejected for this PR, see
  item 4. It would change no outcome, since the other three conditions still refuse.
- **`bool` fields:** rejected. `LiveAdmission { true, false, true, .. }` does not say which
  condition is which.
- **Normalize the host (lower-case, strip a trailing dot) before comparing:** rejected. It admits
  more names than the constant; the runner dials the constant itself, so exact equality costs
  nothing.

<a id="dec-935"></a>

## DEC-935: The paper run judges its broker facts at a clock read taken after the last of them

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted; item 2's preflight quote check amended by DEC-936 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | claude-code (builder), under DEC-176: the reading tightens no bound less and adds no risk |
| **Story or claim** | E2, [first paper trade brief](../tasks/first-paper-trade.md); claim #1357; evidence #1355; DEC-921 |

### Decision

1. `mandate-paper`'s `run()` reads its run clock once, **after** the preflight's last broker
   read (account, positions, open orders, asset record, quote, minute bars), and judges,
   computes and records everything after the preflight at that instant: the shell judge (asset
   record, ETP classification date, quote, regular session, close window), the liquidity
   figures, the daily closes' trust check, the model's evaluation, the contexts, the watch's
   bound and the cycle's clock. The configuration's date is the clock's date at the start of the
   run, before the control stream is read.
2. No bound moves. Every age bound (the asset record's and the quote's `iex_quote_max_age_s`)
   is measured from that later read, so each fact is at least as old as it was at the start's
   clock, never younger; a fact stamped after that read (`AheadOfClock`, `quote_at > now`) is
   refused as before; the quote's own age check at its read, inside the preflight, is
   unchanged; the binding gate rechecks the quote and the session at its own clock immediately
   before submission, as before.
3. The configuration's date and the run clock's date cannot differ for a run that places: the
   UTC date changes at 19:00 or 20:00 New York, after the regular session's close, so the
   judge's regular-session check refuses any run whose later read falls on another date.

### Rationale

#1355's dry run refused every real run: the asset record is stamped at its own read
(`TradingClient::asset`), always after a clock read taken at the start, and the judge refused
it as ahead of the clock ("Validate: a current asset record"). The run's tests passed because
their clocks never moved between reads. Measuring from a read taken after every fact is the
only reading under which "a fact stamped after the clock is refused" and "every fact the run
read is eligible" hold together. It shortens no window and drops no check: against the start's
clock, any fact the run accepts now is older, so each age test is stricter, and the
future-stamp test applies to the same facts at a clock that is no earlier than any of their
reads.

The ETP classification date and the daily bars' last session are compared with the later
clock too; both come from records read after the start, so the start's clock could refuse a
record written while the run read it, the same defect. A later clock accepts a record dated
between the start and the read only where the record was already there to be read.

### Alternatives considered

- **Widen the judge with a skew tolerance.** Refused: it loosens a bound and lets a
  future-stamped fact through.
- **Re-read the clock inside the judge for each fact.** Rejected: the judge stays a function of
  its inputs (no clock read in the shell), and one read after the last fact gives each fact a
  clock taken after it with one instant for the whole judgment.
- **Stamp the asset record with the start's clock.** Refused: it would make the record look
  older or younger than it is, and its age would no longer be its own.

### Not covered

#1355 also shows "Reconcile: a current IEX quote" twice. That refusal is the quote's own age
check at its read in the preflight, which already measures from a clock read after the quote
arrives; a quote stamped ahead of this host's clock (clock skew) or older than 10 s refuses
there, by design, and this decision changes nothing about it.

<a id="dec-936"></a>

## DEC-936: A 250 ms clock-skew tolerance on the paper preflight's IEX quote check

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | the founder (DEC-79: it loosens a bound DEC-935 kept) |
| **Story or claim** | E2, [first paper trade brief](../tasks/first-paper-trade.md); claim #1362; evidence #1355; amends DEC-935 item 2 and its "Not covered" |

### Decision

1. The paper preflight's IEX quote check (`DataClient::latest_quote`, judged by
   `judge_quote_age` in `mandate-alpaca`) accepts a quote whose own stamp `t` is up to
   **250 ms** (`IEX_QUOTE_CLOCK_SKEW`) ahead of the host clock read at the quote's read. More than
   250 ms ahead, by even a nanosecond, is still refused `AheadOfClock`. A crypto quote keeps no
   tolerance: the founder's approval names the IEX quote only.
2. The 10 s maximum age (`iex_quote_max_age_s`) is unchanged. It is measured from the later of
   the quote's `t` and the host clock, so a quote stamped ahead of the clock is aged zero, never
   negative, and a quote 10 s and a nanosecond older than the clock is `Stale`.
3. The observed skew is recorded on the quote the check returns, as `LatestQuote::clock_skew`:
   how far `t` was ahead of the host clock at the read, zero when it was not ahead. A quote parsed
   without a clock read carries `None`.
4. Nothing else changes. The asset record's age check (`AssetSnapshot::current`), the shell
   judge's quote check at the run clock and the binding gate's recheck immediately before
   submission (both DEC-935 item 2), and the risk gate's freshness rule (DEC-163 item 1) still
   refuse a quote stamped after their own clock. A quote within the tolerance that is still ahead
   of the run clock refuses at "Validate: a current, uncrossed IEX quote"; one the clock has
   passed by then is judged as any other.

### Rationale

#1355's E2 runs refused "Reconcile: a current IEX quote" with `AheadOfClock`: the IEX quote's
`t` was 2 to 24 ms ahead of the host clock, and the host could not reach NTP to correct it. A
stamp a few milliseconds ahead says the two clocks disagree, not that the quote is old; 250 ms is
an order of magnitude above the skew observed and far below the 10 s age bound, so a quote
accepted under it is at most 10.25 s old by the exchange's clock. Measuring the age from the later
instant keeps the bound from ever reading a future stamp as extra freshness. Recording the skew
makes a drifting host visible in the facts before it reaches the tolerance.

### Alternatives considered

- **Keep refusing (DEC-935).** Every E2 run on a host whose clock trails the data host's refuses,
  and fixing the host clock is not in the run's reach where NTP is blocked.
- **Widen every quote check, including the shell judge and the binding gate.** Not approved: the
  founder approved the preflight check only.
- **Shift the host clock by the observed skew.** Rejected: one clock adjusted by a fact it judges
  would let the quote set its own age.

<a id="dec-938"></a>

## DEC-938: The paper run journals its own fill, and a classifier refusal stops at Classify

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Claude (builder agent), under DEC-79 (reversible engineering decisions) and DEC-176 (items 2 to 4: each reading adds no risk and weakens no safety invariant) |
| **Story or claim** | E2, the first paper trade; the defects #1355 recorded; DEC-921 (one PR, tests first) |

### Decision

The first paper fill (1 SPY at 763.75, 2026-10-16) stopped the run with `the tracer stopped at
Journal: the journal answered Invalid`. The root cause was that Alpaca names an equity by its
ticker (`SPY`) in every answer, a `FILL` activity included, while the executor names it by the
asset id it sent. The executor's fill check compares instruments, so the run's own fill read as
another instrument's: external activity. Its first record, `ExternalActivityIngested`, has no
registered schema, so the journal refused the whole batch. Behind that, the fill records were not
in the shape the journal registers either, and the late fill's reconciliation could not run.

1. **The connector names a registered instrument by its asset id.** `mandate_shell::symbols`
   maps each ticker the run registered (the confirmed mandate's pinned instrument: its `symbol`
   and `asset_id`) to its asset id. It does this in every broker answer: orders and their legs,
   positions, fills, and listed orders. A ticker the run did not register stays as the broker
   spelled it, so an instrument the run does not trade is still external activity.
2. **Fill records take the registered shape.** `FillApplied` and `LateFillApplied` carry
   `instrument_id` (the fold already reads both spellings, DEC-446 item 7). `fees` is a list:
   empty when the broker reported none, as Alpaca's `FILL` never does, and otherwise one USD
   `commission`, `charged` (trading spec §6.2's only per-fill broker charge). The records carry the
   four `config_refs` the catalogue requires (`fee`, `cal`, `set`, `ins`), from a new
   `InstrumentSnapshot::fill_refs` port. The paper run registers no settlement calendar, and
   `mandate-accounting` settles the paper run on its registered trading calendar, so
   `settlement_calendar` names that object: the one the settlement was actually computed on. A
   snapshot that knows no configuration names none, and the journal refuses that fill.
3. **`LateFillApplied` is registered with `FillApplied`'s version-1 schema.** Journal spec §9's
   catalogue gives both one row, with the same required refs and fields. The entry read `filled`
   before its fill was ingested, so the fill is late (trading spec §5.7: a fill for an order in a
   terminal state) and has to be journaled as `LateFillApplied`.
4. **A classifier that cannot answer stops the step at Classify.** It leaves no classification,
   so it has no decider. Rule 5 of journal spec §9.1 needs a `decided_by` on any `DecisionMade`
   the dry run allowed, and the closed label set has no label for "the classifier could not
   answer". Before this change the fail-safe `deny` went out with `decided_by: null`, the journal
   refused it, and the refusal was reported as Journal. Now the shell poisons the step, as it does
   for a flatten planner that cannot plan. It reads the poison before it performs any effect, so
   nothing of the step is journaled or sent, and the run reports Classify. Nothing is sent either
   way; the record the journal would refuse is never drafted.
5. **A reconciliation that ingests a late fill asks for the next one after its own
   `ReconciliationRun`.** The request then reads on from the checkpoint that run recorded, not
   from one already passed. The shell gathers and runs that reconciliation the same way as the
   first, at startup and during the watch, and never steps its reads one by one. A fill is
   ingested once, so the next reconciliation asks for nothing more. If one still asks after two
   rounds, the run stops at Reconcile.

### Rationale

Every item makes the code meet the journal and trading specs as written. No validation is
loosened: the fill now passes the schema the journal already registered, and item 3 registers
the sibling record the catalogue defines with it. Items 2 and 4 are DEC-176 readings. Item 2
records the configuration the computation used. Item 4 stops rather than journaling an unlabelled
decision. Neither adds risk.

### Alternatives considered

- **A new `decided_by` label for the fail-safe deny (for example `fail_safe`).** That is a change
  to journal spec §9.1 and mandate spec §6.4's closed vocabulary, and the spec guard keeps spec
  changes out of code PRs. It is left as a follow-up for a spec PR, if the record is wanted.
- **Prefer the asset id the wire carries.** Orders and positions carry `asset_id`, but a `FILL`
  does not, so the registered ticker is the only key that covers every answer.
- **Treat a fill of an entry that read `filled` as an ordinary `FillApplied`.** That would go
  against trading spec §5.7's literal rule, and skip the reconciliation it requires.

### Not decided here (backlog)

- The executor reads the paper environment only from a `StreamOpened` payload member that the
  registered schema no longer carries. On the paper path it therefore books no simulated fee
  (trading spec §10), and `FeesCharged` has no registered schema yet.
- `ExternalActivityIngested` has no registered schema, and an unattributed `FillApplied` has no
  `client_order_id`, so a genuinely external fill is still refused at the journal.
- On the paper path the executor's account-stream records carry the risk clock
  `1970-01-01T00:00:00Z`: no tick reaches the executor before its first records.

<a id="dec-940"></a>

## DEC-940: The live cycle hands each wake's answers to the runtime and waits for the grant

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each one under DEC-176 — every reading holds, refuses or sends nothing where the code before it sent nothing too) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder, the G1b grant-cycle lane), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-921 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; G1b and LT-10 of the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md); claim [#1365](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1365); DEC-912, DEC-914, DEC-920, DEC-923 |

Before this, `wait_for_the_grant`, `answers`, `cross_check_the_opening`, `watched` and `lt10` in
`mandate-paper` had no production caller. A cycle whose classifier said `ask` journaled its
request and stopped with `autonomy_not_auto`, and the shell tailed the control stream only at a
cycle's open. Nothing in a run handed a later answer to the runtime, so an `ask` opening could
only die at its deadline. These are the readings the call sites take.

### Decision

1. **An asked cycle stays open (`ProductionCycle::run_observed_asked_watched`).** The shell opens
   the cycle as before. When the cycle never asks, the run is the run it was: the wait is never
   called and the watch asks the shell's own `watch_may_end`. When it asks:
   - a dry run (no `--place-one-order`) stops at the ask as before, because it appends to no
     journal an answer could reach;
   - a placing run hands its open session to the runner's wait as an `AskedCycle`.
   The session stays private to the shell (DEC-475); the runner reads, wakes and releases through
   three calls only: `agent_rows`, `wake`, and `submit`.
2. **The opening is held until the runner releases it.** While the wait runs, a risk-adding
   submission stops at the shell's broker door after its `OrderSubmitted` is journaled, exactly
   where a dry run's `would_place` stops, and is held. Only `AskedCycle::submit` sends it, once.
   A wait that stops, or returns with the opening still held, sends nothing and the run refuses
   (`Cause::Absent`, "the held opening the grant wait released"). The held order's journaled
   `OrderSubmitted` is then an unsent submission, which the next start's reconciliation resolves
   as it resolves a crash between the journal and the send. Exits and protective orders are
   never held (rule 13): the hold applies only to a purpose that adds risk.
3. **Each wake hands the answers to the runtime.** `wake(now)` moves the session's event time to
   `now`, reads the workspace control stream whole, hands every row to the runtime as
   `Input::Journal` in journal order (the runtime's copy fold makes repeats inert, EI-3), ticks
   the risk clock to `now`, and returns both streams as the journal reads them. The runtime then
   writes its own `ApprovalResponded` and `ApprovalRevalidated` (DEC-920 item 1). During a wake
   the classifier's `ask` is not a stop: re-validation re-classifies the bound order, and an
   `ask` by the same trigger is what the grant answered. The runtime's `ApprovalRevalidated`
   decides, and the runner's fold reads it. Every other refusal stops the wait.
4. **The runner's frame (`granted_opening`).** It binds the ask off the agent stream (DEC-914
   item 1), then `wait_for_the_grant` re-reads per wake through `AskedCycle::wake` at the watch's
   poll interval, so the release is `answers`/`grant` over the runtime's admitted, re-validated
   records and never a raw answer. Then `cross_check_the_opening` runs, and only then `submit`.
   The cross-check reads the registered gate's own collar members, the pinned instrument's asset
   class and 20-day median dollar volume, the preflight's IEX ask as the independent quote, and
   the broker's own ask from a new port, `Session::broker_ask`. For Alpaca that is the same IEX
   quote, so the check passes trivially on the paper path. For Robinhood it is `None` until the
   MCP session reads a quote (O2), so a live asked opening refuses `quote_absent` and nothing is
   sent.
5. **The asked run's watch ends through `watched` and `lt10`.** After the submit, the shell's
   watch asks `lt10_end`, which is `watch_may_end` (the cap, `watched`, `lt10`) in the shell's
   terms: settled ends the run, unsettled reads on, the cap fails closed with the shell's own
   `cancel_unconfirmed`, a bound no session holds refuses, and any other answer fails closed.
   A paper `auto` run keeps the shell's own `watch_may_end`; the two answer the same facts the
   same way.
6. **Gaps found, not fixed here.** Each fails closed: the opening is refused and nothing is
   sent. Until all three are fixed, no grant can open a position in any build:
   - **No mark reaches the runtime.** No production code journals `MarkUpdated`, so an ask
     records `reference_mark: null`, drift cannot be computed, and re-validation skips every
     grant with reason `drift` (DEC-156 item 3). Pinned by
     `a_grant_with_no_mark_is_skipped_and_never_released`.
   - **A granted intent has no execution policy.** `mandate-runtime`'s `bound_proposal` sets
     `execution: None`, and the production `ExecutorSink` refuses an order intent without one.
     Pinned by `a_granted_intent_without_its_policy_stops_at_the_sink`. The shell probes use a
     test-only sink that supplies the fixture's policy, so they can test the hold behind it.
   - **The grant is judged against the preflight's quote.** The binding gate judges the
     preflight quote's age at the executor's hand (`judge_submission_time`,
     `iex_quote_max_age_s`), so a grant answered later than that bound is refused there.
7. **No runtime tick at or past the deadline.** A wake at or after the ask's deadline hands the
   runtime nothing; the fold denies `approval_timed_out` with no read. The runtime's own timeout
   record, `ApprovalTimedOut`, is catalogued but has no registered payload schema, so its append
   is refused as `Invalid`. The approval stays pending on the journal for the runtime's own fold
   (DEC-912 item 1).

### Rationale

The wait has to hold the submit while the runtime does the admitting, and the runtime hands its
intent and the executor submits inside one step. So the hold sits at the one place every
submission already passes: the shell's broker door, where the write-ahead check and the
one-order door are. Holding there needs no change to the runtime or the executor, and it keeps
the release where DEC-920 put it: on the runtime's own records, read back by the runner's fold.
Reading the answers in the wake rather than in a second tail keeps one path into the runtime.

Each gap in item 6 is outside this lane's crates (runtime, executor, journal), and each refuses
rather than sends, so the wiring lands as a stop that is correct today. Fixing any of them is a
safety-critical change of its own.

### Alternatives considered

- **Check the grant inside the shell before handing the answer to the runtime.** Rejected: it
  is a second admission, which DEC-912 and DEC-920 reject.
- **Have the runner hold the intent rather than the submission.** Rejected: the intent is the
  runtime's handoff inside one step, so the runner could only see it after the executor had
  already asked to submit.
- **Wait in a dry run too.** Rejected: a dry run appends to an in-memory journal no answer can
  reach, so it could only time out after up to `timeout_s`.
- **Tick the runtime at the deadline so it journals its own timeout.** Rejected for now: the
  journal refuses `ApprovalTimedOut`'s payload, which would turn a clean deny into a journal
  refusal.

<a id="dec-943"></a>

## DEC-943: The run journals its own IEX quote reads as marks, and the binding gate judges a fresh quote at the grant

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each one under DEC-176 — no bound moves, and every reading refuses where the code before it refused or sent nothing) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-921 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; DEC-940 item 6, gaps 1 and 3; claim [#1375](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1375); DEC-156 item 3, DEC-920, DEC-935, DEC-936 |

DEC-940 item 6 named three gaps between an `ask` opening and its grant, each failing closed.
This decision closes gaps 1 and 3. Gap 2, the granted intent's execution policy, is claim #1374's.

1. **No mark reached the runtime.** No production code journaled `MarkUpdated`. The request
   recorded `reference_mark: null`, and re-validation skipped every grant as `drift`.
2. **The binding gate judged the preflight's quote.** `TrustedPaperContext` rechecked the
   preflight quote's age at the gate's clock. A grant answered more than `iex_quote_max_age_s`
   (10 s) after the preflight was refused there.

### Decision

1. **The run journals its own quote reads as marks.**
   - The shell writes `MarkUpdated` on the account stream at the registered version 1:
     `instrument_id` (the pinned asset id), `price`, `source` `quote`, `feed` `iex`, and
     `risk_clock`.
   - The price is the quote's bid, a long's risk mark (trading-domain spec §8.2).
   - The envelope's `event_time` is the quote's own stamp. The `risk_clock` is the session's
     second (mandate spec §5.2: `event_time` is never used for risk timing).
   - The record is written as the executor's, the account stream's one writer. Its id is
     derived from the stream's head, as the executor derives its own.
   - Two writes, both before the runtime reads the mark:
     - **At the cycle's open**, from the preflight's IEX quote. It is written after the
       control stream's tail and before the observation and the output, so a request records
       it as its `reference_mark`.
     - **At each wake of an asked opening's wait**, from that wake's fresh quote (item 2). It
       is written before the control stream's answers reach the runtime, so the re-validation
       that wake may run compares the request's reference with that mark.
   - Only `run_observed_asked_watched` writes marks. That is the one door `mandate-paper`'s
     `run()` uses, for an `auto` cycle too. The older doors (`run`, `run_observed`,
     `run_observed_watched`) write none.
2. **Each wake reads a fresh IEX quote, and the binding gate judges it.**
   - Before each wake that hands the runtime anything, the runner (`granted_opening`) reads
     `Session::fresh_quote`. That is `mandate_shell::paper::fresh_quote`: the preflight's own
     `DataClient::latest_quote`, with the same `iex_quote_max_age_s` bound and DEC-936's 250 ms
     skew tolerance at the read. Then come the shell judge's checks: the reviewed symbol, the
     IEX feed, and a bid not above the ask.
   - The read is handed to the binding gate's `GateQuote`. From then on every binding request
     judges that quote, as the market quote and by its age at the gate's own clock. The gate's
     recheck is unchanged (DEC-935 item 2, DEC-936 item 4): ten seconds, and a stamp after the
     gate's clock refuses.
   - The read's bid is journaled as that wake's mark (item 1).
   - A read that fails, is stale, or is out of tolerance stops the wait before the runtime is
     handed anything (`market_data_untrusted`, "a current, uncrossed IEX quote at the wake"),
     and nothing is sent. The bound is never relaxed for a grant.
   - A wake at or past the ask's deadline reads no quote; DEC-940 item 7's denial is unchanged.
   - A Robinhood session refuses the fresh read until its session is built (O2), as its
     preflight does.
   - Exits and protective orders are never held (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). The fresh read is the
     asked opening's wait alone. A `GateQuote` only ever receives a quote the run just read
     and judged current, so no exit is judged on an older quote than before.
3. **The runtime's fold reads the registered member.** The journal's registered `MarkUpdated`
   version 1 names its instrument `instrument_id`. The runtime's fold read `instrument`, which
   no record the journal accepts carries, so a real mark would have refused the fold. The fold
   now reads `instrument_id` and refuses a record without it (`non_canonical`). The runtime's
   fixtures, its golden journal and the reference-case harness move with it. No production
   journal holds a `MarkUpdated`, because none was ever written.
4. **`Session::cycle` borrows the session.** The wait reads its fresh quote through the same
   session after the cycle is built, so `cycle` takes `&self`. The Alpaca session clones its
   transport, as it already did for the preflight.

### Rationale

The runtime decides drift from its own folded marks (mandate spec §6.4 check 12), so the only
way to hand it the run's quotes is the record the spec names: `MarkUpdated`, journaled before
the step that reads it (`AGENTS.md` rule 5's order). A fresh read per wake keeps the binding
gate's existing ten-second bound meaningful for a grant that arrives minutes later, without
widening it. The wake that releases the opening is the one whose quote was read immediately
before the gate judges it.

Refusing on any failed fresh read, not only the releasing wake's, is the conservative reading:
the wait cannot know in advance which wake the runtime will act on. A refusal sends nothing,
and the approval stays pending for the runtime's own fold.

Every reading here refuses or holds where the code before it refused. Today the
`role:approver` reference is not yet resolved to a user (see Not covered), and gap 2 is still
open, so a grant still cannot open a position in a production build. The end-to-end tests
reach the order through the two test doubles that name those gaps.

### Alternatives considered

- **Widen the binding gate's quote bound to the ask's timeout.** Rejected: it loosens a bound
  (DEC-79 reserves that), and a ten-minute-old quote says nothing about the price the order
  meets.
- **Read the fresh quote only at the wake that releases.** Rejected: the release is the
  runtime's, inside the wake. The runner learns it only after the binding gate has judged, so
  the quote must be handed before every wake.
- **Have the executor write `MarkUpdated` from `Input::Market`.** Rejected for this lane: it
  changes the executor's step and its journaled batches, a larger safety-critical change. The
  shell already writes the account stream's `StreamOpened` as the executor's.
- **Accept both `instrument` and `instrument_id` in the runtime's fold.** Rejected: two
  spellings of one member is the drift the registered schema exists to prevent. The journal
  accepts only `instrument_id`.

### Not covered

- **The approver set is unresolved.** `runtime_view` hands the runtime the mandate's approver
  references as written (`role:approver`), and admission check 3 matches the responder's user
  id against that set. So no grant is admitted in a production build. This fails closed, and
  belongs to the approvals lane. The end-to-end tests resolve the role to the founder in a
  mandate-stage double, as the shell's grant probes already do.
- **Gap 2** (the granted intent's execution policy) is claim #1374's. The tests here keep a
  policy-supplying sink double.
- **The advisory gate's dry run** (re-validation check 11) still reads the preflight's
  snapshot at the preflight's clock. It is advisory; the binding gate decides.

<a id="dec-947"></a>

## DEC-947: The live run reads the agentic account's snapshot after every credential-free check, admits itself from its own facts, and logs in over a bearer-carrying MCP session

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, under DEC-929): item 2 amends FT-3's order for a live run, a founder-reserved safety ordering, by the reading that adds the least risk; items 1 and 3 to 5 are reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176, each refusing at least as much as before |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1378](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1378)), under DEC-929 (item 2) and DEC-79 and DEC-176 (items 1, 3, 4 and 5) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1378; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R2, R3 and R4; the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md)'s FT-3 and G1a; DEC-918, DEC-919, DEC-921 (one PR, tests first), DEC-922, DEC-924, DEC-929, DEC-933, DEC-934, DEC-937 |

### Context

Three refusals stood between the rehearsal's runner and the simulator once a live deployment
exists (R0 rehearsal record, R2 to R4):

- **R2.** `run()` handed the live door no snapshot, so every live run refused
  `lapse_evidence_not_supplied` (DEC-919). DEC-933 item 3 built the lapse's real inputs but left
  the snapshot to "the lane that connects the Robinhood connector". The snapshot is read through
  the Robinhood session, which needs the founder's login, so it cannot come before a credential
  read. FT-3 orders every check that needs no credential first, and DEC-918 item 3 put the lapse
  in front of the shell's fold.
- **R3.** `run()` handed the shell `LiveAdmission::refusing()` (DEC-934 item 4). The rehearsal
  build has `live`, so the runner pinned Robinhood's host while it dialed the simulator on
  loopback, and DEC-934 item 3's byte-for-byte equality would refuse the rehearsal for ever.
- **R4.** A logged-in Robinhood session read and sent nothing (`SESSION_UNBUILT`, DEC-937 item
  2): no session joined the login (whose token DEC-946's `with_bearer` can now carry) to
  the shell's Robinhood bridge.

### Decision

1. **The snapshot is the session's.** `Session` gains `account_snapshot`, which the Alpaca paper
   session answers with none and the Robinhood session with `RobinhoodBridge::account_snapshot`:
   `get_accounts`, `get_portfolio`, `get_equity_positions` and `get_equity_orders`, scoped to the
   recorded account, and no quote and no order. A session that reads none still refuses a live
   connection `lapse_evidence_not_supplied` (DEC-919 unchanged), and an unreadable snapshot
   refuses at `Stage::Reconcile`.
2. **The order of a live run (FT-3 amended for a live run only, under DEC-929).** In `run()`:
   1. the door's `chosen` and `account_stream`, then `acknowledge` (`--confirm-live`) and
      `harden` (core dumps off), unchanged;
   2. the dial ([`dial`](../../../crates/mandate-paper/src/robinhood.rs)): the build's endpoint
      and the account number, read from the process environment, nothing sent;
   3. the shell's new `checked_before_credentials`: the same walk, document read and fold as
      `confirmed_version`, every V-rule but V-002 included, with a live deployment admitted on
      the three of DEC-924 item 1's conditions the run holds before any credential (the `live`
      build, `--confirm-live`, and the dialed host equal to the pin). It answers no version;
   4. the one `Ports::connect`: the login, then the MCP session (item 4);
   5. the snapshot (item 1), then `lapse_read` on it and on the account stream's rows;
   6. `confirmed_version` with the door's condition now held, then everything after it as before.

   So no credential is read before every check that needs none has passed, apart from the
   configuration and artifact checks the fold's answer feeds; those still run before anything
   that can place. Between the connect and the cycle the session only reads; a refusal anywhere
   after the connect leaves it unused and sends nothing. A paper run keeps today's order exactly
   (LT-14): its lapse makes no check and its connect stays after the configuration.
3. **The admission is the run's own facts, and the rehearsal pins loopback.**
   `live_admission(built, acknowledged, door, dialed, pinned)` maps each fact to its field and
   decides nothing; the shell's `admits` judges. `built` is `Live` exactly when the runner was
   compiled with `live`. `pinned` is `pinned_host(Build::CURRENT)`: none in the default build,
   Robinhood's `MCP_HOST` in the `live` build, and `REHEARSAL_HOST`, `127.0.0.1`, the
   simulator's own address, in the `rh-sim` build. `MCP_HOST` is now compiled only under `live`
   without `rh-sim`, so the rehearsal binary holds no production host at all (the binary-bytes
   test checks it). DEC-934 item 3's exact equality is unchanged: the rehearsal admits only a dial
   of `127.0.0.1`, and the `live` build only Robinhood's host. This is DEC-922 items 1 and 2
   read into DEC-924 item 1's fourth condition: in the one build that cannot reach a broker, the
   pinned host is the simulator's.
4. **The MCP session over the login's token (O2).** DEC-946's `McpTransport::with_bearer` carries
   the token as `authorization: Bearer` on every request, marked sensitive and never printed
   (LT-9). The runner's Robinhood session is the login, then that transport pinned to the dialed
   endpoint, then
   `McpClient::connect` with no stored contract pin, so a server that lists a fund-movement tool
   or lacks an allowlisted one gives no client at all (connections spec §8.1's first-connection
   check), then one `RobinhoodConnector` scoped to the account number, inside the shell's
   `RobinhoodBridge` on the login's runtime (DEC-928 item 2). Its preflight is DEC-933 item 2's
   (`preflight_facts`, Alpaca's market reads, `robinhood_facts`); its cycle is the shell's new
   `robinhood_production_cycle`, whose connector is `RobinhoodShellConnector` and whose
   reconciler is `RobinhoodReconciler` around the run's executor reconciler, over the same
   bridge. Its broker ask is #1380's `BrokerAsk::robinhood` over the same bridge (DEC-945 item
   4), so the cross-check reads Robinhood's own quote at the check.
5. **Where the account number and the market keys come from.** The connector needs the agentic
   account's number (CN-8), which the founder typed and which no record carries in clear
   (journal spec §6.4). The run reads it from `MANDATE_RH_ACCOUNT_NUMBER` in the process
   environment, never prints it, and refuses `robinhood_account_unset` before anything is sent
   when it is missing or empty; the connector then refuses unless `get_accounts` lists exactly
   that number as agentic (DEC-875). The Alpaca paper keys for the market reads are read at the
   connect, from the environment, with no request; without them the session is built and its
   preflight refuses (`MARKET_UNREAD`), so a run without keys still reaches the lapse and the
   fold and stops at R5.

### Not decided here

The cycle this session builds still refuses before an order: the executor sends the mandate's
asset id as the order's symbol, which Robinhood does not accept, and `RobinhoodReconciler` reads
its snapshot past `BrokerSymbols` (DEC-938), so positions come back by ticker. Both fail closed.
Naming Robinhood's instruments by ticker on the way out and by asset id on the way back is
DEC-938 item 1's reading for a second broker, recorded as the R0 rehearsal's R8.

### For the founder

Item 2 reads your Robinhood credential (the login) before the lapse, which FT-3 did not allow.
It is the least-risk order available: the login happens only after every check the fold can make
without it, and between the login and the cycle the session only reads your account. Revert it by
a later decision if you want the login strictly after the lapse; that needs the lapse's snapshot
from somewhere other than the logged-in session, which no read offers today.

### Rationale

The snapshot exists only behind the login, so the lapse cannot come before a credential read;
moving the fold's credential-free checks in front of the login keeps FT-3's purpose, no
credential for a run that would refuse anyway, for every check that can be made. Splitting the
fold by a predicate on the same walk keeps one derivation of the deployment, so the check before
credentials can never pass where the full fold would refuse for any reason but the door. Pinning
loopback only in the build that refuses every non-loopback endpoint keeps DEC-934's exact
equality and LT-1, and removing `MCP_HOST` from that build means the rehearsal cannot hold a
production pin even by mistake. The account number from the environment fails closed and is
checked by the broker's own listing; the founder already sets the environment for the
rehearsal's endpoint.

### Alternatives considered

- **The login strictly after the shell's whole fold, the lapse after it:** the fold needs the
  door's condition, which needs the lapse, which needs the login, so the fold would have to admit
  on a door it has not checked. Rejected: that hands `DoorChecks::Passed` before the door passed
  (DEC-934 item 6).
- **Weaken the host condition to accept any loopback host in the `live` build:** rejected by
  DEC-922's alternatives and LT-1.
- **A copy of the simulator's host in the shell:** DEC-934 item 3 keeps the constant in the
  runner; the shell receives it.
- **The stored contract hash as the MCP client's pin:** no record the runner reads carries it
  yet (R1's connect check writes it). With no pin the client applies the first connection's
  check, which refuses outright where a pin would only halt openings. When R1 lands, the pin
  should come from its `ConnectionChecked`.
- **Pick the one agentic account `get_accounts` lists, with no typed number:** rejected by CN-8:
  the connector holds the number the founder typed and never adopts another account.
- **Read the Alpaca market keys lazily in the preflight:** same effect with an extra environment
  read inside the cycle's reads; reading them once at the connect is simpler to review.

<a id="dec-948"></a>

## DEC-948: The paper run stops on any reconciliation mismatch, books its simulated fee, and journals at the run clock

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Claude (builder agent), under DEC-79 (reversible engineering decisions) and DEC-176 (each item tightens a check or makes the code meet the spec as written, and weakens no safety invariant) |
| **Story or claim** | E2, the first paper trade; the follow-ups #1369's review and DEC-938's backlog recorded; DEC-921 (one PR, tests first) |

### Decision

1. **A mismatch stops the run wherever the reconciliation ran.** The watch used to drop the
   verdicts of the reconciliations it asked for, so a mismatch there raised no alert and the run
   went on. Now the startup and the watch both use one rule: the first round that answers
   `Mismatch` pushes the opaque alert key `reconciliation_mismatch` (rule 6) and stops the run
   with `ShellError::ReconciliationMismatch`. Nothing is sent after that, and no round it asked
   for is run. The startup used to run every round it was asked for before it read any verdict.
2. **The executor learns its environment from the stream's envelope.** Journal spec §9's
   registered `StreamOpened` carries the environment in the envelope, not in the payload. The
   executor's fold reads only the payload member, so on the paper path it never learned that it
   was on paper and booked no simulated fee (trading spec §10). When the shell folds an
   account-stream `StreamOpened` whose payload has no `environment`, it hands the executor the
   environment the journal stored with the record. The executor then books the fee the
   registered fee configuration defines on each of its own paper equity fills.
3. **`FeesCharged` version 1 is registered as the simulated equity fee.** It is a closed record
   of §9's key fields (`family`, `day`, `accrued`, `charged`), plus `simulated`,
   `client_order_id`, the three equity components `sec`, `taf` and `cat` (each always present, and
   `null` where the side owes none), and `risk_clock`. `family` is only `equities`, the one family
   anything books today. A broker-posted or crypto fee registers its own shape when its writer is
   built. The record names `fee_config`, the one reference the catalogue requires. It is the same
   object its fill names, taken from `InstrumentSnapshot::fill_refs`. A snapshot that names none
   leaves the fee unbound, and the journal refuses it, as it refuses an unbound fill.
4. **The executor's risk clock starts at the run clock.** Mandate spec §5.2 defines the risk
   clock as the latest tick the executor has seen. On the paper path no tick reached it before
   its first records, so they all carried `1970-01-01T00:00:00Z`. The shell now hands the
   executor a tick at the run clock's whole second before `Input::Started`. A tick before the
   start only sets the clock: it evaluates nothing, emits nothing, and leaves the executor
   unstarted. Any other input before the start is still refused `not_started`.
5. **The cash comparison measures from the snapshot before this one, and counts each fill
   once.** Trading spec §11 compares the broker's cash with the last broker cash snapshot plus the
   fills since then. The shell records the snapshot's own account report before the run (DEC-458
   item 2), so that report was the base. That had two effects. First, the broker's cash was
   compared with itself. Second, a fill the run then ingested, which that cash already held, was
   counted again. So every late fill mismatched by its notional, and no real drift could ever
   show. Item 1 made that false mismatch stop the paper run after its first fill. Now, when the
   base is a report folded after the snapshot's head (the snapshot's own report), the model is the
   report it replaced, plus every fill folded since that one: before the report, after it, and the
   ones the run ingests. The executor keeps the replaced report and the fills folded up to it
   (`PriorBase`). With no earlier report there is nothing to measure from, and the snapshot is its
   own base, as at a first start. A base set by an earlier report is measured from as before. This
   tightens the check: a drift between two snapshots now mismatches on the shell path, where
   before it could not.

### Rationale

Items 1, 2 and 4 make the code do what the specs already say: a mismatch alerts and holds
(trading spec §11, `AGENTS.md` rule 3), paper books its shadow fees (§10), and records carry the
risk clock (mandate spec §5.2). Item 3 registers the exact record the executor already wrote, in
a closed shape. That is narrower than the catalogue's open row, so it loosens no validation. Item
5 brings the cash comparison back to §11's wording, so that each snapshot is measured from the one
before it, and it detects more than the code did. Without item 5, item 1 would stop every paper
run after its own fill on a false mismatch. Without item 1, the watch would go on hiding a real
one. The independent review of the first draft of item 5 found it measured the snapshot from
itself. That would have removed the false mismatch only by leaving nothing compared, so the draft
was replaced before merge.

### Alternatives considered

- **Record the snapshot's account report after the reconciliation run.** That would compare cash
  against the earlier base too. But it reverses DEC-458 item 2, and it breaks the fail-closed rule
  that an ambiguous account report stops the run before any reconciliation runs. It was rejected
  in favour of keeping the replaced base in the fold.
- **Leave the ingested fills out of the model when its own report is the base.** This was item 5's
  first draft. It removes the false mismatch, but it compares the broker's cash with itself, so
  the check sees nothing. It was rejected at review.
- **Add `environment` to `FoldedEvent`.** About seventy constructions across eleven crates would
  change for one record type. The shell hands the executor what the journal stored instead.
- **Emit only the components a fill owes, and register optional members.** The journal's records
  have exactly their listed members, each always present (journal spec §4.2), so a missing
  component is `null`.
- **Tick the executor right after `Input::Started`.** The records the start itself writes (an
  abandoned intent, the startup reconciliation's) would still carry zero.

### Not decided here (backlog)

- A fill's `set` reference still names the trading calendar until a distinct settlement calendar
  is registered (DEC-938 item 2).
- A truly external fill is still refused at the journal: `ExternalActivityIngested` has no
  schema.
- A labelled fail-safe deny needs a spec change (DEC-938 item 4).
- A fill that lands between the snapshot's account read and its activity read is in the model but
  not yet in the broker's cash, so it stops the run on a false cash mismatch (fail-safe).
- The first account report an account journal ever holds has no earlier report to be measured
  from. When it is also the snapshot's own report, that one comparison checks the broker's cash
  against itself, and the fills that run ingests go uncompared. This happens once per account
  journal.
- `PriorBase` reaches back one report only. An account push between two snapshots becomes the base
  the next snapshot is measured from, so drift between the earlier snapshot and the push is never
  compared with the model. Pushes re-anchored the base before this decision too.

<a id="dec-949"></a>

## DEC-949: The grant wait ticks the runtime at the ask's deadline, so the runtime closes it

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each one under DEC-176 — every reading denies or sends nothing where the code before it denied and sent nothing too) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder, the grant-deadline-wake lane), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-921 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; DEC-940 item 7; DEC-944; claim [#1399](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1399) |

DEC-940 item 7 left the runner's grant wait handing the runtime nothing at or past an ask's
deadline, because the journal then refused the runtime's `ApprovalTimedOut`. DEC-944 (journal spec
v0.41, #1379 and #1383) registered that record with rule 135, so the reason is gone. Before this
decision the fold still denied `approval_timed_out` at the deadline and nothing was sent, but the
ask stayed pending on the journal: no `ApprovalTimedOut` was ever written by a live or paper run.
These are the readings this lane takes.

### Decision

1. **A wake at or past the deadline ticks the runtime alone.** The shell's `AskedCycle` gains a
   fourth call, `expire(now)`: it moves the session's event time to `now`, hands the runtime one
   `Input::Tick` at `now`'s risk-clock second, and reads both streams back, as `wake` does. The
   runtime's own `expire` then journals `ApprovalTimedOut {approval, on_timeout: skip}`, caused by
   the ask (rule 135). `granted_opening` calls it on every read at or past the deadline, and once
   when the ask it binds is already at or past its deadline, then denies `approval_timed_out`.
   Nothing is sent (rule 3): the runner's fold denies at or past the deadline whatever the read
   holds, and the opening stays held at the shell's door.
2. **The last pause ends at the deadline.** The wait pauses the lesser of the poll interval and
   the time left to the deadline, so the tick lands at the deadline itself, the first tick at or
   after it that mandate spec §6.4 names, and never up to an interval past it.
3. **The deadline's wake reads no quote and hands no answer.** `wake` needs a fresh IEX quote for
   its mark (DEC-943 item 2) and hands the control stream to the runtime first. Neither is needed
   to deny, and both could only hurt: a failed quote read at the deadline would stop the run
   before the runtime closed the ask, and an answer handed before the tick would be judged at the
   previous wake's risk clock, still before the deadline, so the runtime could admit and
   re-validate a grant after its deadline had passed. So `expire` ticks only. An answer committed
   before the deadline and not yet read stays on the control stream; the runtime reads it later as
   `not_pending`.
4. **A refused tick stops the run.** If the shell refuses the tick (a journal refusal, for one),
   the run stops with that refusal instead of the clean `approval_timed_out`. Either way nothing
   is sent; the refusal is the more informative stop.
5. **Rule 13 is unchanged.** The tick passes through the same door as every wake: only a purpose
   that adds risk is held, so an exit, a protective order or a risk exit the tick raises is never
   held by the wait.

### Rationale

The timeout is a deny, and the runner already denied; what was missing was the runtime's own
record of it, which the journal now admits. Ticking the runtime at the deadline is the one input
the runtime needs to write it, and it is the input it already takes at every wake. Keeping the
deadline's wake to a tick alone means the only thing it can do is close the ask.

### Alternatives considered

- **Call `wake` at the deadline with a fresh quote.** Rejected for item 3's reasons: a quote is not
  needed to deny, and a failed read would leave the ask pending again.
- **Hand the control stream before the tick, as `wake` does.** Rejected: it would let the runtime
  judge an answer at a risk clock before the deadline after the deadline had passed.
- **Keep pausing a whole interval.** Rejected: the tick could land up to an interval past the
  deadline, and the runtime's record would carry that later time.
- **Tick the runtime only when the deadline passes during the wait, not for an ask bound already
  past it.** Rejected: that ask is just as pending, and one tick closes it.

<a id="dec-952"></a>

## DEC-952: Paper and live never share a stream: a live workspace of its own (R0 blocker R1b)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, under DEC-929): the coordinator's call on how a live workspace is opened, taking the reading that keeps the journal's one-environment-per-stream rule and refuses more; the builder's readings of it under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The coordinator (Claude Code), under DEC-929; items 3 to 6 by the R1b builder (Claude Code) under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-11; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s blocker R1b; [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md) steps 2 and 3; claim #1387 |

`mandate workspace open` opened `ctl:{workspace}` in `paper` only, and the journal fixes a
stream's environment at its first event (`environment_mismatch`), so the live
`ConnectionEstablished`, and every live version, confirmation, deployment and grant after it,
could never be appended to the workspace's control stream. The rehearsal stopped there (step 2f).

### Decision

1. **Paper and live never share a stream** (the coordinator, under DEC-929). A live deployment
   gets its own workspace, whose control stream is opened in `live` at its first event; a paper
   workspace stays exactly as it was. The journal's rule that a stream has one environment is
   kept, not narrowed.
2. **Opening.** `mandate workspace open --workspace … --environment live` opens the control
   stream in `live`; `--environment` defaults to `paper`, so every existing paper command line is
   unchanged, and any other value is refused `environment_invalid` before the journal is opened.
   An id whose control stream holds any event is refused `workspace_already_open` in either
   environment, so a live workspace can never be opened over a paper one or the other way round.
3. **Every owner command states its environment.** The owner flags every control command takes
   (`--workspace`, `--user`) gain `--environment`, `paper` by default, as the gestures already had
   (DEC-925): the registrations (`config register`, `model register`), `approvals list`, `show`,
   `approve` and `skip`, and the gestures (`version create`, `version confirm`, `agent deploy`).
   `connection record` keeps its own required `--environment`. Explicit rather than read from the
   stream: each environment needs its own acknowledgement, as the runner's `--confirm-live`
   (DEC-937 item 3), so a paper command line can never act in a live workspace.
4. **A command in the other environment is refused before it writes.** An owner's event is
   appended only to a control stream whose first event is in the owner's environment; otherwise
   it is refused `workspace_environment`, with nothing stored, minted or appended, rather than
   left to the journal's raw `environment_mismatch`. The check sits where each command first
   could write: in `commit` (answers and agent commands) and `commit_caused` (`connection
   record`) before any step-up evidence is minted; in the registrations before they store their
   object; and in `version::admitted`, which every version gesture (create, confirm, deploy and
   their show-code steps) passes before it stores or mints anything, after the paper and live
   document checks, so `mandate_not_paper` and `paper_only` keep their precedence. A show-code
   step that finds its version passes the same check, so it is refused too. A stream with no
   event is the journal's to refuse (`not_stream_opened`), as before.
5. **Registrations in a live workspace.** `config register` and `model register` were paper only
   (DEC-526, DEC-527). A live workspace needs its own registered configuration, since the runner
   reads the configuration from the workspace's control stream, so a `live` owner may register in
   a live workspace; by item 4, only there. A registration places nothing: the version that
   names it is still created, confirmed and deployed through DEC-529 item 3's live admission. An
   owner neither in `paper` nor in `live` is still refused `paper_only`.
6. **Tests that modelled a mixed stream.** The CLI's test journal does not enforce the
   journal's environment rule, and some fixtures seeded a live connection or live gestures on a
   control stream whose first rows were paper, or pinned `--environment` as an unknown argument
   of the registration, approval and `workspace open` commands. Those fixtures now seed one
   environment per control stream, and the parse tests accept `--environment`, as this decision
   requires; what each test asserts about its own command is unchanged.
7. **The founder's steps and the rehearsal.** The first live trade brief's steps 2 and 3, and
   `scripts/r0-rehearsal.sh`, open the live workspace with `--environment live` and pass it to
   every owner command. With it, the rehearsal passes every configuration and gesture step (2b to
   2k) and stops at the first run (2l), on R2's lapse evidence.

### Rationale

Keeping one environment per stream keeps every reader simple and every replay unambiguous: a
control stream's environment is its first event's, and nothing on it can say otherwise. A live
workspace of its own also separates the founder's live records from every paper experiment in
the journal, which is what DEC-529's narrowings assume. Refusing a mismatched command before it
writes turns the journal's generic refusal into one the owner can act on, and puts it ahead of
any stored object or minted step-up.

### Alternatives considered

- **Let the control stream carry both environments** (each event's own environment in its
  payload): rejected by the coordinator; it changes the journal's stream rule, every reader of
  the control stream, and the replay, to save the founder one `workspace open`.
- **Read the environment from the stream instead of `--environment`**: rejected; a paper
  command line would then act in a live workspace without saying so (item 3).
- **Check only at the journal**: rejected; the refusal would come after the object was stored
  and the step-up minted, and would name no code the CLI documents.

<a id="dec-954"></a>

## DEC-954: The live run's core-dump hardening builds on every target, verifies what the kernel took, and refuses where it cannot

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder), under DEC-79 (reversible engineering) and DEC-176 (each reading only tightens) |
| **Story or claim** | LT-9 of the [first live trade brief](../tasks/first-live-trade.md); claim #1392 |

### Decision

1. **Builds everywhere.** `disable_core_dumps` (`crates/mandate-paper/src/harden.rs`, DEC-937
   item 4) called `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE)`, which exists only on Linux, so `mandate-paper` did
   not compile on macOS, where the founder runs the paper and live CLIs. Each call is now behind
   the `cfg` of the targets that have it. The crate builds on every target, and no `unsafe` is
   added: every call still goes through `rustix`'s safe wrappers.
2. **The core-file limit on every unix target, read back.** Wherever `rustix` gives `setrlimit`
   and `getrlimit` (every `unix` target except ESP-IDF, Fuchsia, Horizon, Redox and Vita, so
   Linux and macOS both), the run sets `RLIMIT_CORE` to 0, soft and hard, then reads it back with
   `getrlimit`. Anything but zero for both limits refuses. The read-back catches a kernel or
   sandbox that returns success but does not apply the limit, which a return code alone cannot.
3. **The dumpable flag on Linux, read back.** On Linux (and Android, which shares the kernel)
   the run also sets `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` to 0, then reads it back with `PR_GET_DUMPABLE`. Only
   `NotDumpable` passes. macOS has no such flag, and nothing portable replaces it: its
   `ptrace(PT_DENY_ATTACH)` is a different guarantee that `rustix` does not wrap, and it would
   need `unsafe`. On macOS, the zero core-file limit is the whole hardening.
4. **Fails closed.** If any call fails, any read-back differs, or the target has no core-file
   limit (Windows, for example), `disable_core_dumps` returns `PaperError::Harden`. A live run
   then refuses before any credential read (LT-9, DEC-937 item 4). A paper run never calls it,
   so paper behaviour is unchanged.
5. **CI runs the runner on macOS.** A new part, `cargo xtask ci portable`, runs clippy
   `-D warnings` over all of `mandate-paper`'s targets, then its hardening tests (the read-back
   verdicts and the two kernel read-back tests in `tests/live_entry.rs`). CI runs it in a new job,
   `fast-portable`, on `macos-14`, and the required `fast` verdict needs it, so a Linux-only call
   in the runner fails a required check. Locally on Linux, `cargo xtask check` runs the same part
   on the host. A cross-check from Linux (`cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin`) was tried
   and cannot work: `ring`'s build script compiles C that needs the macOS SDK
   (`TargetConditionals.h`), which no Linux runner has. The job runs the default build only,
   never `live` (DEC-529 item 3).

### Rationale

The read-back makes "core dumps are off" a fact the kernel confirms rather than something
assumed from a successful return code. Refusing on a target with no limit keeps LT-9's promise
without making the crate fail to compile there. Each reading only tightens DEC-937: the Linux
behaviour is the same two calls, plus two reads that can only add refusals, so DEC-176 lets an
agent take it.

Running the runner's lint and hardening tests on macOS, rather than only type-checking it, proves
that the hardening succeeds where the founder runs it. A live run whose hardening always refused
on macOS would otherwise be found only at the first live run.

### Alternatives considered

- **`cfg`-gating the whole hardening to Linux and returning `Ok` elsewhere:** this weakens LT-9
  on macOS, since a live run would read credentials with core dumps never turned off.
- **The `libc` crate for `ptrace(PT_DENY_ATTACH)` on macOS:** this needs `unsafe`, which the
  workspace forbids, and stops a debugger rather than a core file.
- **Adding `aarch64-apple-darwin` to `rust-toolchain.toml` and cross-checking from Linux:**
  `ring` cannot build without the Apple SDK, and every job would download the target's standard
  library for nothing.
- **A raw `cargo check` step instead of an `xtask` part:** ES-12 says every CI job is one
  `cargo xtask ci <job>` command that `cargo xtask check` also runs locally.

<a id="dec-957"></a>

## DEC-957: A live run's artifacts bind only registrations from its own live workspace's stream (R0 blocker R9)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176, each refusing at least as much as before for every input `main` could already reach; under DEC-929 for item 3, which keeps a founder-reserved fee lock rather than opening it) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-16 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1398](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1398)), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-929 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1398; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R9; DEC-505, DEC-526, DEC-921 (one PR, tests first), DEC-924, DEC-934, DEC-947, DEC-952 |

### Context

`Artifacts::from_registered` (`crates/mandate-shell/src/paper/artifacts.rs`) refused every
mandate whose environment was not `paper` ("a paper mandate"), a lock from the paper adapter's
first slice. Since DEC-947 a live run reaches it after the live door, the login, the lapse and
`confirmed_version` with all four of DEC-924 item 1's conditions held, so the rehearsal stopped
at step 2l with nothing sent. Lifting the lock alone would let a live run bind whatever the fold
handed it, and nothing recorded which stream or environment a registration came from: the
control records the shell read carried a sequence number, a type and a payload, no more.

### Decision

1. **A live mandate only from a live admission.** `from_registered` no longer refuses a mandate
   for being `live`. A live mandate can reach it only inside a `ConfirmedVersion`, and
   `confirmed_version` is the one function that answers one: it admits a live deployment on
   `LiveAdmission::admits` alone (all four conditions; DEC-924 item 2, DEC-934 item 5).
   `checked_before_credentials` answers no version (DEC-947 item 2). A test pins that a live
   stream with the default admission, or with every condition but the door, yields no version.
2. **Every binding from the deployment's own stream, in the mandate's environment.** Each
   `ControlRecord` carries its `Origin`: the stream it was journaled on and that stream's
   environment. `Registered` keeps its record's origin, and `ConfirmedVersion::deployed_on` is
   the origin of the `AgentDeployed` it was folded from. `from_registered` refuses `Absent`
   ("every binding journaled on the deployment's own control stream") unless the deployment's
   stream is in the mandate's environment and the model, fee schedule, calendar, rule set and
   instrument snapshot were each registered on exactly that stream (same stream id, same
   environment). So a paper object in a live run, a live object in a paper run, and any object
   from another workspace refuse before the artifacts exist, which is before any cycle and any
   send. A paper run whose records all come from its own paper stream, which is every paper run
   the journal can produce (DEC-952 item 1), is unchanged.
3. **The fee schedule names the mandate's environment.** After the fee schedule is read as before
   (`paper_only_fee_config`, unchanged, still refuses any schedule naming anything but `paper`),
   its `environment` member must name the mandate's environment, or the run refuses
   `Absent` ("a fee schedule for the mandate's environment"). A paper run's refusals keep their
   order and codes. A live run therefore cannot be priced from the paper-only overestimates by
   registering a schedule that names `paper` on the live stream; and a schedule that names `live`
   is refused by `paper_only_fee_config` itself, as DEC-526 item 4 already said. A live fee
   schedule is the next blocker, R10, and its figures are the founder's (`PAPER_ONLY` is
   "Proposed (founder) as values"); this decision does not open it.
4. **Where the origin is read.** The runner's control read (`control_record`) and the grant
   fold's (`record_of`, and `journaled`'s unread rows) take the origin from the stored row's
   `stream_id` and envelope `environment` through `Origin::of`, which reads exactly `paper` or
   `live`; any other text refuses the read (`PaperError::Control`, or `GrantRefusal::Mismatched`
   for the grant fold), never a guess. The journal fixes a stream's environment at its first
   event and the runner reads only `ctl:{workspace}`, so on `main` every record of one read has
   one origin; the check holds the artifacts to that rather than assuming it.

### Rationale

DEC-952 gave a live deployment a workspace of its own so paper and live never share a stream.
Binding the artifacts to the deployment's own stream turns that into a property the artifacts
check, not one they inherit from the caller. Carrying the origin on each record keeps the check
per object, as the registrations are effective per kind. Ordering the fee check after the
existing reader keeps every paper refusal exactly as it was.

### Alternatives considered

- **Lift the paper-only lock and nothing else** (the R9 row's first proposal): rejected. It would
  let a live run bind a registration from a paper workspace or another workspace if a caller
  ever handed one, and it would let a `paper`-named fee schedule price a live account.
- **One origin for the whole read, passed beside the records:** fewer edits, but `configuration`'s
  and `confirmed_version`'s signatures would change (the runner's source-shape test pins the
  latter), and the check could not say which object came from where.
- **Check the workspace in `Artifacts::deployment` against `ctl:{workspace}`:** it adds nothing
  the runner's read does not already fix, and every caller's fixtures would need matching ids.
- **Ship a live fee schedule now (R10):** rejected here. The figures are the founder's, and
  DEC-921 asks for one PR per item.

<a id="dec-961"></a>

## DEC-961: The rehearsal build reads the live run's market data from a fixture directory, and only it can (R0 blocker R5)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176, recorded under DEC-929; the change lives only in the `rh-sim` build, which reaches no broker and no data host, and the `live` build is unchanged) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1414](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1414)), under DEC-79, DEC-176, DEC-921 and DEC-929 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1414; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R5; DEC-922, DEC-933 item 2, DEC-936, DEC-945, DEC-947 item 5, DEC-529 item 12 |

### Context

R5, observed at the rehearsal's 2l on `main` at `1b9fbb4`: a Robinhood run's preflight takes the
asset record, the latest IEX quote and the trailing window's IEX minute bars from Alpaca (DEC-933
item 2), and the asked opening's wake and cross-check read the IEX quote again (DEC-943, DEC-945).
`Linked::session` built the Alpaca client from the environment's paper keys, so an agent's run,
which reads no key (rule 8), stopped at `MARKET_UNREAD`. The `--bars` directory must also hold the
pinned ticker's trusted IEX daily bars, which only `mandate download` writes, from Alpaca, and
which cannot exist for the rehearsal's made-up `RHETF`.

### Decision

1. **The `rh-sim` build's market source is a fixture, and only that build has one.** A
   `SessionMarket` trait (mandate-paper) says where a Robinhood session's market reads go;
   `Market`, a type alias chosen by `cfg(feature = "rh-sim")`, is `SimMarket` in the rehearsal
   build and `AlpacaPaperHttp` in every other build, the `live` one included. `SimMarket` and its
   variable `MANDATE_RH_SIM_MARKET` are compiled only with `rh-sim` (and in the crate's own unit
   tests); `dial` reads the variable through `Market::FIXTURE_VAR`, which is `None` for Alpaca, so
   a build without `rh-sim` never holds the variable's name. The rehearsal build no longer reads
   Alpaca keys for a Robinhood session at all, which only tightens DEC-922 item 2 (it now reaches
   no Alpaca host either). `tests/market_fixture.rs` reads the runner binary it is built with:
   without `rh-sim` (default and `live`) the variable's bytes must be absent, with `rh-sim`
   present, each case the other's seeded bug; the `rh-sim` case failed on the tests-first commit.
   CI runs the absence case in the default build only (DEC-529 item 3 lets it only compile
   `live`), and the `live`-without-`rh-sim` runner is held by the same `cfg`, which that compile
   checks; the builder ran the absence case with `--features live` too.
2. **The fixture answers below `mandate-alpaca`'s clients, with Alpaca's own bodies.** `SimMarket`
   implements `TradingTransport` and `DataTransport` over `<dir>/market.json` (strict JSON, every
   member decimal or plain text: `symbol`, `asset_id`, `exchange`, `bid`, `ask`, `bid_size`,
   `ask_size`, `minute_volume`). It answers only `GET /v2/assets/{symbol}` (an active, tradable,
   whole-share US equity with no attribute), the latest IEX quote of its symbol, stamped with its
   clock as the request is answered, as a live feed stamps it, and the bars request of its symbol,
   one bar at the bid with the fixture's volume for every minute from the request's first start
   to its last. Anything else is `RefusedPath` with nothing answered. So `DataClient`'s age bound
   (the rule set's `iex_quote_max_age_s`), DEC-936's 250 ms skew, the symbol, the feed, the bars'
   grid, window and page checks, the shell's liquidity and gate checks, and DEC-945's cross-check
   of Robinhood's ask against the IEX ask within the collar all apply unchanged; the unit tests
   show the stale (10.001 s), skewed (251 ms) and wrong-symbol cases refused by the clients
   themselves, with the fixture's clock offset from the reader's. In the rehearsal both clocks are
   the system's, so a stale or skewed quote cannot occur there end to end; those refusals are
   shown by the unit tests only. A fixture that is missing or unreadable gives no market, and the
   preflight refuses `MARKET_UNREAD` as before.
3. **The stored daily bars come from a dev-only example.** `examples/rehearsal_bars.rs`
   (mandate-paper, built from its dev-dependencies, so no product binary holds it) writes the
   ticker's IEX daily-bars dataset with `mandate-marketdata`'s own `Store`: the last 30 regular
   sessions through the last one completed at the system clock, closes rising by 0.1 to the given
   close, 50,000,000 shares a session. The shell trusts it by the same `inspect` rules as a
   download (coverage through the last completed session, no gap, no split).
4. **The rehearsal's numbers.** `market.json` scripts the IEX quote at 84.99 / 85.01 beside the
   simulator's Robinhood ask of 85, inside DEC-529 item 12's collar (`collar_liquid_x` 0.01 for a
   median dollar volume above 50,000,000), 20,000 shares a minute, and the daily closes rise to 85.
   `scripts/r0-rehearsal.sh` builds the example (step 1c), writes both (2a0) and hands the runner
   `MANDATE_RH_SIM_MARKET` and `--bars`.

### Rationale

The rehearsal must run the same code the live order runs. Answering at the transport, with
Alpaca's wire format, keeps every client, shell, gate and cross-check rule in the path, and the
type alias keeps the fixture out of every build that can reach a broker. No key is read and no
host is dialed (rules 7 and 8).

### Alternatives considered

- **Serve the Alpaca endpoints from `mandate-rh-sim` over loopback.** Rejected: the Alpaca clients
  pin `https` to Alpaca's two hosts (ES-23), so the runner would need a loopback Alpaca transport
  anyway, and the simulator (a pure crate) would need HTTP routes for a second broker.
- **A fixture quote with a scripted fixed timestamp.** Rejected: a quote must be at most 10 s old
  at the preflight, the wake and the check, which a file written before the run cannot be.
- **A fixture mode in `mandate download`.** Rejected: the founder's CLI would then be able to
  write trusted market data from a file.
- **Keep reading Alpaca keys in the rehearsal build when they are set.** Rejected: the rehearsal
  would then read real market data with real keys, which the fixture makes unnecessary.

<a id="dec-962"></a>

## DEC-962: The rehearsal build sets its run clock from MANDATE_RH_SIM_CLOCK, and only it can (R0 R12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: the founder decided the override on 2026-10-17, "Sim clock override", in the coordinator session; the readings below are reversible engineering choices under DEC-79 and DEC-176, recorded under DEC-929; the change lives only in builds with `rh-sim`, which reach no broker and no data host, and the `live` build and the founder's CLI are unchanged) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1416](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1416)), under the founder's decision, DEC-79, DEC-176, DEC-921 and DEC-929 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1416; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R12; DEC-922, DEC-923, DEC-941, DEC-961 |

### Context

R12, observed at the rehearsal's 2l on Saturday 2026-10-17 (DEC-961's run): the shell's judge
refuses `a run clock inside the regular session` (`crates/mandate-shell/src/paper/judge.rs:95`),
correctly, because the run's clock is the system's and the market is shut. The founder decided to
finish the rehearsal this weekend by overriding the run clock in the rehearsal build only.

### Decision

1. **The runner's clock (`mandate-paper`).** `RunClock`, a type alias chosen by `cfg(feature =
   "rh-sim")` as DEC-961's `Market` is, is `SystemClock` in every build without `rh-sim`, the
   `live` one included, and `SimClock` in the rehearsal's. `SimClock` reads the instant
   `MANDATE_RH_SIM_CLOCK` names (RFC 3339, any zone) when the process starts and moves exactly as
   the system clock moves from then on, so the run's elapsed time, pauses and deadlines are real;
   unset, it is the system clock. It is every clock read the run makes: `Production`'s
   `Ports::Pause` (the judge, the preflight, the wake, the grant wait, the watch and every journal
   stamp), the connect check's `now`, and the fixture market's quote stamps (`SimMarket` is opened
   on it). A set value that is not an instant refuses the process before anything is read
   (`PaperError::SimClock`); the run never falls back to the system clock for a clock it was given.
   `SimClock`, the variable and `PaperError::SimClock` compile only with `rh-sim` (and in the
   crate's unit tests), so `tests/sim_clock.rs` shows the runner built without `rh-sim` (default
   and `live`) holds no byte of the variable's name and ignores it, and the `rh-sim` one holds and
   reads it, each case the other's seeded bug. CI runs the absence cases in the default build and, since DEC-963, the `rh-sim` cases in
   `cargo xtask ci test-rh-sim` (`mandate-paper` and, from this change, `mandate-cli`, each with
   `rh-sim` alone; DEC-529 item 3 lets CI only compile `live`, so the builder ran the `live` and
   `live,rh-sim` cases). The override has no function body that only `rh-sim` compiles, so every
   mutant of it is judged by the default build's unit tests.
2. **The owner's clock (`mandate-cli`, a new `rh-sim` feature).** The founder's grant is judged by
   the runtime at its effective time, the later of its `submitted_at` and the folded clock
   (DEC-156), and its step-up evidence must be at most 300 s older than that
   (`mandate_approval::stepup`), and the CLI stamps every gesture and answer with its own clock.
   A CLI on the system clock would stamp them on Saturday while the run's clock is Monday, so
   every one would be stale. The CLI therefore gets a feature of its own, `rh-sim`, which only the
   rehearsal script turns on, and with it the moment the owner ran a command is
   `MANDATE_RH_SIM_CLOCK`'s instant when set (one read per command, as before). The founder's CLI
   is built with no feature and holds neither the variable nor the module (`tests/sim_clock.rs`).
   This tightens nothing and loosens nothing in the founder's build; DEC-922 item 1's reading
   (only an explicit rehearsal feature reaches the simulator's world) is extended to the CLI.
3. **The simulator's broker clock (`mandate-rh-sim`).** The script takes an optional `clock`
   member, an RFC 3339 instant read strictly like every other member: the core's clock when the
