   script's core is built, and on the rehearsal server the clock each `tools/call` sets on the core
   before it acts, moved by the time the server has served. So the orders it records are stamped on
   the rehearsal's timeline, and the restart's `created_at_gte` listing (DEC-941) finds them.
   Without the member nothing changes: the core has no clock and its orders no creation time.
4. **One timeline (`scripts/r0-rehearsal.sh --sim-clock INSTANT`).** The script reads INSTANT when
   it starts and, for each process it starts, hands it the instant moved by the time since
   (`sim_now`): every CLI and runner process gets `MANDATE_RH_SIM_CLOCK`, the simulator its
   `clock`, the daily-bars writer its `<at>` (the example now takes an optional fourth argument, so
   the stored closes run through the last session completed at the run clock), and the instrument
   snapshot's `etp_classified_at` the run clock's date. With `--sim-clock` the CLI is built with
   `--features mandate-cli/rh-sim`; without it nothing changes and the system clock runs
   everything. The registered calendar has no holiday, so any weekday's 09:30 to 15:50 New York
   time is inside a regular session outside the close window; the agent's run used
   `2026-10-19T15:00:00Z` (a Monday, 11:00 New York time).
5. **What the override does not do.** It changes no rule: the session, age, skew, collar and
   cross-check rules all run unchanged against the instant. It reaches nothing new: the `rh-sim`
   build still dials only loopback (DEC-937 item 2) and reads no key (DEC-961). The live order is
   still made by the `live` build without `rh-sim`, on the system clock, inside a real session.

### Rationale

The rehearsal must run the code R0 runs. Moving the clock under every rule, rather than relaxing
the session rule, keeps every rule in the path, and the type alias and features keep the override
out of every build that can reach a broker or that the founder runs. A clock that moves from the
instant, rather than a frozen one, keeps every age, timeout and deadline real.

### Alternatives considered

- **Relax the session check in the rehearsal build.** Rejected: it removes a rule from the path the
  rehearsal exists to exercise, and every other rule would still read Saturday.
- **A frozen clock at the instant.** Rejected: the grant wait, the watch and every quote age would
  never move, so timeouts could never fire and the rehearsal would not be the live run's shape.
- **Override only the runner.** Rejected: the owner's grant and gesture codes would be judged
  against the runner's Monday and refused stale (item 2).
- **One offset shared through the variable.** Rejected: the founder named the variable an instant;
  the script computes each process's instant on one timeline instead, to within milliseconds.

<a id="dec-964"></a>

## DEC-964: CI runs the runner's tests in the `live,rh-sim` build, in one guarded job, narrowing DEC-529 item 3

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder-reserved, DEC-79: it narrows DEC-529 item 3, a safety rule the founder accepted). The founder confirmed it in their own words on [PR #1429](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1429#issuecomment-6229369331) on 2026-10-17 at 03:59 UTC: "I approve DEC-964: CI runs mandate-paper’s tests in the live,rh-sim build, loopback only, no credentials." |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder, answering the coordinator's question in the coordinator's session on 2026-10-17 at about 02:10 UTC, and confirming it on PR #1429; drafted by `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1420](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1420)) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1420, following #1417; DEC-963 item 3, DEC-529 item 3, DEC-922, DEC-464, DEC-871, DEC-873 |

The coordinator reserved DEC-964 for this decision.

### Context

DEC-529 item 3 lets no CI or release build enable the runner's `live` feature except one
compile-only `cargo check`, and `cargo xtask live-feature` enforces it. DEC-963 item 1 made every
`mandate-paper` test pass in the `live,rh-sim` build, the build the R0 rehearsal runs, and its
item 3 proposed that CI run it, leaving the decision to the founder.

The coordinator asked: "DEC-529 item 3 lets CI build the `live` feature only in one compile-only
check. Should CI also run the tests in the `live,rh-sim` build (simulator only, it still can't
reach real Robinhood)?" The founder answered, as the coordinator relays it: "Yes, run them".

### Decision

1. **One CI job tests the runner's rehearsal build with `live`.** The job `fast-rh-sim-live` of
   `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `cargo nextest run --locked --no-tests=fail --package
   mandate-paper --features live,rh-sim`: the tests of every crate that declares both `live` and
   `rh-sim`, which is the runner alone, since only the crate marked `live_feature` may declare
   `live`. An xtask test holds the crates declaring both equal to the runner and checks the job
   runs each. The required `fast` verdict needs the job.
2. **The build dials loopback alone.** In a build holding `rh-sim`, `rh-sim` wins over `live`
   (`Build::CURRENT` is the rehearsal), `MCP_HOST` and its pin are not compiled, and the endpoint
   is refused unless it is plain `http` on an IPv4 loopback address (DEC-922 items 1 and 2). A
   test in the suite the job runs,
   `a_build_holding_rh_sim_dials_loopback_alone_whatever_else_it_holds`, pins that the build that
   runs is the rehearsal, pins only the simulator's loopback host, dials nothing without the
   simulator's endpoint, and refuses Robinhood's own endpoint and any host off loopback.
3. **No credential reaches the job.** The step runs `.github/scripts/no-credentials.sh` first in
   the same shell, which fails, naming only the variables and never a value, when any
   environment name holds `KEY`, `SECRET`, `TOKEN`, `PASSWORD`, `PASSWD`, `CREDENTIAL`, `ALPACA`,
   `APCA` or `ROBINHOOD` in any case. `cargo xtask live-feature` refuses the job when any of its
   lines, or the workflow's top-level `env:`, names `secrets` or `github.token`, when an `env:`
   name there holds one of those pieces, or when the step is not exactly the guarded run.
4. **The live-feature check allows exactly this.** Besides the one compile-only check, one
   workflow line may pass `live`: `cargo nextest run` of the runner alone with exactly `live` and
   `rh-sim` (bare or as `<runner>/<feature>`), and only in `fast-rh-sim-live` of `ci.yml`. The
   same form in another job, another workflow, a script or build file, a second time, with
   another package, the whole workspace, another subcommand or another feature, is refused; that
   run may hold no live-only package. Every other refusal of DEC-529 item 3 stands: no other
   build, test or run with `live`, no `--all-features`, nothing that turns `live` on implicitly.
5. **Its own job, under ten minutes.** The run is a job of its own rather than a step of
   `fast-pending` (DEC-464): from a cold target it built and passed all 236 tests in about
   70 seconds on 4 cores. No `cargo xtask` group runs it, so `cargo xtask ci fast` and
   `cargo xtask check` do not; the `fast` jobs run `cargo xtask ci fast` plus this one job
   (DEC-871 as narrowed here). The live-feature check reads the build in the workflow itself,
   which it could not if xtask ran it.

### Rationale

DEC-963 left the `live` halves of its build-reading pins checked only by the founder's local
build. Running them in CI keeps the build the rehearsal and the live order use from rotting, and
the rehearsal build is the one `live` build that cannot reach a broker. Putting the invocation in
the workflow rather than in xtask keeps it where the live-feature check can see, place and limit
it, and the guard and the check together keep credentials out of the only CI job that enables
`live`.

### Alternatives considered

- **A `live,rh-sim` run inside `cargo xtask ci test-rh-sim`.** Rejected: the live-feature check
  reads workflows, not xtask's own commands, so it could not limit the run to one job, and the
  nightly workflow's `cargo xtask ci nightly` would run it too.
- **A step in `fast-pending`.** Rejected under DEC-464: add a shard rather than lengthen one.
- **The guard only as a static check.** Rejected: an action may write variables at run time, so
  the guard also runs in the shell that starts the tests.

<a id="dec-965"></a>

## DEC-965: The run's connection environment comes from the connection the run chose (R0 blocker R13)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176; every paper input refuses exactly as before, and a live run reaches these paths only after DEC-924's four conditions, which the founder accepted) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of claim [#1421](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1421)), under DEC-79, DEC-176 and DEC-924 |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; claim #1421; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R13; DEC-505 item 3, DEC-921 (one PR, tests first), DEC-924, DEC-952, DEC-957, DEC-962 |

### Context

On the rehearsal's clock (DEC-962) step 2l stopped at `the tracer stopped at Validate: a mandate
with no validation or policy violations`: V-001. The shell's run context
(`crates/mandate-shell/src/paper/context.rs`, `run_context`) handed the mandate validator
`connection_environment: Some(Paper)`, a constant from the paper adapter's first slice, so every
`live` mandate broke V-001 at the cycle's validation, although `version confirm` and `agent
deploy` had validated it against the recorded live connection. A probe build with only that
constant replaced showed two more constants of the same kind behind it, on the same path: the
cycle's admission refused every mandate that was not `paper` (`ShellError::NonPaperEnvironment`,
`the tracer runs only against the paper environment`), and every draft's envelope said the
constant `paper` (`envelope::ENVIRONMENT`), which a live workspace's streams, opened `live`
(DEC-952), would carry wrongly or refuse.

### Decision

1. **The run context names the chosen connection's environment.** `load_contexts` and
   `load_contexts_with_clock` take `connection: Environment`, the environment of the connection
   the run chose, and `run_context` hands V-001 `Some(connection)`. `mandate-paper`'s `run()`
   passes `connection.environment` of the live door's choice (`chosen`, the connection record
   DEC-924's admission passed on), never a constant. A paper connection validates exactly as
   before; a live mandate on a live connection passes V-001; and any mismatch between the
   mandate's environment and the connection's still fires V-001 (a property test over mandate
   and connection environment pairs pins it). The door's `connection` already refuses a record
   whose environment is not the mandate's (`connection_environment`), so V-001 is the second,
   independent check.
2. **The cycle admits a mandate only in the chosen connection's environment.** `Setup` carries
   `connection_environment`, set by `run()` (and by the own-fill booking) from the same
   connection. `admit` refuses `ShellError::EnvironmentMismatch` (`environment_mismatch`) when
   the admitted mandate's environment is not that one, before any stream exists. It replaces
   `NonPaperEnvironment`: on a paper connection a `live` or `backtest` mandate refuses exactly as
   before, under the new code; on a live connection a `paper` or `backtest` mandate refuses.
3. **Every draft says the chosen connection's environment.** `Envelope` carries `environment`,
   which every draft the shell composes takes from `Setup::connection_environment`
   (`journal_environment`); the constant `ENVIRONMENT` is gone. TI-7 (E7-7) is read as: every
   journaled draft carries the run's connection's environment, and item 2 ensures the admitted
   mandate's is the same. Every paper run still journals `paper` only; the paper scanner test
   is unchanged and a new one scans a live run for `live`.
4. **Places that name `paper` and stay.** `crates/mandate-shell/src/control.rs:372` (DEC-505
   item 3): the fold's value for a connection the control stream holds no record of. The only
   connection the door can choose for such a mandate is `paper_default`'s paper one, and a live
   mandate there is refused `Unrecorded` by the door and V-001 by the fold, so the constant is the
   chosen connection's environment by construction. The test doubles and fixtures that build
   paper runs stay paper. One more constant was found that is not an environment:
   `crates/mandate-shell/src/tracer.rs:779` opens a missing account stream with `broker`
   `alpaca`. A live run never reaches it (the connect check opens the live account stream
   first, observed at seq 1 of `acct:ws_r0:<ref>`), so it is recorded, not changed.
5. **Rehearsal result.** With these three changes the live run passes V-001 and the admission,
   and the shell journals the agent stream's `StreamOpened` as `live`. The rehearsal stops at two
   new blockers, recorded in the rehearsal record as R14 (2m and 2s: the runtime's fold refuses
   the account stream's `ConnectionChecked`) and R15 (2l on the Monday clock: the model's output
   from Friday's close is past the mandate's `max_output_age_s`, so the dry run decides nothing
   and prints nothing). Neither is fixed here (the brief: record and stop).

### Rationale

A constant cannot say which connection a run is on; the connection record the door chose can,
and DEC-924 made that choice the gate for a live run. Taking all three values from it keeps one
source for the run's environment and three independent refusals of a mismatch (the door's
record check, V-001, and the admission), where before there were three locks that refused every
live run whatever its connection.

### Alternatives considered

- **Take the environment from the mandate itself.** Rejected: V-001 would then compare the
  mandate with itself and could never fire.
- **Fix only the run context.** Rejected: the probe build showed the admission refusing next,
  and the envelope's constant would have journaled `paper` on a live workspace's streams.
- **Make the DEC-505 fallback take the run's connection too.** Not needed: it applies only to an
  unrecorded connection, for which the door chooses paper or refuses, and the fold runs before
  the door's connection is handed to the shell.

<a id="dec-967"></a>

## DEC-967: After R0 the first hosted version is a managed cell on k3s, built now in parallel off R0's path, trading real orders through the founder's own Robinhood account, with its hosting approved at the local-cell gate

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (founder, DEC-79: hosting, spending and live money). Items 1 to 3 and 6 to 11 were relayed by the coordinator and **confirmed by the founder in the recording agent's session on 2026-10-17** (answering "Are those your decisions?" with "Confirmed"). Items 12 to 16 the founder gave directly in that session. Items 4 and 5 are Accepted (agent: readings under DEC-176 that only narrow what the plan may assume). Item 3 is superseded in part by item 16 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The founder. Items 1 to 3 and 6 to 11 first given in the coordinator's session on 2026-10-17 from about 02:50 UTC, relayed, then confirmed directly; items 12 to 16 given directly. Recorded by an agent (Claude Code, the managed-cells planning lane). Items 4 and 5: the recording agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | The [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md); backlog E21-28 to E21-50; [infrastructure](../../design/infrastructure.md) §1, §3, §7, §12; [DEC-434](DEC-434.md) items 13, 15, 18, 19 and 20; [DEC-822](DEC-822.md); [DEC-529](DEC-529.md) |

### Context

The R0 rehearsal ([brief](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)) is the last step before the first live
trade, and its lanes hold `crates/mandate-paper/src/lib.rs` and, in `crates/mandate-shell/src/`,
`control.rs`, `adapters.rs` and `paper/`. What hosts the product after R0 was open: the
infrastructure design names a Phase 1 paper environment on one host, a staging cell from M8
(DEC-434 item 19), and managed cells after the Phase 2 gate, and it leaves the hosting provider
and every budget to the founder (DEC-434 items 13 and 18).

The coordinator reports asking the founder three questions. The questions and answers, as
relayed:

| Question | Options offered | The founder's answer, as relayed |
|---|---|---|
| "After R0, what should the first hosted version be?" | "Straight to managed cells"; "One VM, paper, always-on" | "Straight to managed cells" |
| "Can I start building the service pieces in parallel now?" | Not relayed | "Parallel, non-R0 crates" |
| Spending to plan around | Not relayed | "Cloudflare Workers for web/" only; no VM or cloud cluster spend approved yet |
| (Direction, about 03:00 UTC, unprompted) | — | "lets do this properly. If I were to deploy agent, i dont want to change infra again and again. Same infra can be built at small scale and then scalable on more traffic. dont over engineer, but dont under engineer." |
| Cell #1's host | Managed GKE or EKS; decide later; k3s on Hetzner | "k3s on 1-2 Hetzner VMs" |
| The journal's Postgres | Not relayed | "In-cluster, operator-managed" (CloudNativePG: synchronous standby, WAL archive to object storage, point-in-time recovery) |
| About 03:15 UTC | — | "we would want to build both control plane and data plane APIs too, right?" |
| Hetzner location, then storage (about 03:10 UTC) | Not relayed | Location Ashburn, VA (us-east). Storage: "could we use minio?", then "cloudflare r2 is fine too if it is not expensive" |

### Decision

Items 6 to 11 follow items 1 to 3 because they were answered after them; items 4 and 5 are the recording agent's readings.

1. **The first hosted version after R0 is a managed cell** (infrastructure §1.1, §3.2: a
   Kubernetes cell running one or more workspace deployments, every process its own pod), not one
   always-on VM under systemd (item 6 says how small it starts). It runs **paper and venue demo environments only**. Live stays out of every cell
   until the Phase 2 gate, counsel's sign-off and the M13 penetration test, as infrastructure §1.1
   and OPS-5 already require, **except** the founder's own Robinhood account, which item 15 makes
   live.
2. **The cell's service pieces are built now, in parallel lanes, in crates off R0's path.** No lane
   edits `crates/mandate-paper/src/lib.rs`, `crates/mandate-shell/src/control.rs`,
   `crates/mandate-shell/src/adapters.rs` or anything under `crates/mandate-shell/src/paper/` until
   R0 closes. A lane uses `mandate-shell` and `mandate-paper` only through what they already make
   public. The lanes, their order and the crates each needs are the
   [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md).
3. **The only spend approved is Cloudflare Workers for `web/`** (superseded in part by item 16). No VM, Kubernetes cluster, managed
   database, object store, key management service, container registry, paging tool or paid CI
   runner is approved. Everything the cell needs is built and tested first on a **local and CI
   cell** (kind or k3d on the CI runner and on a developer machine), with no hosting spend. Each
   hosting cost the cell will need is listed in the plan as a decision for the founder, with a
   rough cost.

6. **One architecture, small first.** The same Helm-packaged cell runs on k3s at small scale (one
   or two nodes), grows by adding nodes and then cells, and moves to managed Kubernetes later
   without changing the application or the chart. No work item may assume a host-specific feature
   that would force a rewrite, and nothing that matters only at large scale goes in the first
   slices. The single-VM systemd path in `deploy/` (DEC-822) becomes a dev and demo convenience,
   not the production path.
7. **Cell #1's compute is k3s on one or two Hetzner VMs in Ashburn, VA** (near the brokers, market
   data and US users), with server types confirmed as offered there at order time. The VMs are
   still spend the founder approves with their cost (the plan's H1).
8. **The journal's Postgres runs in the cell, managed by the CloudNativePG operator**: a
   synchronous standby, WAL archived to object storage, point-in-time recovery. This answers
   DEC-434 item 15 (self-run) for cell #1.
9. **Object storage is Cloudflare R2** (location hint eastern North America), off the Hetzner VMs,
   so backups sit in a separate failure domain. Its first story checks that R2 gives the
   write-once retention and the start-up lock check journal spec §6.2 requires: per-object S3
   Object Lock, or R2's bucket lock rules. If only bucket-level, either the start-up check reads
   R2's bucket-lock rule and accepts nothing weaker, or the cold store alone goes to a provider with
   standard S3 Object Lock in compliance mode (for example Backblaze B2), recorded as a decision.
   Storage sits behind a plain S3 endpoint and a lock-check interface, so providers swap by
   configuration. R2 is spend the founder approves with its cost (the plan's H3).
10. **MinIO only as an optional in-cell cache**, never the only copy and never the backup. Using it
    at all puts its AGPL-3.0 licence to the founder and counsel first (DEC-434 item 16).

11. **Three API surfaces, each a lane:** the workspace services API (first, since the web app, the
    CLI and the owner's MCP adapter use it), a minimal first slice of the global control plane
    (directory, cell routing, fleet health; licensing, billing and the relay later), and the data
    plane (market-data ingest and serving, and the internal interfaces between workspace services
    and the agent processes). The plan says for each which spec it implements, what exists, the
    first slice, and how it runs in cell #1 without rework.

Given by the founder directly in the recording agent's session on 2026-10-17, in their own words
(seen by the recording agent, not relayed):

12. **Robinhood is integrated too:** "I want Robinhood integrated too."
13. **The API is not coupled to the web app:** "And APIs too. Lets not couple web with this, web will
    call the API but users will also be able to" (the message ends there; the plan reads it as
    users calling the API directly; item 17 confirms it). The workspace API is the public interface; `web/` is one client
    with no private route.
14. **Complete integration, no special work:** "complete integration and no special work" (spelling
    corrected), given in answer to whether Robinhood should stop at the simulator in the cell or also run
    live. The plan's reading: Robinhood goes through the same connector path as Alpaca, is fully
    built and drilled in the cell against `mandate-rh-sim`, and turning live on is configuration of
    the same code. **What it does not decide:** live in a hosted cell still needs the Phase 2 gate,
    counsel's sign-off and the M13 penetration test (infrastructure §1.1), and a narrowing of
    DEC-529 item 3 (only the runner may build `live`). Those stay Proposed (the plan's L-1) until
    the founder records them; meanwhile no cell image enables `live`. **Item 15 narrows this.**
15. **Real trades through Robinhood from the cell, not the simulator:** "i dont want simulator but
    real trade through robinhood" (spelling corrected). Accepted (founder, DEC-79: live money),
    given directly in this session. Cell #1 places real orders through Robinhood **for the
    founder's own agentic account**, under the founder's mandate (its allocation and
    `max_order_usd`, starting from the values DEC-529 used, with `ask` for openings), on the same
    connector path as every broker. Live for any other user still waits for the Phase 2 gate,
    counsel and the penetration test. **What it narrows, for that one account and that one
    executor image only** (each stays in force everywhere else):
    - **ES-23 and OPS-5, and infrastructure §1.1** ("only one environment may ever touch live
      money", production, after the Phase 2 gate): cell #1 gains one live connection and one
      signed live executor image; every other image stays without `live` and every other process
      without a route to a live host. §1.1 says so.
    - **DEC-529 item 3** (only the runner may declare `live`; CI only compiles it): a second crate,
      the cell's executor binary (`mandate-daemon`), may declare `live`; `xtask/layers.toml` and
      `cargo xtask live-feature` change to allow exactly that crate (E21-48), and CI still only
      compiles it. `mandate-paper`'s own `live` feature is the R0 runner's and is not touched.
    - **DEC-529 item 13** ("one order, then stop"; "no agent runs the live deployment") and
      **DEC-529's lapse** (the flat account and the spent connection record): replaced for the
      cell by a standing live deployment the deployment manager runs. The connection record no
      longer spends on the first fill; the mandate's envelope is the bound.
    - **DEC-529 item 10** (the session lives only on the founder's machine): replaced for the cell
      by the hosted login, with the token held only in the vault and the executor's memory
      (E21-47, CN-1).
    - **`AGENTS.md` rule 8, as DEC-529 already narrowed it:** real orders are placed on the
      founder's account by the founder's own deployment and confirmations. No test, CI job or
      agent places an order or handles the credential, and no agent builds, starts or reads the
      live run's output. The founder builds the one live executor image from a commit on `main`
      and signs it with the hardware key, as DEC-529 item 3 has the founder build the runner.
    **What it keeps, which the cell must supply (E21-48 criteria):**
    - **Trading spec §4.2 and DEC-529 item 12, the live quote source.** A live equity agent needs
      the `sip` profile; DEC-529 item 12 narrowed that, for one order, to Robinhood's own quote
      cross-checked with Alpaca's IEX quote. The cell's live deployment gets no wider reading:
      either the `sip` profile (paid data, the founder's spend), or item 12's cross-checked quote
      once trading spec §4.2's change extends it from one order to a standing deployment. Until one
      is recorded, the live deployment refuses openings for want of a quote source.
    - **DEC-529 item 11, the account.** Kept in full: the agentic account is not shared with
      another agent, the founder attests it is a cash account or has margin disabled, and the gate's
      buying power is the lower of cash and the broker's figure. The account is flat when the
      cell's live deployment is first deployed; after that the mandate's envelope bounds it.
    - **DEC-155 item 4 and mandate spec §6.1, step-up.** `cli_confirm` stays paper-only except for
      DEC-529's one order. The cell's live confirmation, deployment and each grant use E9-4's
      step-up (a signed passkey assertion), so E21-48 waits for E9-4.
    **What it does not decide (Proposed for the founder, DEC-79):** whether counsel's review
    (DEC-529 item 9 waived it for one order only) and the M13 penetration test are needed before
    the first live order from a hosted, internet-reachable cell on the founder's own account.
    Until the founder decides, the most conservative option holds: E21-48 waits for both. The
    first real order from the cell also waits for the conditions the existing rules set (the
    plan's E21-48).

Readings the recording agent accepts under DEC-176 (each only
narrows what the plan may assume):

4. **DEC-822 is untouched.** The demo's API host (DEC-822, founder-accepted spend for the
   2026-10-26 demo) is neither superseded nor extended. The managed-cells plan neither depends on
   that VM nor adds a process to it. Whether it keeps running after the demo is asked of the
   founder in the plan's decisions list (H0).
5. **The local and CI cell is not staging.** DEC-434 item 19's minimal staging cell needs hosting
   spend, so it waits for the founder's approval (the plan's decision H1). Until then the local
   and CI cell runs the drills staging would run, and its results are recorded as local-cell
   results, never as staging's. A drill the design says must run in staging (infrastructure §6.4)
   is not counted as passed by a local-cell run.

Answered directly in the same session, after the items above:

16. **Hosting spend is approved, effective when the local-cell gate passes.** Asked "Do you approve
    that hosting spend now?" (both Hetzner VMs, about $35 to $45 a month; Cloudflare R2, about $0
    to $2; a second-region copy of the cold store, about $0 to $1), the founder answered "Approve
    when gate passes". So the plan's H1 (two VMs in Ashburn), H3 (R2) and L-2 (the second-region
    copy) are approved, and nothing is bought until every local-cell drill passes in CI on `main`
    (the plan's local-cell gate). The founder orders them; agents never get host or account access
    (DEC-822). This supersedes item 3's "no VM spend" for these three only; every other cost in
    the plan (H4 to H10) is still the founder's to decide when its trigger comes.
17. **Users reach the API from their own code and their own agents.** Asked what users should be
    able to do with the API directly, the founder chose "Code plus own agents": a user's own token
    for their code (item 13), and owner-connected agents over MCP (DEC-141, workspace API spec
    §3.8) as a first-class lane of this plan, not a later story.

### Rationale

Going straight to managed cells means the processes, probes, drain and hand-over, vault, packaging
and observability are built once, the way the product ships them, instead of first for a VM under
systemd and again for Kubernetes. Building them now, off R0's files, keeps the first live trade's
path free of merge conflicts. Holding all hosting spend until a local cell passes its drills means
the first money spent runs software already proven against the same chart.

### Alternatives considered

- **One VM, paper, always-on** (offered; not chosen, as relayed). Faster to a hosted paper agent,
  but the supervisor, probes and packaging would be rebuilt for the cell.
- **Wait for R0 before starting the service pieces.** Not chosen, as relayed.
- **Managed GKE or EKS for cell #1, or deciding later** (offered for item 7; not chosen, as
  relayed). The chart keeps managed Kubernetes open (item 6).
- **MinIO as the object store** (asked about; item 10 keeps it to an optional cache): it would put
  the backups on the same VMs as the journal, and bundling it is a licensing question.
- **Treat the local cell as staging** (item 5). Rejected: staging is a hosted environment the
  founder pays for and DEC-434 item 19 names; calling a CI cluster staging would overstate what has
  been drilled.

<a id="dec-969"></a>

## DEC-969: The runtime folds `ConnectionChecked`; the rehearsal's dry run expects the ask (R0 R14, R15)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each under DEC-176 — each refuses more or fills a gap by the reading that adds no risk; none touches live money, spending, legal text or a safety rule) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The R14/R15 builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176; one PR with its tests first (DEC-921) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R14 and R15; claim #1428 |

The placing run refused `the runtime refused its input: ConnectionChecked is not interpreted
until the story that writes it` (`crates/mandate-runtime/src/state.rs`, `fold`'s catch-all),
because the connect check DEC-946 journals is the account stream's second record and the
runtime's fold listed no such type (R14). The executor's fold refused it too (`E7-2`, its
`owner_elsewhere`), one step later on the same replay. The dry run on the rehearsal's Monday clock
decided nothing because the model's output was past its age (R15). These are the readings that
close them.

### Decision

1. **What the runtime folds.** `ConnectionChecked` (journal spec §9.8) is interpreted: the latest
   one of the deployment's own connection (`connection_id` equal to the deployment's) is held as
   whether every listed check passed and its risk clock. A check of another connection is read
   and validated but governs nothing here: another connection's failure says nothing of this
   one, and its pass must never clear this one's failure.
2. **A failed check refuses openings.** While the latest own check has any result `failed`, an
   `open` or `increase` is judged in the runtime's dry run as `deny`, reason `account_restricted`,
   before the gate is asked. That is the outcome connections spec §8.1 gives a later check's
   failure (`suspended`, agents `exits_only`) and trading spec §7.3 row 5 the gate's reason for
   it. The executor's own halt (`AccountRestrictionChanged`, then `AgentModeApplied`, committed
   before the failed check, journal spec §9.8) is unchanged; this is the runtime refusing the
   same openings from the record itself, so neither depends on the other arriving first. A
   connect-time failure (`connect`, `reconnect`, `reauthorize`) refuses the connection and moves
   no state (DEC-674), and reading it here only refuses more. The reason is the gate's existing
   code, so no new reason enters trading spec §9.1.
3. **A stale check refuses openings.** A passing check covers an opening while
   `now < at + 86 400` risk-clock seconds (`CONNECTION_CHECK_COVERS_S`, one day: the period of
   the daily check, connections spec §8.1 and §9.2). From then on the opening is refused as in
   item 2 until a newer passing check is folded. `now` is the evaluation's tick, or for a grant
   the second the response is processed at. The spec states no bound; any bound only refuses
   more, so DEC-176 lets an agent take it. Consequence for the founder's live run: an
   opening more than a day after the last check needs a fresh one; a second
   `mandate-paper --connect-check` appends only its `ConnectionChecked` (DEC-946 item 6) and
   renews it. The executor's `executor_start` and `daily` checks are not written yet (E7-17).
4. **Never an exit** (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). Items 2 and 3 read only proposals whose purpose adds
   risk (`open`, `increase`). A risk exit, an owner exit, a discretionary exit, a protective order
   and a kill switch's flatten are judged as before, whatever the checks say; the restart's
   re-hand is unchanged. The same dry run governs a grant's re-validation (mandate spec §6.4 check
   11): a grant read after a failed or stale check is skipped `dry_run` `deny`,
   `account_restricted`, and nothing is handed.
5. **What still refuses.** A `ConnectionChecked` whose shape is outside journal spec §9.8 refuses
   the fold `non_canonical_payload` rather than guessing (DEC-85): no `connection_id`, an unknown
   `occasion`, `results` missing or not a list, an item that is not an object or names an unknown
   check (`uniqueness` included: it is the control services', never the executor's) or an
   unknown result, a failure with no reason or a pass with one (rule 58), a list missing `scope`,
   `environment` or `account` (rule 57; `contract` may be absent, since only an MCP connection
   lists it and the record does not say which it is), and a risk clock that is neither the
   canonical integer nor a §4.7 timestamp on the whole second. `ConnectionStateChanged` and
   `ConnectionCredentialRefreshed`, which nothing writes yet, and every unknown type still refuse
   `not_interpreted`. In the executor, `ConnectionChecked` folds as a record only: its risk clock
   is read like every account event's, and the halt it may follow is the executor's own earlier
   records.
6. **No absent check is a failure.** With no `ConnectionChecked` of its connection folded, the
   runtime leaves openings to the other gates, as before: Phase 1 paper connections journal none,
   and a live connection cannot be recorded without a passing connect check (journal spec §9.8
   rule 68, `connection record`'s `check_missing`). `FOLD_VERSION` stays 1: no golden journal
   holds a `ConnectionChecked`, so no committed fold output changes.
7. **R15: the age rule is unchanged, and on a Monday it decides nothing.** The model's output is
   the moving-average crossover's opinion at the close of the last completed regular session
   before the run clock (`mandate_modelhost::evaluate`), and is fresh only while
   `now − as_of ≤ max_output_age_s` and `now < expires_at` (mandate spec §8.1; the builder's
   check). The mandate schema caps `max_output_age_s` at 86 400. So on Monday 2026-10-19 at
   15:00 UTC the latest possible output is Friday's 20:00 UTC close, 67 hours old, and no script
   change can produce an output that is fresh at that clock without relaxing the rule: the
   script already writes the bars at the sim clock, and the model's output follows the bars and
   the calendar. The dry run on a Monday (or the day after a holiday) correctly decides nothing.
   The script now expects the spec's answer on a day the output is fresh: the R0 mandate asks
   for every opening (DEC-529), and a run without `--place-one-order` never asks, so 2l passes
   when it stops at `Classify: the proposal classified ask` with nothing sent. The rehearsal is
   run on Monday as asked, and also on Tuesday 2026-10-20 at 15:00 UTC, where the output is fresh,
   to reach the steps behind it. For the founder: a live order on a Monday cannot come from a
   daily-close model under the current schema; that is a limit on the envelope, not a defect, and
   widening `max_output_age_s` past a day would be a spec change for the founder to make.

8. **What the rehearsal showed, and where it stops.** On Monday the placing run replays the
   connect check and decides nothing (item 7). On Tuesday the dry run stops at the ask, the
   placing run asks, and the founder's `cli_confirm` grant is committed and then refused by the
   runtime `step_up_method`, because the shell hands the admission no live `cli_confirm` scope
   (R16); verify of the control and agent streams fails `artifact_missing` at the approval's
   content object (R17). Both are recorded in the rehearsal record and not fixed here: R16 is a
   step-up change with its own tests first, and R17 needs the journal spec's reading of
   `artifact_refs` for inline content.

### Rationale

The record is now on every live account stream, so refusing it stops every live replay. Folding it
as inert would discard a fact that, by the specs, halts openings; folding it into the dry run makes
the runtime refuse the same openings the executor's restriction refuses, from the journal alone and
deterministically on replay, without touching the mode machine (no new `AgentModeChanged`), so no
committed journal changes. Reading only risk-adding purposes keeps rule 13 by construction.

### Alternatives considered

- **A local hold in the effective mode** (`exits_only` while the check fails or is stale): it would
  journal `AgentModeChanged` copies the spec gives to the executor's `AgentModeApplied`, and a
  staleness hold read against the replayed risk clock could differ from the live one at a restart.
- **Inert, as the other record-only types**: simplest, but leaves a failed check with no effect in
  the runtime until the executor's restriction arrives.
- **A new reason code** (`connection_check_failed`, `connection_check_stale`): clearer, but a new
  gate reason is a trading spec §9.1 change (ES-22); `account_restricted` is already the spec's
  outcome for an unusable connection.
- **For R15, a rehearsal mandate with a longer `max_output_age_s`**: the schema refuses above a
  day, and it would be relaxing the age bound the dispatch rules out.

<a id="dec-970"></a>

## DEC-970: The live run hands the runtime DEC-923's `cli_confirm` scope, and stores each request's content object (R0 R16, R17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each under DEC-176. Item 1 carries DEC-923, which the founder accepted, to the run without widening it; item 4 takes the strict reading of the journal spec's artifact rule. Neither touches live money, spending, legal text or a safety rule) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The R16/R17 builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176; one PR with its tests first (DEC-921) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R16 and R17; claim #1428 |

On the Tuesday rehearsal clock the founder's `cli_confirm` grant was committed on the control
stream and then refused by the runtime `step_up_method`, because the shell built its mandate view
with `live_cli_confirm: None` (R16). Verify of the control and agent streams failed
`artifact_missing`, because the `ApprovalRequested` lists its `content_hash` in `artifact_refs` and
nothing put the content object in the store (R17). These are the readings that close them.

### Decision

1. **Where the scope comes from.** DEC-923's `LiveScope` is read by the run exactly as
   `mandate approvals approve` reads it (`crates/mandate-cli/src/approvals.rs`, `live_scope`).
   The record is the mandate connection's latest `ConnectionEstablished` or `ConnectionRevoked` by
   `seq`, a revocation outranking an establishment at the same `seq`. That record must be an
   establishment whose `environment` is `live`. Its `broker` gives the scope's broker:
   `robinhood` is `Robinhood`, and anything else is `Other`, which DEC-923 never admits. The
   confirmed mandate (the version the run deployed, DEC-957) gives `risk.max_order_usd` and the
   pinned instruments' asset ids. The owner is the one `mandate_shell::approvers::members`
   already reads for DEC-953, so a membership record the run does not fold, or a record naming no
   user, gives no scope. The function is `mandate_shell::approvers::live_cli_confirm`.
2. **Only a live run.** The scope is read only when the connection the live door chose is
   `live`. A paper run gives none, and the runtime admits `cli_confirm` on paper anyway (DEC-155
   item 4). The runtime still reads the scope only on a `live` mandate view
   (`crates/mandate-runtime/src/escalation.rs`, `answered`). `mandate-approval`'s
   `live_cli_confirm` still checks every DEC-923 condition at each grant: Robinhood, a mandate cap
   of at most 100 USD, one pinned instrument, a US equity, an order of at most 100 USD, and the
   owner as approver. This change decides none of them and widens none.
3. **How it reaches the runtime.** `RunContext` gains `live_cli_confirm`, which `run_context`
   leaves `None` and the runner sets from item 1 after `load_contexts_with_clock`. The shell's
   `runtime_view` hands it to `ApprovalSettings::live_cli_confirm`. With no scope, every live
   `cli_confirm` grant is refused `step_up_method` as before, and nothing is sent (`AGENTS.md`
   rule 3).
4. **R17: store the content object, never stop listing it.** Journal spec §9.7 types
   `ApprovalRequested.content_hash` as a `ref`. §3 lists every `ref` in `artifact_refs`, and the
   type table says "the object must be stored and re-hashes (§11 check 6)". So the strict reading
   is to store the object, not to drop the hash from `artifact_refs`. The placing run's journal
   (`StoreJournal`, given the run's store as `Sources.content_store`) puts each `ApprovalRequested`
   draft's canonical `content` in the store under its `content_hash`, before the append. If the
   content is missing, if the hash does not name exactly that content, or if the store refuses the
   put, the batch is not appended (`Unavailable`). No record then names an object the store does
   not hold, and the ask is never made. The CLI's `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` cites the same hash
   and finds the same object, since the run and the CLI share the store. A run with no content
   store (the dry run's scratch journal, the booking pass, and the tests' journals) puts nothing,
   as before.

### Rationale

DEC-923 was accepted for exactly this deployment, and the CLI already checks it locally before it
commits a grant. The runtime refused only because the run never told it the scope. Reading the
scope from the same records, by the same rule, keeps the CLI's local check and the runtime's
admission in agreement. Storing the content object keeps verification strict: an exported stream
verifies against the store it was written with, or fails.

### Alternatives considered

- **A constant scope in the live build**: it would admit a grant whatever the records say, which
  DEC-923 forbids.
- **Reading the scope in `mandate-paper` from the door's `Connection`**: the door holds no owner,
  and the owner rule is already the shell's (DEC-953). Two readers of one rule would drift.
- **Dropping the inline content's hash from `artifact_refs`**: smaller, but it contradicts §3's
  "every `sha256:` reference" and §9.7's `ref` type. That would be a spec change (ES-22) that
  loosens verification.

<a id="dec-971"></a>

## DEC-971: Release images written by xtask on an empty base, cosign signatures, and a start-up check that trusts only compiled-in release keys outside dev and CI

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted; item 7 decided by the founder, DEC-2605 rows 8 and 9, with its hardware key superseded by [DEC-2024](infrastructure.md#dec-2024) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The lane R2 builder (`claude-code`), as reversible engineering choices under DEC-79 and DEC-176; cosign is D-7, which the founder pre-approved as a kind (DEC-990). Each item tightens the start-up rule OPS-15 states and loosens nothing |
| **Story or claim** | E21-35 (lane R2 of the [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md), DEC-967), claim [#1439](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1439) |

### Decision

1. **One image a release binary, written byte for byte by xtask.** `cargo xtask release images`
   writes an OCI image layout for each binary of a [DEC-972](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-972) release: an empty base,
   one uncompressed tar layer holding only the static binary at `/<binary>` (owned by root, mode
   0555), and, when the binary is signed, a second layer holding only `/<binary>.sig` (0444). The
   config runs the binary as its entrypoint as user `65532:65532`, with no `Cmd`, no environment,
   no working directory, no shell and no account. An image is a pure function of its binary and
   signature, so it needs no Docker daemon, and the binary's layer is the same whoever signed it.
   A read-only root filesystem is the pod's setting (E21-36's chart); nothing in the image writes.
   `mandate-rh-sim`'s image is the loopback test double for the local cell's drills (DEC-124),
   never a broker.
2. **The start-up check (OPS-15)** is `mandate-release`'s `startup::check_running`, which a
   process runs before anything else. It reads its tier from `MANDATE_DEPLOYMENT_TIER` (`dev`,
   `ci`, `staging`, `production`). **No setting, or one it does not know, is production.** It reads
   the running executable's bytes (`/proc/self/exe`) and the signature beside the path it started
   from (`<executable>.sig`, or the file `MANDATE_RELEASE_SIGNATURE` names), a cosign signature:
   ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256, DER, base64, verified with `ring`. Then:
   - a signature that verifies against a **compiled-in release key** (`startup::RELEASE_KEYS`)
     starts in every tier, as `release`;
   - in **dev and CI only**, an unsigned build starts as `unsigned`, and a signature that verifies
     against the test key `MANDATE_RELEASE_TEST_KEY` names starts as `test`. Outside dev and CI the
     test key's file is not even read;
   - every other case is refused, with a generic code and exit status 2: `release_unsigned`
     (no signature outside dev and CI), `release_signature_not_verified` (a signature no trusted
     key verifies, **in every tier**), `release_signature_malformed`, `release_unreadable`.
   The admitted value carries the build digest, which the process journals as `actor.build`.
   The tier is a setting of the pod, so whoever can set a pod's environment can run an unsigned
   build as `ci`: the chart (E21-36) sets `MANDATE_DEPLOYMENT_TIER` itself, from the cell's values,
   and no tenant or workspace input reaches it. A wrong signature is refused whatever the tier.
3. **Where the check runs.** `mandate-agentd` runs it first in `main`. `mandate-rh-sim` does not:
   it is the test double, never run against a broker. `mandate` (the CLI) runs it after R0, with
   DEC-972 item 7's slice, because its entry is R0's (DEC-967 item 2). The long-running processes
   E21-30 adds run it through `mandate-process`'s start-up hook (E21-28) once that lands.
4. **CI signs only with an ephemeral test key.** `release images --test-sign` makes a cosign key
   pair with no password, signs each binary offline (no transparency log), deletes the private
   half, and keeps the public half. `release drill` then runs `mandate-agentd` from three images
   unpacked as a runtime unpacks them (the test-signed one, the same binary unsigned, and one
   signed by a key nothing trusts) and requires the refusals and admissions item 2 states. Every
   `release` workflow run does this, pull requests included.
5. **Publishing: from a push to `main` only, to GHCR, by digest** (per arch, under one
   multi-arch index: [DEC-1060](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-1060)). The `publish` job holds the
   only `packages: write` and `id-token: write` in the workflow, runs only on a push to `main`
   (an xtask test checks both), rebuilds the release and requires `build-a`'s digest, then pushes
   each image to `ghcr.io/kunwarshivam/mandate/<binary>` as `main-<commit>`. It requires the
   registry to report the image's own digest, signs that digest keyless with cosign under the
   run's GitHub OIDC identity, attaches the binary's SBOM as a CycloneDX attestation, and keeps
   `PUBLISHED` (each `image@sha256:…`) as the run's artifact. The push uses the OCI distribution
   API through `curl`, with the token only in 0600 header files and cosign's stdin, never on a
   command line or in a log, and an upload location the registry names on another host is
   refused, so the token goes only to the registry. The repository is public, so its GHCR images
   cost nothing (H5); GHCR makes a new package private, so the founder sets each of the three
   public once after the first `publish` (no spend), or the cell pulls with a read token.
6. **Promotion.** `main` → the local cell's CI drills (lane C1 pulls the images by digest, or uses
   the run's layouts) → staging once H1 exists, as its own story. Nothing deploys from a branch.
   Published images carry no binary signature until item 7 is decided, so they start only in dev
   and CI.
7. **Proposed, the founder's:** the founder's hardware key (ES-17, H8) is the only release key,
   and none is wired: `RELEASE_KEYS` is empty, so today **no binary starts in staging or
   production**. The founder decides (a) when to add the hardware key's public half to
   `RELEASE_KEYS` and sign releases with it off CI, and (b) whether hosted cell #1, paper and demo
   only, may run before that as `ci`. Until the founder answers, the conservative option holds:
   nothing starts outside dev and CI unsigned.

### Rationale

OPS-15 asks for a start-up check that refuses an unsigned binary outside dev and CI, and infrastructure §11 asks
for distroless, non-root images with no shell. An attacker who could change a pod's environment
could set the tier to `dev`, so the tier only loosens what is unsigned or test-signed, never what a
wrong signature is: a wrong signature is refused everywhere, and the release keys come from the
binary itself, not its environment. Keyless signatures prove which workflow published an image,
but checking them needs Fulcio's chain and Rekor at start, so the in-process check verifies
against pinned keys. Writing the layout in xtask gives a reproducible image without an unpinned
daemon.

### Alternatives considered

- **`docker buildx` or `ko`:** a daemon or tool outside D-7, and image digests that depend on it;
  the image is three JSON documents and a tar.
- **A tier taken from the trading environment** (live means production): the check would then pass an unsigned binary
  running paper in a hosted cell, which OPS-15 forbids.
- **A trust file read at start in every tier:** whoever can change the pod could add a key.
- **Keyless verification in process:** Fulcio's chain and an online transparency log at every
  start, for a check that a pinned key does offline.
- **`cosign load` to push:** it only reads layouts `cosign save` wrote.

<a id="dec-972"></a>

## DEC-972: The release build: static musl binaries under cargo-auditable, an SBOM from each binary, two builds compared in CI, and the binary's SHA-256 as its build digest

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted; item 3's "each `main` commit" superseded by [DEC-2025](infrastructure.md#dec-2025): each released `main` commit, the tip of a burst of merges |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The lane R1 builder (`claude-code`), as reversible engineering choices under DEC-79 and DEC-176; the tools are D-7, which the founder pre-approved as a kind (DEC-990) |
| **Story or claim** | E21-8 (lane R1 of the [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md), DEC-967), claim [#1439](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1439), PR [#1466](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1466); reserved as DEC-970 and renumbered when [#1464](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1464) merged that number first |

### Decision

1. **What a release is.** `cargo xtask release build <out>` builds, from the checked-out commit
   with `--locked`, the binaries `mandate` (`mandate-cli`), `mandate-agentd` and `mandate-rh-sim`
   (the loopback test double the local cell runs beside an executor, DEC-124; never a broker) for
   `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`, statically linked (`-C target-feature=+crt-static`), in the
   `release` profile. It names no feature, so no release build holds `live` (DEC-529 item 3); a
   test checks that no shipped package is the crate `xtask/layers.toml` marks `live_feature`. The
   live executor image is B3's (E21-48) and is not built here.
2. **What makes it reproducible.** Every machine path rustc could write into a binary is
   remapped to a fixed prefix (`--remap-path-prefix`: the checkout to `/mandate`, `CARGO_HOME` to
   `/cargo`, `RUSTUP_HOME` to `/rustup`); `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is the commit's time; incremental
   compilation is off; the build's environment is cleared to the home, terminal, proxy and
   certificate settings plus its own, so no `RUSTC_WRAPPER`, inherited rustflags or `cargo run`
   variable reaches it; the toolchain is `rust-toolchain.toml`'s; and the musl target's C code
   (`ring`) is built with the runner's `gcc`, named rather than searched for. The build keeps its
   own target directory (`target/release-build`), so its flags never rebuild the workspace's.
   **Residual:** `gcc` and the linker are the runner image's, not pinned. If the `ubuntu-24.04`
   image changes them between the two jobs, the builds can differ and `compare` fails: loudly, and
   never by passing a build it should not. Each build logs `rustc -vV`, `gcc --version` and
   `ld --version` so such a failure can be told from a real one.
3. **The proof is two builds in CI.** `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds each `main` commit
   twice, on two runners, in two checkout paths, with no cache, and fails unless both print the
   same digest of their `SHA256SUMS`, which lists every binary's SHA-256. A pull request that
   changes the release build runs the same jobs and keeps nothing; only `main` and a manual run
   keep `build-a`'s output as the run's artifact (ES-12). The workflow runs only `cargo xtask`, so
   the tools' pins and the build's flags live in one place, `xtask/src/release.rs`.
4. **The build digest is the binary's SHA-256.** `mandate-release`'s `BuildDigest` is the SHA-256
   of an executable's bytes, the same digest `SHA256SUMS` lists, written `sha256:<hex>` as the
   envelope's `actor.build` admits (journal spec §3). A process takes its own from
   `/proc/self/exe` on Linux, which names the running file even after its path is replaced, so a
   release installed over a running one cannot make the old process journal the new digest. The
   digest is what R2 signs and checks at start (OPS-15, E21-35), so the release's identity, its
   signature and what the journal names are one value.
5. **The SBOM comes from the binary.** `cargo-auditable` embeds each binary's resolved dependency
   list in the binary; `syft` reads it back and writes one CycloneDX JSON SBOM a binary
   (`sbom/<binary>.cdx.json`), offline. The SBOMs carry a generation time, so they are not
   compared; the binaries are.
6. **Tools.** `cargo-auditable` 0.7.7 and `syft` 1.52.0, each the x86_64 Linux asset of its
   GitHub release, checked against a pinned SHA-256 (checked against the project's own published
   checksums when pinned), installed by `cargo xtask release tools` under `$CARGO_HOME/mandate-release-tools`, never
   inside the checkout: cargo hashes the path of `cargo-auditable`, its `RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER`,
   into every workspace crate's metadata, so a tool inside each checkout made two checkouts'
   binaries differ (found by the first two-checkout run).
   They are not in `.cursor/install.sh` or `cargo xtask check`, which never build a release.
7. **Wiring the digest into each writer is its own slice.** The writers that journal today name
   a placeholder `build` (a digest of the crate's name and version): `mandate-shell`'s
   `envelope.rs`, on R0's run path, and `mandate-cli`'s `control.rs`, the CLI's control-stream
   writer, which R0's rehearsal drives. Neither is touched until R0 closes (DEC-967 item 2);
   switching both to `BuildDigest::running()` is queued **after R0**. The long-running processes
   (`mandate-agentd`'s hosts, E21-30's runtime and executor) take it at start when they first
   journal, and E21-35's start-up check reads the same value.

### Rationale

The infrastructure design (§7.1 step 2) already chose static Linux binaries, `--locked`,
`SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, a double-build diff, an SBOM and cargo-auditable metadata; this records how.
Remapping paths and pinning the compiler are what keep two checkouts byte-identical: two local
builds of one commit from two checkout paths and two target directories gave identical
`SHA256SUMS` before this was written. Hashing the file a process runs, rather than embedding a
hash at build time, is the only build digest a binary can know about itself without a second
build, and it is the value a signature over the binary signs.

### Alternatives considered

- **glibc binaries on a distroless base:** the image then carries a libc to patch; musl static
  binaries run on an empty base (E21-35).
- **`cargo-cyclonedx` for the SBOM:** it writes one SBOM a workspace crate from the manifests,
  not one a shipped binary from what was linked; `cargo-sbom` likewise. Syft reading the
  auditable data describes exactly the artifact that ships.
- **One job building twice:** the same runner and the same disk would share whatever made the
  first build; two runners share nothing but the commit.
- **An embedded build ID as the digest:** a value compiled in cannot be the hash of the file that
  contains it, and would not match `SHA256SUMS` or the signature.
- **Switching the writers now:** R0's run and rehearsal journal through them this week; a change
  to every event's `actor.build` belongs after R0 closes.

<a id="dec-975"></a>

## DEC-975: The web app reads and commands through the workspace API behind one server-side data-source switch, with a provisional read contract until the read-model schemas land

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The `claude-code` builder of lane W2 (the demo lane of the managed-cells plan, DEC-967), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: every item is a reversible engineering choice inside `web/`, and each reading taken where the spec is silent is the one that adds no risk |
| **Story or claim** | E11-9, claim [#1443](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1443); supports E21-45; workspace API spec §3, §4.3, §4.5, §4.7, §4.9, §5.2, §5.4, §5.5, §6.3 |

### Decision

1. **One switch, on the server.** `web/src/lib/data-source.ts` reads `OWLHEAD_DATA_SOURCE`
   (`fixtures`, the default when unset or empty, or `api`), `OWLHEAD_API_ORIGIN` and
   `OWLHEAD_WORKSPACE_ID` from the server's environment when a request is served (`connection()`),
   never from a `NEXT_PUBLIC_` variable and never inlined at build (ES-12). Neither value is a
   secret: the browser reaches the API with its own session cookie (spec §3.3 item 5,
   `credentials: "include"`). The origin must be `https`, or `http` on a loopback host for
   development and the e2e suite, with no path, query or credentials.
2. **Fail closed.** Any other value of `OWLHEAD_DATA_SOURCE`, or an `api` choice whose origin or
   workspace does not parse, is `misconfigured`: the app shows the deployment unreachable and holds
   no data. It never falls back to the fixtures, so fixture figures never pass for a record (rule 3).
3. **Fixtures stay the default** for `npm run dev`, the unit and e2e suites, and the landing page's
   live demo: `/demo` builds its workspace from the fixtures directly and never reads the switch
   (DEC-906 item 4).
4. **The web app calls only documented operations** (DEC-967 item 13). `web/src/api/operations.ts`
   lists each read and command the app uses with the spec section that documents it;
   `contract.test.ts` fails an entry whose method and path the spec does not write as
   `` `METHOD /path` `` (its operation tables and §5's headings), a query member the
   spec does not name, a request made while loading the workspace and sending every command that no
   entry matches, a command body with a member its request schema does not allow or without one it
   requires, and a `/v1/` path written anywhere in `web/src` outside `src/api/` and the mock route.
   This is E21-45's check from the web side until the OpenAPI document is published.
5. **A provisional read contract, until `schemas/workspace-api/read-models/` lands.** The spec
   names each read's route (§4.7, §4.9) but not its body. Until the schemas do, each body is the
   envelope (§3.2, §6.1) plus the `web/src/fixtures/types.ts` shape §4.9 maps to it, as
   `web/src/api/mock-server.ts` serves it:

   | Route | Body members |
   |---|---|
   | `GET /health` | `served_at`, and `market_data`, `broker`, `deployment`, `relay`, each `{state, freshness: {observed_at, stale, age_seconds, limit_source}}` |
   | `GET /dashboard` | `decisions` (`GateDecision[]`), `external_positions` |
   | `GET /agents` | `agents: [{agent_id}]` |
   | `GET /agents/{id}` | `agent`: `Agent` without `positions`, `orders`, `past_orders`, `fills`, with the mandate in force inline |
   | `GET /agents/{id}/positions` | `positions` |
   | `GET /agents/{id}/orders` | `orders`, `past_orders`, `fills` |
   | `GET /agents/{id}/timeline` | `events` (`TimelineEvent[]`) |
   | `GET /approvals?state=pending\|resolved` | `approvals: [{approval_id}]` |
   | `GET /approvals/{id}` | `approval`: `Approval` with `content_hash` |
   | `GET /connections` | `connections`: `Connection` with `environment` |

   The decoders (`web/src/api/reads.ts`) refuse, as an unreadable answer the screens show as
   unreachable: a JSON number anywhere but a count member (a decimal sent as a float); a value a
   closed enum the screens branch on does not list (agent mode, startup, restriction code, order
   state and purpose, approval status, gate verdict and reason, timeline kind, health state,
   environment, broker); an approval without its `content_hash` (API-12); and a read without its
   watermarks (API-14); and a per-id read that answers for another id than the one asked. A request
   listed in both halves of the inbox is read once, and a component whose freshness says `stale` is
   stale whatever its state says. When the schemas land, their generated decoders replace these and this item
   is superseded. Lane A1's spec change ([#1446](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1446))
   defines the first of them (`read-models/agents`, `agent`, `approvals`, `approval`), thinner than
   these bodies; the next W2 slice decodes those, signs in through `POST /v1/sessions`, and shows
   `membership_unavailable` as its own state, and this item then covers only the reads the schemas
   do not yet define.
6. **What the app does not know, it reads at its conservative value.** Over the API the number of
   approvers is 0 and `independent_approval_required` is `null` (required, V-047) until a members and
   policy read is wired; the account's equity and price history are not drawn (the charts render
   nothing) and the sample headlines are hidden, since no route serves them and seeded bars beside a
   deployment's figures would read as a record; the "Fixture data" tags that mark the workspace's
   own figures are hidden, and the ones that mark sample content stay.
7. **Commands go through the API's idempotent write routes** (spec §5.2, §5.4): one key per
   gesture, the same key and body on every resend (API-4), at most four sends with backoff while the
   API does not answer, then `unknown`, never success and never "nothing happened" (API-13). A
   recorded command is followed through `GET /commands/{id}` to `applied`, `refused` or `ended`,
   and the workspace is read again (§6.3); an older read never overwrites a newer one. Commands are
   sent whatever the reads say (API-8): while the reads fail, a command is prepared from the last
   workspace they gave, so pause-all still reaches every agent last seen and stop-all its connection,
   though the screens show none of it. The
   mapping: pause, resume and stop to the agent's routes; release to stop with `release: true` and
   the SHA-256 of the warning lines shown as `warning_shown`; the kill switch at the agent's scope;
   pause-all as one pause per agent not stopped; stop-all and close-all as the kill switch at the
   connection's scope, or the workspace's when no connection has been read; an answer to
   `POST /approvals/{id}/responses` with the content hash the read gave.
8. **Step-up and records, until their routes exist.** `step_up` is `null` on every command: the
   browser's step-up ceremony (E9-4) and the artifact upload a `record` names are not built. The API
   forwards a missing step-up for the stream owner to refuse (§5.4), or answers an approve with
   `step_up_required` and records nothing (§5.2); the screen then says "Not recorded" and that
   nothing was approved. `record` is `null` wherever its schema allows; an answer's required
   `record` is the SHA-256 of the screen's lines as shown, with the web build named `owlhead-web/E11-9`.
9. **Not sent in this slice:** setting up an agent and changing a mandate (drafts, versions,
   confirmation, deploy; spec §4.1, §4.2). Over the API they send nothing and say so.
10. **The mock API at `/v1` is development and e2e only** (`web/src/app/v1/`): a production build
    answers 404 there, which `scripts/no-scenarios.mjs` checks after every build. The e2e suite
    `npm run test:e2e:api` runs the e2e build with the API as its data source against it, and runs
    against the API server instead once E21-31's routes are on `main`
    (`OWLHEAD_API_E2E_ORIGIN`, `OWLHEAD_API_E2E_WORKSPACE`).

### Rationale

The demo of 2026-10-26 shows the web app on a hosted cell's paper workspace (DEC-990; its item 3 puts the demo lanes on DEC-921's one-PR flow, tests first), and the
plan makes the web app one client of the public API (DEC-967 item 13). A switch on the server keeps
every test and the landing page on fixtures, puts nothing in the bundle, and lets one build serve
either. Failing closed is rule 3: an unreadable configuration or answer is shown as unreachable, not
as figures. The read bodies are a gap in the spec; pinning them to §4.9's own mapping changes no
screen contract, which is what §4.9 says replacing fixtures with calls should do, and the strict
decoders keep a mismatch from rendering as something it is not.

### Alternatives considered

- **A `NEXT_PUBLIC_` switch.** Inlined at build, so one build could not serve both, and the choice
  would sit in the bundle; refused by ES-12's rule for the bundle.
- **Reading through the Next server** (server components fetching the API and passing data down).
  The session cookie is host-only on the API's host (§3.3 item 5), so the app's server cannot hold
  it, and a server-side path into the cell is what DEC-967 item 13 refuses.
- **Falling back to fixtures when the API is misconfigured or down.** Shows fixture figures as a
  deployment's; refused (rule 3).
- **Waiting for the read-model schemas.** They are not scheduled before the demo; the provisional
  contract is superseded by them without a screen change.
- **Faking step-up in the browser.** A mock passkey's "verified" would be evidence of nothing;
  sending `null` lets the API and the stream owner refuse it where the spec says they do.

<a id="dec-976"></a>

## DEC-976: The local vault's entries, file checks, and import (E10-13 V1, DEC-692 part 2)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane V1 (`claude-code`), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: each item either tightens how DEC-692 holds a credential or picks an implementation DEC-692 left open; none adds a custody path, and the one item not built (journaling) is deferred, not dropped |
| **Story or claim** | E10-13 slice V1, claim #1441; DEC-692 items 4 to 7; managed-cells plan lane V1 (DEC-967) |

### Decision

1. **No new crate.** DEC-692's part 2 named `chacha20poly1305`, `getrandom` and `libc`. The
   cipher is `ring`'s `CHACHA20_POLY1305` and the nonce comes from `ring`'s `SystemRandom`;
   `ring` is already a workspace dependency with a row (`docs/dependencies.md`), so the vault
   adds no crate to the lockfile. `libc` is not needed (item 3).
2. **The entry format.** One file per entry: the tag `MVL\x01`, a random 12-byte nonce, and the
   ciphertext with its 16-byte tag. A secret is 1 to 8192 bytes. The associated data is the
   version tag `mandate-vault-local/v1\0`, then the kind (`code`, `token`, `client_secret`), the
   `key_ref`, and the binding (`connection` with its `connection_id` and environment, or
   `platform` for the client secret), each a two-byte big-endian length and its bytes. The file
   name is the `key_ref` in lowercase hex, a dot, and the kind. A `key_ref` is 1 to 100 of
   `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ - /`. Writes go to an `O_EXCL` temporary file in the same directory, set to
   their exact mode on the open file (so the umask cannot change it), synced, renamed over the
   entry, and the directory synced.
3. **The per-file check, tightened.** DEC-692 item 5 named `O_NOFOLLOW` and `fstat` for tokens
   and the client secret. The vault checks every read, codes included, before decrypting:
   - the name's `lstat` must be a regular file (a symlink, directory, FIFO, or device is refused
     and never opened);
   - the open file's `fstat` must be the same inode (device and inode number), so a name swapped
     between the check and the open is refused. This gives what `O_NOFOLLOW` gives with `std`
     alone, and the open flag's value differs by platform;
   - one link exactly, so a hard link planted beside an entry fails both names;
   - a code is owned by the API user, in the executor's group, `st_mode` exactly `0100640`; a
     token or the client secret is owned by the executor user, `st_mode` exactly `0100600`;
   - a size the format allows, checked before reading.

   Residuals, disclosed. The API user has group write on `tokens/` (DEC-692 item 1), so it can
   rename files there, though it can neither read nor forge one:
   - it could swap a FIFO in between the `lstat` and the open and stall that one read. It learns
     nothing and stores nothing;
   - it could move a token aside and later rename it back over a rotated one, so the executor
     reads the older token of the same connection. The associated data binds the name, not a
     generation, so the older file still decrypts. An older token is normally revoked at the
     broker once rotated, and Alpaca issues no refresh today (connections spec §5.4); the full
     vault (E21-32) has no group-writable directory, so this ends with it.
4. **Roles, tightened.** The API may write only codes and may unlink a code or a token, but not
   the client secret. The executor may not write a code, since only the API's files pass a code
   read. The connect core's `Vault` port answers a call the role does not have with
   `VaultUnavailable` and touches no file.
5. **The import.** `mandate vault import alpaca-oauth-client-secret` reads stdin only: one
   trailing `\n` or `\r\n` is dropped and the rest must be 1 to 8192 bytes of printable ASCII
   with no space. It reads the ids of `root` and both service users from `/etc/passwd` and
   `/etc/group`, opens the
   vault as the executor, stores the secret, and reads it back through the executor's checks; an
   import run as anyone but `owlhead_exec` fails that read, deletes its entry, and is refused.
   The write has already replaced any earlier client secret by then, so such a run leaves none:
   the fix is to run the import again as `owlhead_exec`, which `deploy/set-secrets.sh` does.
   It prints no part of the secret, whatever refuses it.
6. **Journaling deferred** (DEC-692 item 7). `VaultOperationRecorded` is not in the journal
   spec, and a journal event lands as a spec change first (ES-22). Until it does, the vault
   journals nothing; the event is follow-up work for E7-17's vault events, before the vault
   holds anything but the demo host's paper credentials.

### Rationale

Reusing `ring` keeps the supply chain as it is, and its AEAD is the one the TLS stack already
trusts. Checking codes as well as tokens costs nothing and closes the same planted-file attack
on `pending/`. The same-inode comparison is the portable form of `O_NOFOLLOW`. Reading the
import back is the one check that proves the file is the executor's, without a new crate to ask
the kernel who the process is.

### Alternatives considered

- **`chacha20poly1305` and `getrandom` as DEC-692 said.** Rejected: new crates for what `ring`
  already provides.
- **`O_NOFOLLOW` through `libc` or `rustix`'s `fs` feature.** Rejected: `libc` needs `unsafe`,
  which the workspace forbids, and the `fs` feature widens an approved row for no extra
  protection over the inode comparison.
- **Building `VaultOperationRecorded` in this slice.** Rejected for now: it is a journal spec
  change with its own vectors, and ES-22 puts it in its own PR before the code.

<a id="dec-977"></a>

## DEC-977: `SecretSource`, its local and OpenBao backends, per-process policies, leases, and manual unseal (E21-32)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted, except item 5's spend: auto-unseal (managed-cells plan H4) stays a founder decision and is not bought; until then the vault unseals by hand |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | Lane V2 (`claude-code`), under DEC-79 and DEC-176: each item is a reversible engineering choice inside DEC-434 item 14 and infrastructure §5.2; item 5 takes the option that costs nothing and adds no risk, and leaves the spend to the founder |
| **Story or claim** | E21-32, claim #1442; infrastructure §5.2, §5.4, §5.6; DEC-434 item 14; DEC-967 (lane V2); DEC-990 (D-5); builds on DEC-692 and DEC-976 |

### Decision

1. **The interface.** A new safety-critical crate, `mandate-vault`, defines `SecretSource`: a
   session with the vault as one identity. It writes a secret without reading it back, reads
   once (the token exchange, for its code and the client secret), deletes every version, leases
   an executor's token (`lease`, `renew`, `revoke`), and issues and revokes the single-use
   token-exchange grant. Every call is async, and the clock is an argument, never read.
2. **The policy table** (infrastructure §5.2), one function both backends obey:

   | Identity | Read | Write | Delete |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | API | — | every code | every code and token |
   | Connection manager | — | — | every code and token; issues and revokes grants |
   | Token exchange for `c` | `c`'s code, the client secret | `c`'s token | `c`'s code |
   | Executor for `c` | `c`'s token | — | — |
   | Runtime | — | — | — |
   | Secret import | — | the client secret | — |

   The API's token delete and the import identity keep DEC-692's local roles. The OpenBao
   policies are generated from this table with every path written out per connection. None holds
   `*` or `+`. A runtime's policy is a comment alone, because OpenBao refuses an empty one.
   `provision` rewrites them when a connection is added.
3. **The local backend** checks the table in code before it touches a file, so it refuses
   exactly what OpenBao refuses, and maps the local vault's refusals as follows: a failure to
   reach the files is an outage; a file that fails its checks or decryption is an integrity
   refusal. Its grants live in memory in the issuing process (`GrantBook`). On the demo host the
   manager and the exchange share the executor's process; DEC-692 item 6 enforces the grant in
   code there.
4. **The OpenBao backend.** It uses AppRole, one role per process type and connection, because
   AppRole works the same in the cell, in Compose, and on a customer's site. Kubernetes auth can
   replace the login later without changing the interface. A login is refused unless the role it
   reached is the claimed identity's own. Its secret id is a value the caller read from a mounted
   file, never an environment variable. The client:
   - speaks HTTPS only and trusts only the CA certificate it is given;
   - uses no proxy and follows no redirect, with a 10-second timeout;
   - stores each secret base64 in a KV v2 mount;
   - never puts a response body in an error.

   A grant is a single-use AppRole secret id for the connection's exchange role. The id and the
   token it yields each live 10 minutes. Revoking a grant already spent is a no-op. Only 400,
   401, 403 (refused), 404 (missing), and 503 (sealed) are the vault's own answers; any other
   status, a standby's redirect or a 429 included, is an outage, so it never drops a lease.
5. **Manual unseal by default.** The cell's OpenBao uses Shamir unseal, and an operator (the
   founder) unseals it after every restart. That costs nothing. Auto-unseal through a key service
   (H4, about $1 to $5 a month) remains the founder's decision. The client reads `sys/seal-status`
   before every login: a sealed or uninitialized vault is `Sealed`, an outage. So:
   - a running executor keeps its lease (item 7);
   - no new process starts until the vault is unsealed;
   - its agents stay `Recovering`, with protection at the broker (infrastructure §5.2).
6. **The dev server is for tests only.** A server with in-memory storage is refused. Only this
   crate's own unit-test build (`cfg(test)`) allows one, so no other crate's build, test or not,
   can turn it on; the conformance suites live in its unit tests for that reason. CI runs
   OpenBao 2.7.1, pinned by digest (D-5), in dev mode with TLS and a fresh random fake root token.
   That token goes only into a file whose path is in the environment. CI runs the suite as a new
   `cargo xtask ci openbao` part of `full` when the diff selects `mandate-vault` (DEC-1190), and in
   every `mutants` shard.
