template. It sets `MANDATE_WORKSPACE`, `MANDATE_PROCESS` (`runtime` or `executor`),
`MANDATE_SUBJECT` (the agent for a runtime, the account's `account_ref` for an executor) and
`MANDATE_GENERATION`. It passes ids only, never a secret, and does not know an agent's connection
or owner. `mandate-daemon run` took its whole deployment as flags.

### Decision

1. **The manager's template runs `mandate-daemon run --probe <socket>`.** The process takes its
   workspace from `MANDATE_WORKSPACE` and its agent from `MANDATE_SUBJECT`.
2. **A pod for another process is refused.** `MANDATE_PROCESS` other than `runtime` refuses
   `process_not_runtime`.
3. **The flags still work, and never override the manager.** Without `MANDATE_PROCESS`, the
   workspace and agent come from `--workspace` and `--agent`. With it, a flag that names a different
   workspace or agent refuses `deployment_conflict` rather than being guessed between (`AGENTS.md`
   rule 3), as does a manager variable set without `MANDATE_PROCESS`. A launch with no workspace or
   agent refuses `deployment_missing`. A value that is empty or has whitespace around it names
   nothing, and one that is not UTF-8 is read as present, so it is refused rather than taken as
   unset.
4. **`MANDATE_GENERATION` is the manager's alone.** The agent stream's writer epoch is what fences
   an earlier process (DEC-1012).
5. **The connection and the owner are read from the agent's deployment, not passed.** The
   connection is the mandate document's, the document the latest `AgentDeployed` of the agent names
   (journal spec §9.2: the record carries the version, never the connection). The owner the fixture
   wiring takes is the document's approvers and the version's author. Until the process reads them, a
   launch that names no `--connection` and `--owner` refuses `deployment_unresolved` before it is
   ready. That reading is the next change of lane P. The cell's static runtime Deployment (DEC-1013)
   keeps naming them by flag.

### Rationale

One source for the deployment: the journal and the documents it names. The manager stays a
starter of processes that passes ids, and cannot start a runtime against a connection the
deployment does not name.

### Alternatives considered

- **The manager passes `--connection` and `--owner`.** Its resolver would duplicate what the
  mandate document holds, and a stale copy could start a runtime against the wrong connection.

<a id="dec-1016"></a>

## DEC-1016: A runtime process reads its connection and approvers from its agent's mandate document

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: a reversible engineering decision under DEC-79 and DEC-176, scoped with the managed-cells coordinator; every reading it takes is the stricter one). Item 5's approvers amended by [DEC-2388](DEC-2388.md) item 11 |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (builder of lane P3 of the managed-cells plan, DEC-967, DEC-990) |
| **Story or claim** | E21-30 slice 1, E21-33 slice 2; journal spec §9.2; DEC-1013, DEC-1015 item 5 |

### Context

A pod the deployment manager starts names only its workspace and agent (DEC-1015). The rest of the
deployment is in the journal: the latest `AgentDeployed` of the agent names its mandate version,
the hash of the stored canonical document, and `AgentDeployed` never restates the connection
(journal spec §9.2). The document gives the connection and the approvers. The version's creator is
the user who journaled its `MandateVersionCreated`. Lane S provides the cell's shared artifact
store, read-only to the runtime, at `MANDATE_ARTIFACTS`.

### Decision

1. **A launch with neither `--connection` nor `--owner` resolves both.** It reads them from the
   workspace's control stream and the artifact store at `MANDATE_ARTIFACTS`. A launch naming only
   one of the two refuses `deployment_missing`. A fixture run naming neither cannot resolve,
   because its journal is in memory, and refuses `deployment_unresolved`, as does a launch with no
   store named.
2. **The store must already be one, and is only read.** The process opens it with
   `FsArtifactStore::open_existing`, which creates nothing, not even the temporary directory a
   writer needs. A root without its `sha256` directory refuses `artifact_store_unavailable`, and a
   read-only mount without `tmp/` still reads.
3. **The latest deployment decides.** The latest `AgentDeployed` or `AgentStopped` of the agent,
   among the control rows of the process's environment, decides. Stopped or never deployed refuses
   `agent_not_deployed`, and a redeployment runs again. A record of either type that cannot be read
   refuses `control_unreadable` rather than being skipped for an older one, so a stopped agent never
   starts on the deployment before its stop.
4. **The document must be whole and of the process's environment.** A document that is missing,
   does not re-hash to its version, or does not parse as a mandate refuses
   `mandate_document_unavailable`. One for another environment refuses
   `mandate_environment_mismatch` (`AGENTS.md` rule 8).
5. **What is taken.** *(Amended by [DEC-2388](DEC-2388.md) item 11: the approvers now resolve
   through `mandate_identity::approvers`, as the shell's and the executor's do, so a `role:`
   approver resolves among the workspace's members and a `user:` one only to a member; this
   item's reasoning that leaving roles unresolved "can only make an approval harder" no longer
   applies.)* The connection is the document's `connection_id`. The approvers are its
   `autonomy.approval.approvers` named as `user:`. A `role:` approver is not resolved against the
   workspace's members here, and an approver set without it can only make an approval harder to
   grant, or a quorum harder to meet. The deny-all wiring never asks anyway. The author is the user
   actor of the version's first `MandateVersionCreated` in the process's environment, so a later
   creation of the same document by another user never makes the true author independent of their
   own mandate. None, or a non-user actor, refuses `mandate_author_unknown`.
6. **The connection's own state is not checked here.** Whether the connection is established,
   revoked, or suspended reaches the runtime through its account stream (DEC-1014), and every
   order still meets the executor's gate (`AGENTS.md` rule 1).
7. **The resolution is read once, at the start.** A redeployment to a new version reaches a
   runtime only when a new process starts.

### Rationale

The journal and the documents it names stay the one source of a deployment. The manager passes
ids, and a runtime cannot start against a connection its deployment does not name. Resolving roles
against membership, and the rest of the production `MandateView`, belong to the post-R0 wiring.

### Alternatives considered

- **Treat a `role:` approver as its members now.** That needs the identity fold of the workspace's
  membership, which is the production view's (P5).
- **Create the store if it is missing.** A wrong root would then start a runtime on an empty
  store, which hides the misconfiguration.

<a id="dec-1025"></a>

## DEC-1025: The local cell runs the stub issuer as a loopback sidecar of the API pod, and links one demo owner

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: a reversible engineering decision under DEC-79 and DEC-176. It changes only the local cell, runs nothing in a hosted deployment, and weakens no safety rule). Item 1's second bullet and item 3's third check superseded by [DEC-2005](infrastructure.md#dec-2005): the tier is `deploymentTier` |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | `claude-code` (lane A1, claim [#1444](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1444)), at the coordinator's direction, with lane SI on the stub's interface |
| **Story or claim** | E21-52's cell wiring and E21-54's sign-in step; [DEC-2100](DEC-2100.md) items 2, 3 and 9; DEC-1024; [DEC-1023](DEC-1023.md); workspace API spec §3.11 items 2, 5 and 7 |

### Context

The cell's API server (DEC-1024) refuses every sign-in, because the cell has no
issuer and no subject links. [DEC-2100](DEC-2100.md) gives a dev or CI cell a stub issuer:
- it runs on loopback with a key made at each start;
- it is compiled only with debug assertions;
- a `dev` or `ci` cell runs it once E21-53 is on `main`.

This decision settles how the cell runs the stub and links a demo owner.

### Decision

1. **A native sidecar of the API pod, on its loopback.**
   - The stub runs as an init container with `restartPolicy: Always`, as `--bind 127.0.0.1:8090`, in the server's own network namespace. No other pod, node or host reaches it.
   - The tier it is given is the server's `api.tier`.
   - The API server's settings name it exactly: issuer `http://127.0.0.1:8090` and key set `http://127.0.0.1:8090/jwks.json`.
2. **The server starts only once the stub answers.** A second init container waits, up to a minute, until `127.0.0.1:8090` accepts a connection, and otherwise fails.
   - The server fetches the stub's key set at its own start and only every five minutes after. A server started first would refuse every sign-in until its next fetch.
   - The stub's image has no shell, so the wait runs in the postgres image, as the egress gate already does.
3. **It cannot run in a hosted deployment.** Three separate checks enforce this, each shown by a test:
   - The schema allows `images.stub-issuer` only as a local `mandate.local/` build.
   - The chart allows a local build only under `localImages`, which only the local cell's values set.
   - The chart refuses the stub when `api.tier` is not `dev` or `ci`.

   E21-53 also makes the server itself refuse the stub's `http` key set outside `dev` and `ci`.
4. **It runs only when both pieces are in the checkout.** `cargo xtask cell up` builds, imports and names the stub, and mounts subject links, only when two things hold:
   - lane SI's crate (`crates/mandate-stub-issuer`) is in the workspace, with its entry point
     implemented: its tests land first with `main` a `todo!()` (DEC-137), which would crash-loop
     the sidecar and keep the server from starting;
   - the server carries E21-53's check, as DEC-2100 item 9 requires.

   Otherwise the cell runs no stub and mounts no links, every sign-in is refused, and `cell demo` names the sign-in step as skipped. The stub is built in the dev profile, the only one that compiles it.
5. **One demo owner, linked by the CLI's own gesture.**
   - `cell up` runs `mandate identity link --issuer http://127.0.0.1:8090 --member 01KDEM0000000000000000000P` in the image it built, with no network, giving it the subject `demo-owner` on standard input.
   - The resulting file becomes the ConfigMap `subject-links`, which the chart mounts at the fixed path ([DEC-1023](DEC-1023.md)).
   - The subject names a demo principal, never a person.
6. **The demo signs in as `web/` does.** `cell demo` forwards local port 8090 to the stub alongside the API, so the stub sees the `Host` it requires.
   - It asks the stub for a token for `demo-owner`, then sends `POST /v1/sessions` from the app origin with `X-Mandate-Request: 1`.
   - It requires 204 and the `__Host-mandate_session` cookie.
   - This checks sign-in only. What the session may read waits on the founding membership (lane ID1).

### Rationale

- **Item 1:** a loopback sidecar is what DEC-2100 item 3 asks for, a stub reachable only inside the server's network namespace. It adds no Service, no ingress and no policy.
- **Item 2:** the wait turns a race the server would lose silently, a five-minute sign-in outage, into an ordered start.
- **Item 4:** gating on the checkout lets this wiring land before lane SI's code, and lets the cell switch sign-in on by itself the run after that code merges. It never runs the stub before E21-53.

### Alternatives considered

- **A separate stub pod with a Service.** It would put the stub on the cluster network, and DEC-2100 keeps it on loopback.
- **A startup probe on the stub.** Kubernetes probes reach the pod's address, not its loopback, and the stub binds loopback only.
- **Computing the link digest in xtask.** It would duplicate the canonical encoding. Running the CLI's own gesture keeps one implementation.

<a id="dec-1040"></a>

## DEC-1040: The local cell on a laptop, and the restart and kill-switch drills (C1)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering choices under DEC-79 and DEC-176; none loosens a safety rule) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | the builder of lane C1 (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E21-37; claim #1432; [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md) lane C; DEC-990, DEC-993 |

### Decision

1. **No cross toolchain.** Only a linux/x86_64 host can link the cell's static `mandate` binary
   (DEC-993 item 9). Building it on macOS arm64 would need a cross linker or a Rust builder image,
   and neither is among DEC-990's seven dependency kinds, so the coordinator chose to add none.
2. **Another host pulls a pinned image.** On any other host, `cargo xtask cell up` and `cell demo`
   take the `mandate` image from `MANDATE_CELL_IMAGE`, which must name a 64-digit SHA-256 digest in
   the form the workspace chart's schema takes: lane R2's signed multi-arch image (E21-35). The
   cell checks the digest, not the signature; checking R2's signature joins with R2. They skip the build and the import, and k3d pulls
   the image. Without the variable they stop and say that R2 has not published it yet. A pinned
   image wins on linux/x86_64 too, so R2's image can be proven in CI before a laptop needs it. A
   tag without a digest is refused.
3. **darwin-arm64 tool pins.** k3d 5.9.0, helm 4.3.0 and kubeconform 0.8.0 for darwin-arm64 are
   pinned by SHA-256 in `deploy/cell/README.md`, from the same publishers as the Linux pins. They
   are not downloaded by `.cursor/install.sh`, which installs x86_64 Linux builds only.
4. **The cgroup v1 kubelet argument is Linux-only.** On macOS, Docker Desktop's VM runs cgroup v2,
   and the host's `/sys/fs/cgroup` says nothing about it.
5. **Restart drill.** A control stream is written; the journal's CloudNativePG primary pod is
   deleted; once the pod and the cluster are Ready again, the stream exports and verifies by
   journal spec §11. Seeded, it verifies a stream id the restart never held, which must fail as
   `export_stream_empty`, so a drill that verified nothing cannot read as held. That seed proves the
   drill sees a lost stream; it does not plant a loss caused by the restart itself, which would
   need deleting the cluster's volume, and the restore drill (OPS-8) covers that case.
6. **A drill can be skipped, by name.** `kill-switch` (OPS-4) reports `skipped` with the lanes it
   waits on (P1's runtime and executor images, O4's `mandate agent kill`), never `held`, until
   those are on `main`.

### Alternatives considered

- `cross` or `cargo-zigbuild`, or a Rust builder image (`rust:…`) to build inside docker: a new
  dependency kind, outside D-1 to D-7.
- Running the cell on macOS through an x86_64 k3s image under emulation: needs the same x86_64
  binary, which cannot be built there.
- Faking the kill-switch drill with a mock process: it would read as held while proving nothing.

<a id="dec-1050"></a>

## DEC-1050: The journal's Postgres in the cell: a synchronous standby that commits wait for, WAL and base backups through the Barman Cloud plugin, and narrowed egress

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176: reversible engineering choices, each of which only tightens the journal's chart; no spend, no live money, no safety rule weakened |
| **Story or claim** | E21-41 (lane S2 of the [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md)), claim [#1435](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1435); its first slice. The S lane's block is DEC-1050 to DEC-1059 |

### Decision

1. **A primary and a synchronous standby, and every commit waits for the standby.** The
   journal-db chart's Cluster has at least two instances (the values schema refuses one),
   `synchronous_commit = on`, and CloudNativePG's
   `synchronous: {method: any, number: 1, dataDurability: required}`. `required` keeps the standby
   in `synchronous_standby_names` while it is down, so a commit waits for it rather than commits on
   the primary alone. These are written in the template, not values, since a value could only
   weaken them.
   - **The reading of "appends wait and return `Unavailable`"** (E21-41's criterion). With the
     standby down, Postgres has flushed the commit locally and holds it until a standby confirms
     it, and only then makes it visible. An append whose writer gives up while it waits therefore
     cannot be reported `Unavailable`, which `mandate-journal-pg` defines as "nothing was
     written": the commit may still land when the standby returns. So the criterion is read as
     "never `Committed`, and never visible", with `Ambiguous` for an abandoned commit, as the
     crate's contract already says: re-query by `event_id` before acting (journal spec §5.3;
     infrastructure §2.1). This only tightens the criterion, under DEC-176. An append refused
     *before* its commit because no synchronous standby is streaming, so that `Unavailable` is
     returned with nothing written, is a later slice in `mandate-journal-pg`.
2. **WAL and base backups through CloudNativePG's Barman Cloud plugin**, not the operator's
   in-tree `barmanObjectStore`. The in-tree path has been deprecated since CloudNativePG 1.26 and
   needs the `system` Postgres images, which are deprecated too; the plugin works with the
   `minimal` image the cell already pins. The plugin's chart `plugin-barman-cloud` 0.8.1 comes from
   its GitHub release, checked by SHA-256, and its two images (`plugin-barman-cloud` and its
   sidecar, v0.15.1) are pinned by digest (D-5, DEC-990 item 1). The chart renders an
   `ObjectStore` (`journal-archive`), names it as the Cluster's WAL archiver, and schedules a
   daily base backup through the same plugin, with a retention policy. WAL is compressed with gzip.
   - **A key of the archive's own.** The ObjectStore reads its access key from a Secret
     (`archive.keyFrom`, `journal-archive-key` by default) that holds only that key; no database
     role's credential is ever the archive's, and a chart test refuses one that is.
   - **Not journal spec §6.2's store.** The WAL archive and base backups are not write-once
     objects (DEC-973 item 6), so the lock check of E21-40 does not gate them; they go to the S1
     endpoint (R2 for cell #1). Nothing that §6.2 lists is written there.
3. **The plugin's TLS without cert-manager.** The operator and the plugin authenticate each other
   with two certificates the plugin's own chart would have cert-manager issue from a self-signed
   Issuer. A small chart of ours, `deploy/cell/charts/backup-plugin-tls`, makes the same two
   self-signed certificates in the cluster (`genSelfSignedCert`): the server's for the
   `barman-cloud` Service, the client's for the operator, each its own CA. They are valid for 365
   days, kept across upgrades, and remade by an install or upgrade within 30 days of their end;
   `cargo xtask cell up` then restarts the plugin, which reads them only at start. No certificate
   or key is ever a value (DEC-822 item 4).
4. **The journal's egress, narrowed.** The journal's pods reach only the cluster DNS, the
   Kubernetes API (which the instance manager and the plugin's sidecar call), each other on 5432
   and 8000, and the archive's endpoint. The API's addresses are `/32`s read at install from the
   `kubernetes` Service and its EndpointSlice, since a policy controller may see either; a
   rendering with no cluster must name them, and fails rather than opening the port to all. The
   archive is reached either by CIDRs of /24 or narrower on one port, or, for a store in the
   cluster, by its namespace and pod labels, exactly one of the two. A plain-HTTP endpoint is
   accepted only for a Service in the cluster.
5. **The local cell's stand-in for the S1 endpoint** is the MinIO image the object store's
   conformance suite already pins (`docs/dependencies.md`, test only), as its own chart,
   `deploy/cell/charts/archive-store`, in its own namespace: one bucket on an `emptyDir`, a key made
   in the cluster and handed to the workspace's namespace as `journal-archive-key`, ingress only
   from the journal's archive clients (the label the Cluster gives its pods), and no egress at all.
   It is the local and CI cell's only; cell #1 and a managed cluster archive to the S1 endpoint.
   Disclosed: in the local cell the archive's traffic is plain HTTP inside the cluster.
6. **Failure domains, said in the Cluster.** `failureDomain: separate` (the default) makes "each
   instance on its own node" a scheduling rule, so a standby with no other node stays pending
   rather than sharing one. `shared`, the local cell's, lets the instances share the one node, and
   the Cluster's `mandate.dev/failure-domain` annotation says the standby is then no second
   failure domain; `cargo xtask cell up` prints it. This is the "disclosed in the cell's own
   status, not hidden" of E21-41 for a one-node cell.
7. **Two drills, in a CI job of their own.** `standby-down` fences the standby, tries an append,
   and requires that it neither commits nor becomes visible on the primary and that its Job ends
   on its deadline; then it unfences the standby, requires a synchronous standby to stream again,
   a new append to commit, and every stream written to verify by journal spec §11. Seeded, the
   cluster's durability is set to `preferred` first, and the drill must report the commit that
   lands on the primary alone. `archive` writes a stream, closes its WAL segment, takes a base
   backup, and requires Postgres to report the segment archived and Barman's own tools, reading the
   store with the archive's key from a pod of their own, to list the backup and fetch the segment
   back. Seeded, the archive points at another bucket of the same store, which Postgres cannot
   tell, and the drill must report the segment missing. Both run in `cargo xtask ci cell-journal`,
   a job beside `cell`, and K1's `restart` drill, which with the standby is now a failover and the
   old primary's rejoin, moves to `cargo xtask ci cell-failover`, a third, so each stays inside ten
   minutes (DEC-464).
8. **A former primary can rejoin.** K1's `roles.sql` revokes `CONNECT` on the `postgres`
   database from PUBLIC, and when a former primary rejoins after a failover CloudNativePG connects
   there as its replication role, `streaming_replica` (the `restart` drill showed it refused).
   The Cluster's bootstrap (`postInitSQL`) creates that role `WITH LOGIN REPLICATION` and grants it
   `CONNECT` on `postgres`, one database and nothing else, with no password: the operator
   authenticates it by client certificate. The role is made in the bootstrap because the operator
   creates it only when the primary first starts, after the bootstrap, and takes one that exists
   (`configureStreamingReplicaUser` ensures `LOGIN REPLICATION`); it grants the `pg_rewind`
   function privileges on every primary start either way (`configurePgRewindPrivileges`), so the
   role has exactly what the operator would give it, plus that one `CONNECT`. The bootstrap SQL
   holds no `$`, which Kubernetes expands in the init Job's arguments; a chart test refuses one.
9. **What the next slices of E21-41 add**, the story staying open until they land: the
   point-in-time restore drill that passes journal spec §11 over every stream (OPS-8); the failover
   drill with intents in flight, zero duplicates and zero lost acknowledged appends, and the old
   primary fenced before promotion (DEC-434 item 15); cell #1's values, with the instances on
   different droplets and DEC-995 item 6's one-region disclosure, and the archive's R2 endpoint and
   CIDRs; and the writer-side `Unavailable` of item 1.

### Rationale

OPS-2's "durable" is `synchronous_commit = on` with a synchronous standby, and the failure that
matters is the quiet one: a standby that drops out and a primary that keeps committing alone. Only
`dataDurability: required` keeps that from happening, at the cost of appends waiting, which is the
safe default rule 3 asks for: appends fail, so no order is sent, and protection rests at the broker
(infrastructure §2.1, the primary-failure row). The in-tree archive is on its way out of the
operator, and building the cell on it would mean a migration with the archive's history at stake.
cert-manager would bring a cluster-wide controller and three more images to issue two certificates
that never leave the cluster. Narrowing the journal's egress closes the last `- {}` the workspace
chart's default-deny left open.

### Alternatives considered

- **`dataDurability: preferred`.** Refused: it drops a down standby from the synchronous set, so
  the primary commits alone, which is what OPS-2 forbids; the `standby-down` drill plants it as its
  bug.
- **The in-tree `barmanObjectStore` with the `system` image.** Refused: deprecated, as is the image
  it needs.
- **cert-manager for the plugin's certificates.** Refused for now: more surface than two
  self-signed certificates; a later cell that runs cert-manager for other reasons can switch the
  plugin's chart back to it with values.
- **Archive egress to `0.0.0.0/0` on 443.** Refused: it lets a compromised database pod reach any
  host on the internet.
- **A required anti-affinity everywhere.** Refused for the local cell: its one node could never
  schedule the standby. `shared` is allowed only with the disclosure.

<a id="dec-1060"></a>

## DEC-1060: Release images for amd64 and arm64, each built natively on its own runners, under one signed multi-arch index

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The lane R2 builder (`claude-code`), as a reversible engineering choice under DEC-79 and DEC-176, on the coordinator's request (the founder's laptop is Apple Silicon, and the C lane's `cell demo` pulls by digest on non-x86 hosts). The first number of lanes R1 and R2's block (DEC-1060 to DEC-1069) |
| **Story or claim** | E21-35 slice 2 (lane R2), claim [#1439](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1439); builds on [DEC-972](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-972) and [DEC-971](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-971) |

### Decision

1. **Two arches, each native.** A release is built for `linux/amd64` on `ubuntu-24.04` runners
   and for `linux/arm64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runners, by the same `cargo xtask release build`,
   which builds only for the arch it runs on (`x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`,
   `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl`). No cross toolchain, no emulation: each arch's build is
   DEC-972's reproducible path, with each tool's arm64 asset pinned by SHA-256 beside its amd64
   one at the same version.
2. **Each arch proves itself.** Each arch is built twice, on two runners of its own arch in two
   checkout paths, and `compare` fails unless each pair matches. Each arch's `build-a` builds
   its test-signed images and runs DEC-971's start-up drill natively.
3. **Publishing.** On a push to `main`, `publish-amd64` and `publish-arm64` each rebuild on
   their own arch, require their `build-a`'s digest, and push their images as
   `main-<commit>-<arch>`, each signed keyless and with its SBOM attested. Then `publish-index`
   reads each arch's image back from GHCR by the registry's own digest and size, pushes the OCI
   image index over them as `main-<commit>`, checks the registry reports the index's own digest,
   and signs that digest. A cell pulls `…/<binary>@sha256:<index>` on either arch.
4. **Nothing else changes.** The image layout, the start-up check and the signing keys are
   DEC-971's; an arch changes only the image config's `architecture`, so a binary's layers are
   the same bytes the arch's build produced.

### Rationale

The coordinator asked for multi-arch images from the same reproducible path, built natively on
arm64 runners rather than with a new cross toolchain. Native builds need no new dependency, and
GitHub's arm64 runners are free for public repositories. Reading each arch's manifest back from
the registry, rather than passing digests between jobs, means the index lists only what the
registry actually holds, and the workflow passes nothing the closed-world reader (DEC-873)
cannot read.

### Alternatives considered

- **Cross-compiling arm64 on amd64** (a musl cross linker, or `cross`): a new toolchain to pin,
  and an arm64 binary nobody ran before it shipped.
- **QEMU emulation:** slow, and the drill would test the emulator.
- **Separate per-arch tags only, no index:** every consumer would have to pick the arch, and a
  digest would not be portable between the founder's laptop and the cell.

<a id="dec-1070"></a>

## DEC-1070: The §8.2 operator alerts as one closed set, raised from stored records and signals, with an opaque payload and no paging vendor

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176 (reversible engineering choices, each the reading that adds no risk) |
| **Story or claim** | E21-4, lane O2 of the [managed-cells plan](../tasks/managed-cells.md); claim #1440 |

### Decision

1. **A new pure, safety-critical crate, `mandate-alerts`, at layer 3.** It sits above
   `mandate-journal`, whose `StoredEvent` it reads, and `mandate-notify`, whose `NoticeId`,
   `SecureRandom` and canary scan it reuses. Its `forbidden_internal` list keeps it off every
   stream writer, the executor, the runtime, the connectors, the dispatcher and the CLI, so raising
   an alert can never append, act or trade. It is safety-critical because its payload leaves the
   cell, like a notification's (`AGENTS.md`, "Notification payloads").
2. **One `AlertKind` per row of infrastructure design §8.2**, with the row's severity and runbook.
   Three rows name two conditions of different weight, and each becomes two kinds: the kill switch
   (informational; SEV-1 when automated at workspace scope), a stale feed (SEV-3; SEV-2 at scale)
   and backups (a failed backup; a drill overdue or failed). That gives 28 kinds.
3. **Journal-sourced rows are read from the stored record's payload** through `mandate_canon`:
   - `ReconciliationRun` with `result` `mismatch`.
   - `ProtectionChanged` with `action` `interval_limit` or `watchdog`.
   - `GateDecided` with `reason_code` `protection_unattributed`.
   - `KillSwitchActivated`. It is SEV-1 when `scope` is `workspace` and `initiator` is anything
     but `owner`: a risk limit, the platform operator, or none named, which counts as automated.
   - `VerificationRun` with `result` `fail` or `incomplete`.
   - `AnchorComputed` whose `token` is null or absent.
   - `ClockToleranceExceeded`, whatever it holds: no schema is closed yet.
   - `ApprovalDelivered` with `status` `failed`.
   - `OwnerAlertSent` with `kind` `data_feed_down` or `spend_cap`.

   A body that does not parse, or holds no payload object, is an error rather than silence. So is
   a `ReconciliationRun`, `ProtectionChanged`, `VerificationRun`, `ApprovalDelivered` or
   `OwnerAlertSent` whose discriminating member is absent or not text, so a dropped member never
   silences an alert.
4. **Every other row is a `Signal`**, which carries only counts, durations and flags:
   - the global kill switch;
   - an order `Unknown` past its lookup window, which needs a clock the crate does not read;
   - a duplicate order, for which no record exists;
   - a stale feed;
   - gateway, journal-append, standby, fencing, DDL, cold-export, vault-lease and broker
     degradation;
   - a crash loop, an undelivered approval, and a backup or drill.

   Where §8.2 names a threshold it is used: a p99 append over 5 ms, and a cold-export lag over 60
   seconds. Where it names none ("above threshold"), any nonzero count alerts, and two or more
   stale feeds are "at scale". That is the
   conservative reading until E21-39's rules are tuned, and it changes no trading.
5. **The payload is the alert id, the reason code, the runbook id, the severity and fixed text.**
   Nothing else has a field. The id is minted from a `SecureRandom` source, as a notice id is
   (NT-4), so no event id or creation time reaches a pager. What raised the alert stays in the
   cell, keyed by the id.
6. **De-duplication is the caller's.** One cause can raise several alerts: a workspace kill
   switch is journaled on every stream it touches, and every opening held for an unattributed
   leg is its own gate decision. The cell's alert path (E21-39) groups them by its own rules, and
   nothing here drops one.
7. **No paging vendor** (DEC-434 item 20, H6 stays the founder's). A `Sink` trait takes the
   payload bytes. The only implementation is `CaptureSink`, which tests and a cell without a
   paging tool use. The runbook is an identifier (`RB-01` …), and the cell's configuration turns
   it into a link.

### Rationale

Design §8.2 makes journal events authoritative, so the rows they cover are pure functions of
stored records and replay raises them again. The other rows have producers that do not exist yet
(the deployment manager, the model gateway, the writer's metrics, the backup job). A closed
`Signal` gives each future producer an input that cannot carry trading content, and lets each row
fire in a fixture test today. Reusing `mandate-notify`'s id and canary keeps one opaque-payload
rule for owners and operators.

### Alternatives considered

- **Alerts inside `mandate-notify` or `mandate-dispatcher`:** the notice is the owner's closed
  set (NT-1) and the dispatcher writes the notice stream. Operator alerts are neither.
- **Deriving the alert id from the event id:** the same alert would get the same id on replay,
  but a ULID's creation time would reach the pager, which DEC-702 refuses for notices.
- **Waiting for each row's producer before adding its kind:** that leaves rows without a test,
  against E21-4's acceptance.

<a id="dec-1090"></a>

## DEC-1090: The Vercel configuration leaves the repository (supersedes DEC-1005 item 4)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, lane W1 builder), carrying out [DEC-823](earlier-600-899.md#dec-823) item 3 (the founder's: remove `vercel.json` and its test when the app moves to Workers). The founder's "Once you are done remove vercel.", relayed by the coordinator on 2026-10-17, asks for the same |
| **Story or claim** | E21-38, lane W1; [DEC-1005](DEC-1005.md) item 4; [DEC-465](earlier-303-599.md#dec-465) items 1 and 3 |

### Decision

1. `vercel.json` and its test (`web/src/lib/vercel-policy.test.ts`) are removed. `web/scripts/workers.mjs`
   now refuses any `vercel.json` at the repository root, so a second host's configuration cannot
   come back unnoticed.
2. This ends DEC-1005 item 4, which kept the file, still deploying `main` only, until the cutover.
   DEC-465 items 1 and 3 fall away, as DEC-823 item 2 said they would.
3. Nothing here touches DNS or the Vercel project. The coordinator moves `owlhead.ai`, `www` and
   `app` onto the Worker after its first deploy.

### Founder steps this leaves

Disconnect the Vercel project from the repository (Vercel → the project → Settings → Git →
Disconnect), or delete the project. Until then, with no `vercel.json`, Vercel builds and publishes a
preview of every pushed branch, and `owlhead.ai` and `www` keep serving Vercel's last production
deploy until their DNS moves. The previews hold the same public fixture app and no secret.

### Rationale

The app is hosted on Workers alone (DEC-823). A dormant `vercel.json` would let a reconnected
Vercel project serve a second copy, and the founder asked for Vercel to go.

### Alternatives considered

- **Keep `vercel.json` disabling every branch until the project is disconnected.** It has no
  preview window, but it leaves Vercel configuration in the tree, which the founder asked to remove.

<a id="dec-1091"></a>

## DEC-1091: The hosting guard admits `r2-lockcheck.yml`'s use of the `production` environment, pinned to its reviewed text (supersedes DEC-1005 item 2 in part)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, lane W1 builder), under DEC-176: it admits exactly the workflow `main` already carries (#1454, E21-40), and nothing more |
| **Story or claim** | E21-38, lane W1; [DEC-1005](DEC-1005.md) item 2; E21-40's `r2-lockcheck.yml` ([DEC-973](DEC-973.md)); ES-12 |

### Context

DEC-1005 item 2 says only the web deploy job names the `production` environment, and
`web/scripts/workers.mjs` refuses any other workflow that does, or that holds a `CLOUDFLARE_`
value. #1454 then added `.github/workflows/r2-lockcheck.yml`. It is dispatched from `main` only,
runs one job in `production`, and reads R2's key and the Cloudflare token in its one step. The
`web` checks run only on pull requests that touch `web/`, so #1454 never ran them, and the guard
has failed on `main` since.

### Decision

1. `PINNED_PRODUCTION_WORKFLOWS` in `web/scripts/workers.mjs` lists the other workflows that may
   use the `production` environment, each with the SHA-256 of the text a reviewer read. Today it
   lists only `r2-lockcheck.yml` (`fb00fb81…c2d8e`).
2. A listed workflow passes only byte for byte. Any edit, a comment included, fails until someone
   re-reads it and moves the pin in a reviewed change. The Cloudflare-command allowlist still
   applies to it, so it cannot deploy the Worker.
3. Every other workflow keeps DEC-1005 item 2's rule unchanged.

### Rationale

ES-12's property is that no pull-request job can reach the environment. The pinned file holds it
(dispatch only, `main` only), and the pin keeps it holding, because any change is a guard failure a
reviewer must clear. Parsing its shape would be a second, weaker copy of the review.

### Alternatives considered

- **Allow any workflow that runs only on `main`.** That is wider than what `main` carries, and it
  lets a new holder of the token in without a review of the guard.
- **Keep the rule and leave `main` red.** Every `web/` pull request would fail.

<a id="dec-1095"></a>

## DEC-1095: The web app opens and keeps its workspace API session one tab at a time, and shows a signed-out session and an unread membership as states of their own

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The `claude-code` builder of lane W2, under DEC-79 and DEC-176: reversible engineering choices inside `web/`, each the reading that adds no risk |
| **Story or claim** | E11-9, claim [#1443](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1443); builds on [DEC-975](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-975); workspace API spec §3.5 and §3.11 items 5 and 6 (lane A1, #1446) |

### Decision

1. **The session.** With sign-in on (DEC-211), the browser trades the sign-in client's access token
   for the API's own cookie with `POST /v1/sessions` before its first read
   (`web/src/api/session.ts`, `web/src/lib/api-session.ts`). It refreshes the cookie every four
   minutes, one minute inside the five-minute access lifetime, with `POST /v1/sessions/refresh` and
   a fresh token, or `null` when the sign-in provider does not answer, which the API reads as an
   outage that keeps only pause and the kill switch. A read answered 401 opens the session again
   once and reads again. `DELETE /v1/sessions/current` signs out. Only the API's one 401 or a 403
   counts as refused; no answer, or any other status (a deploy, a drain's `busy`, a 5xx), is
   unanswered. Either way the runtime tries to open the session again at its next read, and the
   keepalive keeps beating after a refresh that got no answer, so one failure never leaves the app
   signed out or the session unrefreshed. A session call that takes more than 10 seconds counts as
   unanswered, so a hung call never holds the lock.
2. **One tab at a time.** A refresh rotates the cookie's secret, and a rotated secret presented again
   ends the session (§3.11 item 6). So a refresh holds one exclusive Web Lock that every tab shares,
   reads hold the same lock shared and never run during a refresh, and commands take no lock and
   never wait (API-7). A `BroadcastChannel` carries only the time of the last refresh, so a tab
   that learns another refreshed within the last two minutes does not refresh again. A browser
   without Web Locks serializes within the tab.
3. **Nothing stored.** The token stays with the sign-in client; the keeper puts it only in a request
   body, never in browser storage or a URL.
4. **Signed out shows nothing.** No session can be opened (no token, or the API refuses it): every
   screen says "Sign in to see your workspace" with a link to sign in, and nothing is read.
5. **An unread membership is its own state.** A read answered 503 `membership_unavailable` (§3.5)
   shows "Your membership could not be read" and no data, not the deployment unreachable. Stop still
   opens and still sends its request; the API answers for itself whether it records it (DEC-1007
   item 8, Proposed).
6. **No session without sign-in.** With sign-in off (the fixtures, the e2e build's mock API at
   `/v1`), the runtime opens none.

### Rationale

Lane A1 relayed the session routes on 2026-10-17. The cookie is host-only on the API's host, so only
the browser can hold it (DEC-975 item 1). Serializing refreshes is what keeps one owner with several
tabs from ending their own session by reuse; letting commands skip the lock keeps Stop from waiting
on a slow refresh. Showing the two new answers as their own states keeps rule 3: neither is shown as
data, and neither is mistaken for a deployment that is down.

### Alternatives considered

- **Refreshing in every tab on its own timer.** Two tabs would present the same rotated secret and
  end the session.
- **Holding commands behind the lock too.** A refresh that hangs would hold Stop; refused (API-7).
- **Keeping the token or the refresh time in `localStorage` to share it.** Puts a credential in
  browser storage (brief §5, rule 6 row); the lock and a channel that carries only a time do the job.

<a id="dec-1096"></a>

## DEC-1096: Over the workspace API the web app reads the read models as served, and shows its own five screens, with "not reported" for what the API does not serve

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The `claude-code` builder of lane W2, under DEC-79 and DEC-176, on the coordinator's direction of 2026-10-17 ("show 'not reported' for members the API doesn't serve, never invent them") |
| **Story or claim** | E11-9, claim [#1443](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1443); supersedes [DEC-975](earlier-900-1999.md#dec-975) item 5 for agents and approvals; the read models of lane A1's #1446 and #1499 (DEC-979, DEC-1020, DEC-1021) |

### Decision

1. **The read models as served.** `web/src/api/read-models.ts` decodes `read-models/agents`, `agent`,
   `approvals` and `approval` exactly as their schemas state them: every required member, decimals
   as canonical strings (a JSON number is refused), the closed enums (mode, environment, approval
   state, side, purpose, time in force), the label's shape, and the content hash an answer binds
   (API-12). Each per-id read must answer for the id asked. DEC-975 item 5's fixture-shaped bodies
   for agents and approvals, and its dashboard, positions, orders and timeline reads, are dropped.
2. **The reads the schemas do not define yet are optional.** `GET /health` and `GET /connections`
   keep DEC-975 item 5's bodies; a 404 is "not reported" (each health component reads stale with no
   time, and no connection is known, so stop-all falls back to the workspace scope), never the
   deployment down.
3. **Nothing is invented.** A member the API serves as `null`, or does not serve, shows as "not
   reported": an agent's name, mode, mandate or account positions (`account_positions`, the
   account's holdings at its last reconciliation, both agents' on a shared account), a request's time in force or protection,
   and limit usage, which no read serves until E6-4 journals it. No figure is computed in a missing
   member's place, and no P&L is shown, since the API serves none.
4. **Five screens of their own.** Over the API, Home, the agents, one agent, the approvals and one
   request are `web/src/components/api-screens/`: they read the runtime's `view` and nothing from
   the fixtures. Each page picks its screen by the data source (`BySource`); `/demo` and the
   fixtures keep the fixture screens unchanged.
5. **Every other screen says it is not available yet.** Over the API, a screen not built on the read
   models shows "Not available from your deployment yet" and nothing else (`WorkspaceGate`), until
   its read models are served.
6. **A deadline is a time.** `ApprovalRequested.deadline`, integer risk-clock seconds, is shown as
   those seconds read as Unix time in UTC, as lane A1 confirmed: the scheduler's `ClockAdvanced`
   time, monotone, which may lag the wall clock by up to a tick.
7. **No environment and no feed time is assumed.** The environment shown is the connection's, or the
   one every agent read names; otherwise, and before any read answers, it is "unknown", and the
   badge says "Environment not reported", so a live account is never labelled paper. A kill switch
   sent then carries `environment_shown: null`. A health feed the API does not serve has no time
   (`as_of: null`) and reads "not reported" on the status strip, the alerts and the unreachable
   notice; no time from the device's clock stands in for it.
8. **Commands keep their routes.** An answer binds the content hash the approval read gave; pause-all
   pauses every agent the last read listed as not stopped; stop-all uses the connection the last read
   named.

### Rationale

The coordinator asked for the read models to replace DEC-975's provisional bodies and for nothing
to be filled in. The fixture screens compute headroom, P&L and charts from members the API does not
serve; showing them over the API would mean inventing those members or showing empty figures as if
they were zero. Screens of their own, as narrow as the read models, keep rule 3 and are what the
demo shows; each new read model lets a fixture screen move across.

### Alternatives considered

- **Mapping the read models into the fixture types.** The missing members (headroom, P&L, orders,
  fills) would have to be invented or zeroed; refused.
- **Making every fixture screen tolerate missing members.** A change across dozens of screens before
  the demo, for members that arrive one read model at a time.
- **Hiding the screens the API does not serve.** The navigation would change by data source; a plain
  notice keeps the app's shape and says why.

<a id="dec-1120"></a>

## DEC-1120: The research loop calls through mandate-gateway, the call record carries the verdicts, and the recorded fake is the default

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent; reversible engineering readings under DEC-79 and DEC-176, each of which tightens or closes a gap by the reading that adds no risk) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, intelligence lane I2), under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E19-3, E15-6's caller side; [claim #1515](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1515); supersedes DEC-996 items 4, 5, 7 and 9 in part |

### Context

DEC-996 built the research loop against its own `ModelGateway` trait and a recorded fake, with
provisional `ModelInvocationRecorded` and `CandidateVerdictsRecorded` drafts, because the gateway
and the closed agent-stream record did not exist. Both now do. `mandate-gateway` (#1484, DEC-997,
DEC-998) is the one component that calls a model, and journal spec v0.46 §9.19 closes the
agent-stream `ModelInvocationRecorded`, with the candidates' verdicts as its `verdicts` member
(rule 151). The coordinator asked for the loop to call through the gateway's contract, with the
recorded fake as the default in tests and CI, and for a config path that uses OpenRouter when a
vaulted key exists and the recorded fake otherwise, saying which. `main` holds neither the vault's
`SecretSource` (E21-32, lane V2, Proposed) nor a hosted HTTPS `Transport` for the gateway.

### Decision

1. **The call contract is the gateway's.** `mandate-research-run`'s `ModelGateway`, `GatewayReply`,
   `CallFailure`, `ErrorKind`, and `settle` are removed. The worker builds the call as before, at the
   gateway's own clock, and sends `ResearchCall::request()`, a `mandate_gateway::CallRequest` with
   purpose `research`, key owner `platform`, the journaled observations as its retrieved context, and
   three typed inputs (`context`, `items`, `memory`), each canonical JSON. The gateway renders them
   into the pinned template's data sections, guards the prompt, reserves the cost, calls, and
   validates the output; the answer is the gateway's `CallRecord`, or a `RequestError` for a request
   it cannot make at all. `ModelPin` is the gateway's `ModelRef`. §7.3's treatments are over the
   gateway's `Outcome`.
2. **The research entry is pinned content.** `ResearchEntry` builds the registry entry: the system
   text (opinions only, data sections untrusted), the user text with the three slots, the retrieval
   plan, §6.5's output schema in the gateway's schema language, the output bounds, the deadline, the
   token bound, and the seed, all inside the content hash.
3. **Revisions fail the pinned schema.** The gateway's schema language has no optional member, so the
   pinned schema leaves `predecessor_thesis_id` out, and an output that names a predecessor is
   `schema_invalid`, with no output at all. While §8.6 item 5 allows no revision, this refuses more
   than the harness's per-candidate `revisions_not_enabled`, which stays for the day revisions are
   allowed and the language can state an optional member.
4. **What the gateway's schema cannot state, the harness's strict parse still refuses.** Ranges of
   decimal strings (conviction, confidence), blank or control-character text, and byte lengths are
   not in the schema language. An `ok` output the parse refuses, or one whose candidates the
   platform cannot fill or admission cannot decide, is judged as nothing: the call record is
   journaled with no verdicts (rule 151 permits an empty list), and no thesis record or output
   follows. **Disclosed residual:** such a call is recorded with outcome `ok` and no reason; HI-22's
   "every candidate of a judged run has a verdict" holds because the run is not judged, and the
   inference spec's schema language gaining decimal ranges and an optional member (E15-12) closes
   it.
5. **The backend selection.** `research_gateway` builds the gateway with the OpenRouter adapter
   always, and chooses its transport and key source by `select`: OpenRouter, through the cell's
   hosted transport and the vault's key, only when the vault holds the upstream's key **and** a
   hosted transport is supplied; otherwise the recorded fake, with a fixed placeholder key source so
   a hosted endpoint's call reaches the fake rather than ending `credential_invalid`. The registry,
   the routing lock, the guard, the meter and its $50 cap, and the rate caps are the same either
   way, so a recorded run shows what a hosted run would send, and the journal is identical
   (`the_demo_runs_either_way_and_says_which_gateway_it_used`). `Selection::describe` says which
   transport and why, and never names the key. A process with no hosted transport passes
   `NoHostedTransport`, an uninhabited type, so it can only select the fake. Until E21-32's vault and
   a hosted HTTPS transport land, every deployment selects the fake; `cargo xtask cell demo` says
   so. No test or CI job holds a real key or reaches a model provider (DEC-997 item 4).
6. **The call record heads the judging batch.** Rule 151 puts the verdicts in the call's own
   `ModelInvocationRecorded`, so §6.2 steps 4 and 5 are one step: an `ok` record of the run in
   flight, completed by its deadline, under the pin research still runs, is judged when it arrives,
   and the batch is the call record with its verdicts, then each judged thesis's record (caused by
   the call record) and its output. The append is atomic, and the call record precedes every record
   its output leads to (INF-10). DEC-996 item 5's wait for the record to fold back, its
   `not_pinned` verdicts, and the provisional `CandidateVerdictsRecorded` are gone: an answer under
   a pin research no longer runs, or after its deadline, is recorded with no verdicts. Verdicts use
   rule 151's results: a harness refusal's own code, `judged` for a thesis handed to admission (its
   thesis record carries admission's result), and for an invalidation `unknown_thesis` or `judged`.
   An invalidation citing evidence the run did not retrieve is `judged`, since rule 151 gives
   `unknown_evidence` to theses only; its evidence is E19-5's verdict to check, and §6.2's
   preconditions keep every run from reaching one meanwhile.
7. **The core re-checks the record, and refuses another call's.** A record whose call id, pin,
   purpose, agent, or retrieved context differs from the run's planned call is refused
   `not_interpreted` and never journaled as this run's; the run waits for its own call's record or
   its bound. A cancelled run's call is remembered until its record arrives, as before, and only that
   call's record is accepted for it. A record completed after the run's own wall-clock bound is never
   judged, whatever deadline it carries (§7.1). A judgment the core cannot write (a value it built
   that fails its own canonical form) is no judgment: the record is still journaled, with no
   verdicts, so a paid call is never left unrecorded and the schedule still counts from it (HI-8).
   A `RequestError` ends the run with no record and disables research for the pin until a new
   version, since the same request would be refused again. **Disclosed residual:** that refusal
   raises no operator alert, because a notice must name a journaled event and a refused request
   has none; the run-path hook after R0 (item 9) surfaces it in the shell's own health signal.
8. **The schedule counts from completion.** The last call's instant is the record's `completed_at`,
   which §9.19 carries, both when the record is handled and when it folds back, so a restart folds
   back the anchor the live process used. `called_at` and `used` are gone with the provisional
   record.
9. **The run id's correlation is a hook.** §9.19 carries the run id in the envelope's
   `correlation_id`, which a draft cannot set; the call id identifies the run's one call meanwhile.
   Setting it joins DEC-996 item 2's run-path hook, after R0.
10. **Preconditions unchanged.** `mandate-journal` does not yet register the agent-stream
    `ModelInvocationRecorded` (DEC-998's code half) or the invalidation verdict (E19-5), so
    `JournalCatalog` still registers neither and no run starts in any deployment.

### Rationale

The gateway already enforces the pin, the routing lock, the deadline, the meter, and the identity
check; calling through it removes the loop's own copy of those checks and makes the recorded fake
the same code path a hosted call takes. Every reading the swap forced is taken toward refusing more:
a revision fails the schema, an unreadable output judges nothing, a record of another call is
refused, and the hosted transport is unreachable without both a vaulted key and a transport.

### Alternatives considered

- **Add a reqwest HTTPS transport crate in this change:** it would make the OpenRouter branch
  reachable, but its network code cannot be mutation-tested without a local TLS server, and
  `.cargo/mutants.toml` exclusions are the founder's; it belongs with E21-32's vault, which
  supplies the key it needs. Offered to the coordinator as option B.
- **Keep `CandidateVerdictsRecorded` beside the closed record:** two records of one judgment, and
  rule 151 already holds the verdicts.
- **Journal the call record first and judge on the next poll:** splits the verdicts from their
  record, which §9.19 does not allow.
- **Accept a record of another call and rewrite its outcome to `identity_mismatch`:** journals a
  record the gateway never wrote.

<a id="dec-1130"></a>

## DEC-1130: The vetted-source allowlist file, and the news and filings stores, readers and fetchers (mandate-sources)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent, DEC-79 and DEC-176: reversible engineering readings, each tightening or closing a gap by the reading that adds no risk). Confirming Alpaca news under item 2 stays the founder's (DEC-79: legal). Item 2's in-effect rule is amended in part by DEC-2276: a source is also in effect on the founder's recorded approval |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | An agent (Claude Code, lane I3 of the intelligence layer), under DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E17-7, E2-19, with the news and filings halves of E2-9 and E2-10 that E17-7 needs; claim [#1460](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1460), PR [#1480](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/1480). Drafted as DEC-996 and renumbered from lane I3's block when lane I2's DEC-996 reached `main` first |

### Context

The data-plane spec (v0.2, §4) and the agent harness spec (§6.3, §10.2) say what news and filings
the research agent may read and how, but nothing held the allowlist's contents (E2-19), there was
no news or filings store, and no reader. Lane I2 (the harness research loop) needs a typed read
behind a trait. Lane M2 (E2-6, #1453) owns the workspace data service and market data. Each item
below is a reading of those specs, not a change to them; no spec, schema, or reference case
changes here.

### Decision

1. **The allowlist is a file a version, and E17-7 holds its contents** (closes E2-19).
   `config/sources/allowlist-v<N>.toml`, whose `version` is `N`; a change is a new file, and a
   file on `main` is never edited. Each source has an id, a kind (`news` or `filings`), whether
   its text is licensed, whether counsel confirmed it, a minimum request interval, a response cap,
   and its endpoints, each an `https://` host and a path prefix. Unknown keys, version 0, a
   repeated id, a source with no endpoint, a zero cap, and a non-HTTPS endpoint refuse the file.
2. **A source is in effect only while `counsel_confirmed = true`** (DEC-433 item 20: allowlist only
   sources whose terms allow keeping what a thesis cited, as counsel confirms per vendor). An
   unconfirmed entry is listed for review, permits no fetch, gives no reader any item, and is
   absent from the `SourceAllowlist` admission check 14 reads. Version 1 confirms SEC EDGAR
   (public data, DEC-433 item 16) and lists Alpaca news **unconfirmed**. Confirming it is a new
   allowlist version and the founder's call (DEC-79: legal).
3. **Only the allowlist builds a URL a transport may send to** (DP-9, DEC-433 item 11). A
   `PermittedUrl` has a private constructor, `Allowlist::permit`; the `Transport` trait takes only
   that type. `permit` requires the source to be in effect, the scheme `https://`, the host equal
   to an endpoint's host, the path to start with its prefix (on a segment boundary when the prefix
   has no trailing `/`), and every byte after the scheme from `A-Z a-z 0-9 - . _ ~ / ? & = : , +`,
   with no `..` and no `//`. So `%`, `@`, `#`, `\`, whitespace, ports, and user info are refused,
   and nothing can be decoded into a traversal. The HTTPS transport follows no redirect, so a 3xx
   answer is a refused status, never a second host. No fetch takes a URL from an item. A vendor's
   page token may hold no `+` (a server decodes it as a space, and `%` cannot escape it here): a
   page whose token holds one, or anything outside the token's form, ends the paging, which reads
   fewer items, never other ones.
4. **Knowledge time** (data-plane spec §5.2, DEC-433 item 4): receipt for a live capture; for a
   backfill, the later of the item's published and updated times (news) or its acceptance time
   (filings). An item whose source time is more than 1,000 ms after its receipt is refused
   `future_dated` in either mode, the news and filings reading of §3.4's `future_skew_ms`.
5. **Bounds.** A response over its source's cap is refused whole. An item naming more than 32
   tickers is refused `too_many_tags`. Every structured field has a closed form (ticker, CIK,
   vendor id, accession number, form type, document name, page token), and one outside it refuses
   its item (`bad_field`); one bad item in a page is refused and counted, and the rest are kept
   (§3.1's rule). Stored text is capped (headline 1,000, summary 4,000, link 2,000, excerpt 8,000
   bytes), and reads apply the harness caps of §6.3: news 48 hours, 50 items, 2,000 bytes of text
   an item, headline first, then link, then summary; filings 7 days, 20 entries, 4,000 bytes an
   excerpt. A query's window and limit can only narrow them. A query naming an allowlist version
   other than the one in effect is refused.
6. **Licensed text is withheld from reads by default** (agent harness spec §6.2's third
   precondition, DEC-431 item 17). `LicensedText::Withheld` returns a licensed source's items with
   every typed field and no text, until the inference spec's prompt record keeps licensed text out
   of the artifact store; the harness switches to `Included` only then.
7. **De-duplication and story groups** (§4.4). An exact copy (same source, same SHA-256 of the
   item's raw bytes) is stored once. A later vendor version of a stored item is a new record that
   names its predecessor and joins its group; different content at the same or an earlier version
   is refused `revised_in_place` (DP-2). So an older version that arrives after a newer one, for
   example a backfill after a live capture, is refused and counted rather than stored: reads miss
   it between its knowledge time and the newer one's, which reads less, never the future. A news
   item's group is the digest of its normalized
   headline and summary (lowercased, whitespace collapsed), so syndicated copies share it; a
   filing's group is its accession number, so an amendment (`10-K/A`, its own accession) is a new
   record and the original stays readable as of any earlier time (DP-1). A one-character change
   to a headline splits a group, so a reworded copy still counts as a second source; closing that
   needs more than the spec's three keys, and E2-15 still owns making admission check 15 count
   groups.
8. **Persistence waits for M2.** Both stores are in memory and append-only, with raw bytes kept by
   digest before parsing (§4.2 step 2). Where records live in a cell is the workspace data
   service's (E2-6, lane M2); the stores move onto its storage interface once that is on `main`,
   rather than this lane adding a second one. The in-memory record also holds a bounded copy of
   each text field, derived from the raw bytes, so a read need not re-parse; data-plane spec §4.5
   keeps text only as the raw artifact, so when the records are persisted, only the raw artifact
   is, and the text is derived again at read.
9. **The drift observation is the item's typed record at its knowledge time** (data-plane spec
   §4.6, agent harness spec §6.3): source, class (`News` or `Filing`), knowledge time, content
   hash, and byte length, never text. Folding each item once is the harness core's (lane I2).
10. **Credentials.** The Alpaca news fetcher takes a `NewsAuth` the caller read from the
    workspace's `SecretSource` (lane V2, E21-32); nothing here reads an environment variable.
    V2's policy table has no identity for a news reader yet; that row is V2's to add when the
    fetcher is wired into a process.
11. **The order path cannot reach text** (DP-8). `mandate-sources` sits at layer 6, level with the
    executor and above the builder, the gate, and accounting, so none of them can depend on it
    (`xtask/layers.toml`); a test pins it. Text exists only as `UntrustedText`, which has no
    `Display`, `Deref`, or conversion, and whose `Debug` prints only its length. The HTTPS
    transport is a separate impure crate, `mandate-sources-http`, so the safety-critical core
    stays pure.

### Rationale

Every item narrows what the research agent can read or how a fetch can go wrong, and none changes
a gate rule, a limit, or the envelope. Item 2 is the conservative reading of a founder decision
whose condition (counsel's confirmation) has not been met. Item 6 is the harness spec's own
precondition, enforced in the reader rather than left to each caller.

### Alternatives considered

- **One allowlist file edited in place:** loses the version a thesis recorded; refused by the
  spec's "versioned configuration".
- **Allowlisting Alpaca news now:** DEC-433 item 20 asks for counsel's confirmation first.
- **A URL type built by the fetcher:** any caller of the transport could then send anywhere; the
  private constructor makes DP-9 unrepresentable instead of checked.
- **Persisting to Parquet here:** would duplicate M2's storage interface (E2-6).

<a id="dec-1140"></a>

## DEC-1140: A refused thesis record wins a receive-time tie, and a resolved thesis's exit stays DEC-281's

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: both readings only tighten or restate, DEC-176) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The lane I4 builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E4-4, E17-8; DEC-1000 item 5, DEC-1002 item 3, DEC-281 item 3; claim [#1463](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1463) |

### Context

The coordinator's delta review of #1472 left two minors. DEC-1000 item 5 breaks a tie between thesis
records received at one instant by thesis id, which says nothing about which verdict wins. And
DEC-1002 item 3 says a thesis resolves once every series holds a close at or after its horizon end,
which a reader could take to mean that close is the exit.

### Decision

1. **A refused record wins a tie.** Among the thesis records for the instrument received at or
   before `t`, the latest is the one with the greatest `(received_at, refused, thesis_id)`: at one
   receive time a refused record outranks an admitted one, and the thesis id breaks what remains.
   An admission therefore never survives a refusal journaled at the same instant, whatever the ids.
   This only keeps the instrument out of the universe in more cases (DEC-176).
2. **The exit is DEC-281's.** A thesis's exit is the last close at or before its horizon end
   (`mandate_research::score::exit_close`, DEC-281 item 3), however many closes follow it. A close
   at or after the horizon end only shows that the series has passed the horizon, so the exit can no
   longer move (DEC-1002 item 3). It is never the exit. Taking the first close after the horizon would
   read a price the thesis never claimed and would contradict the scorer. So this item restates
   DEC-281 and changes no code.

### Tests

- Item 1: `version::thesis_visibility_holds_at_its_edges` (an admitted `th_02` and a refused `th_01`
  received at one instant: no opening); the property oracle `openings_only_on_an_active_thesis`
  ranks records the same way.
- Item 2: `evaluation::a_closed_thesis_is_scored_net_of_its_round_trip` (exit 110 at the Wednesday
  close before the 21:00Z horizon, with a later Thursday close of 111 in the series).

### Alternatives considered

- **The admitted record wins a tie.** Rejected: it would let an admission outlive a refusal that
  arrived at the same instant, which adds risk.
- **The first close at or after the horizon is the exit.** Rejected: see item 2.

<a id="dec-1143"></a>

## DEC-1143: The backtest and evaluation reads fail closed: an evaluation only where the deployment is marked internal and its journal is paper, and no doubtful record served

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: a reversible engineering reading under DEC-79; each item resolves a gap in workspace API spec §4.2 and §4.7 by the reading that only tightens and adds no risk, DEC-176) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The lane I4 builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176 |
| **Story or claim** | E4-5, E17-8; workspace API spec §4.2, §4.7; DEC-1001 item 5, DEC-1002 items 6 and 7; journal spec v0.45 §9.18; claim [#1463](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/1463) |

### Context

`mandate-api-server` serves `GET /v1/workspaces/{ws}/backtests/{id}` and `…/evaluations/{id}` from
the control stream's `BacktestRunRecorded` and `EvaluationRunRecorded` (journal spec §9.18), with
each report read from the content-addressed store. Four things are not stated:

- §4.7 and DEC-1002 item 6 serve an evaluation "only in the team's internal paper workspaces
  (DEC-103); elsewhere `not_found`", and nothing in the code or the configuration marks a workspace
  internal. DEC-1141 item 2 leaves the same check off the record.
- The server is configured with no journal environment; each row carries its own.
- §4.2 and DEC-1001 item 5 say a completed run's `report` is the artifact's object, and do not say
  what the read answers when the store lacks it or it fails its digest check.
- Nothing says what a second record with the same `backtest_id` or `evaluation_id` means.

### Decision

1. **The setting.** `MANDATE_API_INTERNAL_PAPER_WORKSPACE` marks the deployment's one hosted
   workspace an internal paper workspace. It is off when absent or empty, on only for exactly
   `true`, off for exactly `false`, and any other value refuses start naming the setting
   (`config::internal_paper_workspace`). It is carried as `Hosted::internal_paper`.
2. **Paper, shown at start and on every read.** With the setting on, the process reads the hosted
   control stream before it listens, within five seconds, and refuses to start, exit status 2,
   unless the stream has a row and every row's `environment` is `paper`. An unreadable, empty, or
   mixed stream refuses (`config::check_internal_paper`): the setting is never taken on trust, so
   it is turned on only after the stream is opened. The evaluation read checks the same of the rows
   it reads, so a live row appended later closes the route again.
3. **Elsewhere, the one 404.** With the setting off, or the stream not paper throughout, every
   evaluation id answers the 404 an absent id answers, a recorded one included, after the request
   is authenticated and authorized as any read is. The route does not say that it exists.
4. **A completed run's report, or 503.** `read-models/backtest` requires a completed run's `report`
   to be an object, so a completed record whose report the store lacks, that fails its digest check
   (logged by name, with no content), that is not a JSON object of its `kind` and `report_version`
   (2 for a backtest, 1 for an evaluation), or, for a backtest, that names another mandate version,
   answers `journal_unavailable`, as does a deployment with no store. A failed read is never an
   empty result, and `report: null` would make a completed run look like a refused one. A refused
   run needs no store.
5. **One record, or none served.** Exactly one schema-version-1 record must name the id. A second
   record with the same id, identical or not, answers `journal_unavailable`: the journal holds no
   single answer, and serving the first or the last would show the go-live screen a report the
   journal does not settle. The read checks only the members it would otherwise serve wrongly, and
   a record failing one of them answers `journal_unavailable` too. For `BacktestRunRecorded` they
   are: `mandate_version` a reference that parses, `marker` = `backtest`, `report_version` = 2,
   and rule 143's pairing (`result: completed` with `report` a reference that parses and `refusal`
   null, or `result: refused` with `report` null and a `refusal` in ES-09's code grammar). For
   `EvaluationRunRecorded` they are `report_version` = 1 and `report` a reference that parses. It
   checks nothing else of §9.18, rule 144's `mandate_version` = `config_refs.mandate_version` and
   rule 145's three thesis lists, of one length and non-empty, among them: the journal's `append`
   enforces §9.18's schema and rules 143 to 145 at write time, once the records are registered in
   `mandate-journal` (the PR to follow #1546), and until then `append` refuses both records, so no
   such row can exist. A row whose body does not parse is skipped, as the other reads skip it.
   An id outside the id grammar names nothing.
6. **No `running`.** Nothing journals a backtest in flight (`POST /backtests` is not built), so a
   backtest is absent until its record commits and `state: running` is never served.
7. **The permission row.** Neither identity spec §4.2's matrix nor §3.7's copy names a row for these
   reads. Both use "View agents, positions, decisions", the row every §4.7 read uses: no row is
   stricter for a backtest, and "Read records, verification; export" is not, since it admits a
   service account that the view row refuses. A backtest answers only in the workspace whose control
   stream records it (API-9).

### Rationale

Every item narrows what is served and loosens nothing. Item 1 and 2 make the internal workspace an
explicit, checked act of the deployment's configuration rather than an inference, so a mistake
closes the route; item 3 keeps an owner's workspace from learning the route exists, as DEC-1002
item 6 intends. Items 4 and 5 follow rule 3 of `AGENTS.md`: an ambiguous or unreadable journal
resolves to no answer, and the schema itself forbids the `null` report. Item 7 takes the matrix as
it stands; a stricter row is a change to the identity spec.

### Alternatives considered

- **Serve `report: null` for a missing artifact.** Rejected: the schema refuses it for a completed
  run, and it reads as no report rather than a failed read.
- **The first or the latest record wins.** Rejected: either serves a report the journal does not
  settle; the writer is single, so a duplicate is a defect to surface, not to resolve.
- **Mark internal workspaces in the journal.** Deferred: it needs a record type and a spec change;
  this setting can be read from such a record later without changing the route.
- **Trust the setting without reading the stream.** Rejected: one wrong setting would show the
  founder's evaluation in an owner's live workspace.

<a id="dec-1150"></a>

## DEC-1150: A working order is read by the profile's listing, and one opening works per instrument (R0 R18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Accepted (agent: reversible engineering readings under DEC-79, each under DEC-176. Items 4 to 6 refuse or hold more. Items 1 to 3 and 7 fill a gap, how a working order is read where the profile cannot query by id, by the strictest reading: adoption only of the one record naming the order by our own id or the broker id our place received, matched as sent, so nothing is believed on a guess. None touches live money, spending, legal text or a safety rule) |
| **Date** | 2026-10-17 (UTC) |
| **Decided by** | The R18 builder (Claude Code), under DEC-79 and DEC-176; one PR with its tests first (DEC-921) |
| **Story or claim** | E7-26, E7-23; the [R0 rehearsal](../tasks/r0-rehearsal.md)'s R18 and its second ask; claim #1428 |

On the Tuesday rehearsal clock, the entry was sent to the simulator and accepted
(`rh-sim-000001`). The run then stopped with `the tracer stopped at Connector: the request was
not sent (not_offered)`, because something asked the Robinhood connector for
`GetOrderByClientId`, which it does not serve: Robinhood's profile cannot query by client order
id. The R0 record first blamed the executor's `query_unknown`. Reading the code showed that the
request that stopped the run was the shell's LT-10 watch, which polled the entry by its client
order id on every wake (`crates/mandate-shell/src/tracer.rs`, `watch`). The executor's
`query_unknown` had the same gap for a working order. In the same run, the runtime asked a second
time for the same instrument while the first opening worked. These are the readings that close
both gaps.

### Decision

1. **The executor asks after a working order by the profile's listing.** On a profile with
   `query_by_client_order_id: false`, an order in any state other than `Unknown`, which the
   broker has already described, is asked after by one `ListOrders` listing. The listing covers
   its instrument and agentic origin, from DEC-862 item 2's margin before its `OrderSubmitted`.
   No query the connector refuses unsent is asked. Unlike an `Unknown` order, this needs no
   dedicated account, because item 2 adopts only a record naming the order by our own id.
   - An `Unknown` order keeps DEC-862's listing on a dedicated account and nothing on a shared one.
   - A profile that can query by client order id still asks by it.
   - An order with no folded submission is asked after by nothing, and stays as it is.
2. **A working order adopts only the record naming it.** A listing's answer for a working order is
   folded through §5.7's table only when exactly one record names the order by our own client
   order id and matches it as sent (DEC-862 item 4's members). Robinhood's records name our
   `ref_id` exactly (DEC-941 item 4). The following leave the order as it was, with nothing sent:
   - a record naming no id (DEC-863's look-alike, which only an `Unknown` order may adopt);
   - a record naming another id;
   - two records naming ours;
   - no record at all.

   So a working order is never taken as filled or gone on a guess. The broker's own order id is
   not the key: journal spec §9.16 says the executor's fold never reads `broker_order_id`, and our
   id carried as `ref_id` is the same identity, already in the fold.
3. **The watch reads its entry the way the profile offers.** The shell's watch asks by client
   order id when the connector's profile can, and otherwise by the same listing, from the run's
   start less the margin. It reads the LT-10 status only from the one record naming the entry. A
   listing with none or several such records reads nothing, and the watch reads again on the
   next wake. The answer goes to the executor as every broker answer does, so item 2 decides
   what it describes. A profile the connector cannot give is read as one that can query by id,
   which the connector then refuses unsent, and the run stops (fail closed).
4. **The runtime follows its own openings on the account stream.** From the journal alone:
   - **Linking.** `OrderRequestRecorded` names the intent (`intent_id`). `OrderSubmitted` names
     its order (`client_order_id`), and its `causation_id` is that request (rule 45), or the
     intent itself on the legacy shape a test double writes. A `replaced` order passes the intent
     to its successor (`replaced_by`).
   - **What ends one.** A `GateDecided` with verdict `deny`; an `OrderAbandoned`; or an
     `OrderStateChanged` of its own order to `filled`, `canceled`, `rejected`, `expired` or
     `abandoned`.
   - **What ends nothing.** A record missing any of these members, or naming an order the runtime
     did not submit. The hold therefore never lifts on a guess; the only cost is holding longer,
     which adds no risk.

   All of it is rebuilt by replay (ES-21). `FOLD_VERSION` stays 1, because these records were
   folded as inert before and no committed fold output changes.
